# Complete normal spectral mixtures remain insufficient after free completion

Status: complete candidate proof, UNVERIFIED. No fact_submit is allocated.
Accepted inputs:1252b8823bb5a290 (interpolation and maximal completion),
dfde2fa326c908bf (complex orbit notation and necessary interpolation), and
b98bb807d514e80d (the exact benchmark recursions). The recursive family and
the real-product method were recalled from PUBLIC candidate79f6ce08b41616b5;
no unverified result is used below. Every needed estimate is included.

The final result is stronger than the supporting individual-mode statement
in Sections1--5: Sections6--7 prove a common unit-step O(c^(-m)) upper bound
for EVERY jointly legal finite normal spectral mixture, with arbitrary complex
spectra, arbitrary horizon-dependent weights and dimension, arbitrary legal
prefix values, and a fully free terminal completion. NO individual-mode
legality is assumed in that final result. A single common interpolation
cycle controls all troublesome modes simultaneously. The exact benchmark
comparison is included, so this is a full obstruction for the stated normal
polynomial-prefix ansatz, not just for separately realizable modes.

## Supporting individual-mode result

Put c=13/5, a=c^(-17), b_j=a c^j, and define

    H_0=(),  H_(m+1)=(H_m,b_m,H_m),
    N_m=2^m-1,
    S_m=sum H_m=a(c^m-2^m)/(c-2).

For a complex z, let x_i=P_i(z) be the chronological prefix polynomial
orbit of H_m, starting from x_0=1, and g_i=z x_i for i=0,...,N_m. Include
the optimum jet (0,0,0). Its complete-block polynomial is

    p_m(z)=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1-b_j z)^(2^(m-j-1)).

Suppose this FULL prefix is interpolable by a convex differentiable
1-smooth function with minimum zero at the origin. Its values may be ANY
legal interpolating values, not only the maximal path values of the
complex-orbit construction. At the appended final point, use ANY convex
1-smooth interpolant F of the chosen jets, including the pointwise maximal
one and with no condition on its terminal gradient. Then the COMMON unit
step satisfies

    2F(x_(N_m)-g_(N_m)) <= 2c^11/(a c^m).                      (1)

This holds for every m>=1, with deliberately nonoptimal constants.
It also holds for any finite orthogonal mixture of such INDIVIDUALLY legal
complex orbits with squared initial weights summing to one, even if the
joint legal function values and final interpolant are not separable.

The canonical sum-only Huber channel on the appended schedule (H_m,1)
is also O(c^(-m)). The exact recursions imply

    B_n<=1+4n^(4/3),

so, since c^3>16, the maximum of the entire stated witness class and that
Huber channel, divided by U_(2^m)^ZJ, tends to zero.

This supporting result is a scoped upper bound on the stated witness family,
NOT on actual W_(2^m)(H_m,1). It uses full schedule-dependent legality, with
no universal spectral lens. By itself it does not cover jointly legal
mixtures of individually illegal modes. Sections6--7 remove that restriction
using one common cycle. General nonnormal kernels remain outside the scope
even after that extension.

## 1. Universal last-query inequalities and the free unit step

Write z=alpha+i beta and sigma=|z|^2. For z=0 all gradients vanish and
convexity makes every queried value, and the appended unchanged value,
zero. Hence assume z!=0. Interpolation between x_0=1 and the optimum gives

    sigma<=alpha,  alpha>0,  |z|<=1,
    f_0<=alpha-sigma/2.                                      (2)

For any queried i it likewise gives

    f_i<= (alpha-sigma/2)|x_i|^2.                             (3)

The unit-step descent inequality, obtained by integrating a 1-Lipschitz
gradient along -g_i, gives F(x_i-g_i)<=f_i-|g_i|^2/2. Thus for the last
query, independently of all earlier value choices,

    2F(x_(N_m)-g_(N_m))
       <=2(alpha-sigma)|p_m(z)|^2.                           (4)

This includes the maximal terminal completion in accepted1252b8823bb5a290.

## 2. Full interpolation imposes a sector or a small-parameter case

Put q=alpha/|z| in(0,1] and h_c=2alpha/sigma. Then h_c>=2 by(2), and
|1-hz|<=1 exactly for0<=h<=h_c.

If every step in H_m is smaller than h_c, every factor contracts, so
|p_m(z)|<=1. Also b_(m-1)<h_c implies

    |z|^2 < 2alpha/b_(m-1) <=2|z|/b_(m-1),
    |z|<2c/(a c^m).

Equation(4) is then less than4c/(a c^m), which is at most the bound(1).
The same conclusion follows if there is no step greater than or equal to
h_c. We now treat the remaining case.

Let r be the smallest level with b_r>=h_c. Then r<=m-1, b_(r-1)<h_c,
and r>=18 because b_17=1<h_c. The chronological prefix before the first
b_r is exactly H_r, and every step of this prefix strictly contracts radii.
Define v=b_r|z|. Minimality gives

    2q<=v<2c q.                                              (5)

Let d=2/c=10/13. Since d^3<1/2 and d^2<4/5, d^17<1/40. Consequently

    S_r/b_r=(1-d^r)/(c-2)>=13/8.                             (6)

The squared-step sum of H_r is

    T_r=sum_(h in H_r) h^2=a^2(c^(2r)-2^r)/(c^2-2),
    T_r/b_r^2<=1/(c^2-2)=25/119.                             (7)

Using log(1+t)<=t in every squared radial factor gives

    log|p_r(z)|^2 <= -2alpha S_r+sigma T_r
                   <=-(13/4)q v+v^2/(c^2-2).                 (8)

The concave quadratic (13/4)q v-v^2/(c^2-2) has its minimum on the
interval(5) at an endpoint. At v=2q its coefficient of q^2 is
13/2-100/119=1347/238>11/2. At v=2c q that coefficient is
169/10-676/119>11/2. Thus

    |p_r(z)|^2 <= exp(-(11/2)q^2).                            (9)

We now use ACTUAL ordered value inequalities inside the prefix, not only
pairwise cocoercivity or radial contraction. At a step of size h with point
x and next point x_+=(1-hz)x, interpolation in the direction from the next
query back to this query gives

    f(x)-f(x_+)
      >=h(1-halpha+hsigma/2)sigma |x|^2
      =[sigma/(2alpha)](|x|^2-|x_+|^2)
        +[h^2 sigma/(2alpha)]
              [sigma(1+alpha)-2alpha^2]|x|^2.                 (10)

For the first inequality, g_+=(1-hz)g,
<g_+,g>=(1-halpha)|g|^2, and |g-g_+|^2=h^2sigma|g|^2. Expanding gives
the displayed identity; no endpoint value or local oracle is guessed.

Suppose q<=2/3. Then sigma-2alpha^2=sigma(1-2q^2)>0, so the second term
in(10) is nonnegative. Sum(10) along the exact H_r prefix. Its terminal
value is nonnegative and (2) bounds the initial value, so

    [sigma/(2alpha)](1-|p_r(z)|^2)<=alpha-sigma/2,
    |p_r(z)|^2>=1-2q^2+alpha>1-2q^2.                         (11)

But for0<t<=4/9,

    exp(-(11/2)t)<1-2t.                                     (12)

Indeed the difference 1-2t-exp(-(11/2)t) is concave, vanishes at0, and is
positive at4/9. For the last claim,
exp(22/9)=exp(2)exp(4/9)>(8/3)^2(1+4/9)=832/81>9;
exp(1)>8/3 follows from the first four terms of its positive series.
Equations(9),(11),(12) contradict each other. Therefore every legal orbit
in the present case satisfies

    q=Re(z)/|z|>2/3.                                        (13)

This is a necessary condition derived from the entire contracting prefix.

## 3. Uniform damping of the dyadic product in the forced sector

Assume0<|z|<=1 and q=Re(z)/|z|>=2/3. Choose the unique integer k>=17
such that

    c^k a|z|<=1<c^(k+1)a|z|,
    y=c^k a|z| in(1/c,1].                                  (14)

If m<=k, every factor has b_j|z|<=1, and
|1-b_jz|^2=1-2q b_j|z|+b_j^2|z|^2<=1; hence |p_m(z)|<=1.

Suppose m>k. A vanishing factor makes the desired damping immediate, so
assume there is none. Write z/|z|=exp(i theta). Directly from multiplicities,

    log|p_m(z)|/2^(m-k-1)
       =sum_(j=-k)^(m-k-1) 2^(-j)log|1-c^j y exp(i theta)|.    (15)

For j<0, log(1+t)<=t gives

    log|1-c^j y exp(i theta)|<=-q c^j y+(c^j y)^2/2.

Summing these terms and using k>=17 and (6) bounds their contribution by

    -(13/4)q y + (25/119)y^2.

For j>=1, c^j y>1 and q>=2/3, so
|1-c^j y exp(i theta)|<=c^j y. The bounds jlog c+log y are nonnegative;
extending their weighted sum to all j>=1 gives2log c+log y. The j=0 term
is at most(1/2)log(1-(4/3)y+y^2). Therefore(15) is at most

    E(y)=-(13/6)y+(25/119)y^2
           +(1/2)log(1-(4/3)y+y^2)+log y+2log(13/5).          (16)

Here we used q>=2/3 also in the negative linear term. We prove the fully
uniform elementary inequality

    E(y)<-1/128  for5/13<=y<=1.                              (17)

First let5/13<=y<=1/2. The positive quadratic term is at most25/476,
and D(y)=1-(4/3)y+y^2 is decreasing here, so D(y)<=322/507. Also
-(13/6)y+log y<=-1+log(6/13), by maximizing that elementary concave
function. Combining yields

    E(y)<=-1+25/476+log A,
    A=(78/25)sqrt(322/507),  A^2=3864/625<(5/2)^2.

We have log(5/2)<15/16, since the first four terms in the positive
exponential series give

    exp(15/16)>
      1+15/16+(15/16)^2/2+(15/16)^3/6
      =61791/24576>5/2.

Thus E(y)<-1+25/476+15/16=-19/1904<-1/128.

For1/2<=y<=1, E is strictly decreasing. Here are the exact derivative
calculations to check that claim, without a numerical maximization. With
D(y)=1-(4/3)y+y^2,

    E'(y)=B(y)/[2142yD(y)],
    B(y)=900y^4-5841y^3+11372y^2-8925y+2142.

Put t=2y-1 in[0,1]. Direct expansion gives

    16B((1+t)/2)
       =-2422-11870t+15842t^2-8082t^3+900t^4
       <=-2422+t[-11870+15842t-7182t^2]
       <=-2422-3210t<0.

For the second inequality, 15842t-7182t^2 is increasing on[0,1] and is
at most8660. The denominator of E' is positive. Hence E(y)<=E(1/2),
already controlled by the preceding interval. This proves(17).

Equations(15)--(17) imply

    |p_m(z)| <= exp(-2^(m-k-1)/128)  whenever m>k.             (18)

## 4. The free terminal objective bound

For a legal orbit the small-parameter case in Section2 already gives
the bound(1). Otherwise(13) permits the product estimates of Section3.
If |z|<=1/(a c^m), then m<=k and(4) is at most2/(a c^m).
If |z|>1/(a c^m), let r=m-k>=1. By(14),(18),

    2(alpha-sigma)|p_m(z)|^2
       <=[2/(a c^m)] c^r exp(-2^(r-1)/64)
       <=2c^11/(a c^m).                                    (19)

For1<=r<=11, discard the exponential and use c^r<=c^11. For r>=12,
2^(r-1)/64=2^(r-7)>=r, by induction from r=12, while log c<1 because
exp(1)>8/3>13/5. The extra factor is then at most1. This proves(19),
and therefore(1), with complete prefix values and arbitrary terminal
completion retained.

For a finite orthogonal mixture, write its components as

    x_i^(l)=sqrt(w_l)P_i(z_l),
    g_i^(l)=sqrt(w_l)z_l P_i(z_l),
    w_l>=0, sum_l w_l=1,

up to harmless rotations in the individual planes. Assume every active
z_l individually has a legal prefix. For ANY jointly legal values and ANY
joint smooth convex interpolant, the same last-query/unit-step inequalities
give

    2F(x_(N_m)-g_(N_m))
       <=2sum_l w_l(Re(z_l)-|z_l|^2)|p_m(z_l)|^2
       <=2c^11/(a c^m).

Separability of the joint interpolant is unnecessary. Individual legality
IS needed to apply Section2 to each z_l; it is not inferred from joint
legality. No such inference is claimed for mixtures of illegal modes.

## 5. Exact-profile comparison, with its recurrence estimate proved here

For m>=17, (6) with r=m gives S_m>=13a c^m/8. Thus the canonical Huber
value for(H_m,1) is at most

    1/[1+2(S_m+1)]<=1/(2S_m)<=4/(13a c^m).                    (20)

We now derive B_n<=1+4n^(4/3) directly from the exact recursions stated in
acceptedb98bb807d514e80d. Put C_n=S_circle(n)+1 and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1.
In these variables the primitive and dominant joins respectively are

    Omega(A,D)=(A+D+sqrt((A+D)^2+4AD))/2,
    G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2.

They are increasing in their nonnegative arguments. For0<=t<=1,

    t^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1.                  (21)

To prove it set x=t^(1/3),y=(1-t)^(1/3), so x^3+y^3=1. Then

    1-x^4-y^4-x^4y^4
       =x^3y^3[1/(1+x+x^2)+1/(1+y+y^2)-xy]>=0.

Each reciprocal is at least1/3, while xy<=2^(-2/3)<2/3; the latter strict
inequality follows by cubing. The endpoints also satisfy the identity.
Since Omega is the positive root of X^2-(A+D)X-AD=0, (21) implies

    Omega(A,D)<=(A^(3/4)+D^(3/4))^(4/3).

Induction in the primitive recursion gives C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3). Also
sqrt(D^2+8AD)<=D+4A, hence G(A,D)<=D+4A. Induction in the dominant
recursion, for every split k, gives

    B_n<=1+4[(n-k-1)^(4/3)+(k+1)^(4/3)]<=1+4n^(4/3).

The initial values are C_0=B_0=1. The last inequality uses the elementary
superadditivity of u^(4/3) on nonnegative u.

The appended horizon is N_m+1=2^m. Multiply(1),(20) by
B_(2^m)<=1+4*2^(4m/3). Their ratios to U_(2^m)^ZJ tend to zero because
c^3=2197/125>16. This closes the proposed all-schedule comparison for the
entire stated individually legal orbit/direct-sum family, even after free
terminal completion and addition of the canonical Huber channel.

## 6. Analytic estimates for supporting modes, independent of legality

The estimates in this section assume only0<|z|<=1 and
q=Re(z)/|z|>=2/3; they do not assume that the orbit is individually legal.
Retain k from(14), and define

    R_u(z)=p_u(z)(1-b_u z),
    E_u(z)=sum_(i in H_u) h_i^2 |z|^2 |P_i(z)|^2.

Here P_i are the local chronological prefix polynomials of H_u starting
at1. The recursion of the actual ordered list gives

    E_(u+1)=(1+|R_u|^2)E_u+b_u^2|z|^2|p_u|^2.               (22)

For u<=k all factors contract, so E_k<=|z|^2 T_k<1. At u=k both
|R_k|^2 and b_k^2|z|^2|p_k|^2 are at most1, hence E_(k+1)<=3.
For u=k+t,t>=1, the damping estimate(18) and b_u|z|<=c^t give

    |R_u|^2, b_u^2|z|^2|p_u|^2
          <=c^(2t)exp(-2^(t-7)).                            (23)

For1<=t<=12 this is at most c^24. For t>=13,2^(t-7)>=3t, by induction
from t=13, so(23) is at most exp(-t)<=2^(-t), using log c<1. Applying(22)
to E_u+1 therefore gives the uniform bound

    E_u(z)+1<K_E:=12(1+c^24)^12  for every u>=0.               (24)

Indeed E_(k+1)+1<=4. The next twelve updates multiply this upper bound
by at most(1+c^24)^12. Every subsequent update multiplies E_u+1 by at
most1+2^(-t); their product is at most exp(sum_(t>=13)2^(-t))<exp(1)<3.
For exp(1)<3, sum n!>=2^(n-1), with strictness when n>=3, in the
exponential series. The earlier u<=k already satisfy(24).

There is also the weighted block-amplitude bound

    |z||R_u(z)|^2<=c^36/b_u.                                (25)

If u<=k, |z|<=1/b_u and |R_u|<=1. If u=k+t>k, multiply(23) by
|z|<=c^t/b_u. For t<=12 the result is at most c^36/b_u; for t>=13 it
is at most1/b_u because2^(t-7)>=3t and log c<1. In particular

    Re(z)|R_(m-1)(z)|^2<=c^37/b_m.                          (26)

These are quantitative bounds on the same chronological orbit, with no
dimension, mixture-weight or function-value assumptions.

## 7. One common complete cycle controls ALL normal modes

Consider ANY finite list of complex numbers z_l and weights w_l>=0 with
sum_l w_l=1. Form the real direct-sum data for the entire H_m prefix by

    x_i=(sqrt(w_l)P_i(z_l))_l,
    g_i=(sqrt(w_l)z_l P_i(z_l))_l,
    x_star=g_star=0.                                        (27)

Real scalar modes are included as beta_l=0. Constant phases in individual
planes can be absorbed by orthogonal rotations. There is no assumption on
the individual spectra or their separate legality. Assume only that these
JOINT data admit actual convex1-smooth interpolation values f_i, with the
given optimum, and let F be ANY legal interpolant of them.

Define constants depending only on c by

    C_aux=K_E c^37+2c[1+4c^2/(c^2-2)],
    C_norm=2c^11+256 C_aux.                                  (28)

We prove the uniform upper bound

    2F(x_(N_m)-g_(N_m))<=C_norm/(a c^m).                     (29)

The spectra, weights, number of modes, and legal values may all depend on
m. This upper bound includes the pointwise maximal terminal completion.

### 7a. The common cycle and its exact recurrence

For any mode z=alpha+i beta, put sigma=|z|^2, kappa=alpha-sigma, and let

    L_r(z)=sum_(i in H_r) h_i |P_i(z)|^2,
    E_r(z)=sum_(i in H_r) h_i^2 sigma |P_i(z)|^2.

Define its reverse-chronological optimum cycle weight by

    D_r(z)=sum_(i in H_r)
                 h_i sigma(1-h_i alpha+h_i sigma/2)|P_i(z)|^2
                  +(sigma/2)|p_r(z)|^2-alpha+sigma/2.        (30)

The cycle goes from the optimum to the end of H_r, backwards through its
queries to its start, then to the optimum. The reverse query edges have
the algebraic weights in(10). Its area and energy alternatively give

    D_r(z)=beta^2 L_r(z)
                 -(kappa/2)[1+|p_r(z)|^2+E_r(z)].            (31)

To check(31), each forward GD edge has area
Im(conj(P_i)P_(i+1))=-h_i beta|P_i|^2. Reversing the path changes the
sign, and the two optimum edges contribute zero area. The cycle energy is
1+|p_r|^2+E_r. The complex cycle identity of accepteddfde2fa326c908bf is
purely algebraic and therefore applies even to an individually illegal mode.

If alpha>0, telescoping squared norms yields

    1-|p_r|^2=2alpha L_r-E_r.

Put d= sigma(1+alpha)-2alpha^2. Substitution into(31) gives

    D_r=[d/(2alpha)](1+E_r)
                    -[sigma(1-alpha)/(2alpha)]|p_r|^2.

Using(22), p_(r+1)=R_r p_r, and
b_r^2sigma|p_r|^2-|R_r|^2=(2b_r alpha-1)|p_r|^2, one obtains the EXACT
recurrence

    D_(r+1)=(1+|R_r|^2)D_r
                         +|p_r|^2[kappa+b_r d].             (32)

For clarity, in the substitution the constant-coefficient difference is
[sigma(1-alpha)-d]/(2alpha)=kappa. Thus no sign in(32) is inferred from
legality or a guessed potential.

Now use the LAST copy of H_(m-1) inside H_m. Its start polynomial is
R_(m-1), and its end polynomial is p_m=R_(m-1)p_(m-1). The SAME indexed
cycle on the full joint prefix has weight

    sum_l w_l D_(m-1)(z_l)|R_(m-1)(z_l)|^2.

Joint interpolation requires

    sum_l w_l D_(m-1)(z_l)|R_(m-1)(z_l)|^2<=0.               (33)

No different cycle is selected for different spectral modes in(33).

### 7b. Classification of every complex parameter

Partition the active modes into the following three groups.

OUTSIDE MODES: kappa<=0. Formula(31) gives D_r>=0 for every r, because
beta^2 L_r>=0 and the bracketed energy is nonnegative. These modes can
supply NO negative credit in(33), and their unit-step objective contribution
2w_l kappa_l|p_m(z_l)|^2 is nonpositive. This includes arbitrary real or
complex parameters outside the cocoercivity disk and the zero mode.

SAFE MODES: kappa>0 and either q=alpha/|z|>=2/3, or every step of H_m is
smaller than h_c=2alpha/sigma. Here alpha>=sigma>0 implies0<|z|<=1.
In the sector case, Sections3 and4 are purely polynomial bounds and give

    2kappa|p_m|^2<=2c^11/b_m.                               (34)

Also each term in(30) is at least-alpha h_i^2sigma|P_i|^2, so
-D_r<=alpha(1+E_r). With(24),(26), this yields

    [-D_(m-1)]|R_(m-1)|^2<=K_E c^37/b_m.                   (35)

In the all-contracting case, every prefix modulus is at most1 and
|z|<2c/b_m. Then E_(m-1)<=|z|^2 T_(m-1)<=4c^2/(c^2-2), and the left
side of(35) is at most2c[1+4c^2/(c^2-2)]/b_m. The objective bound(34)
also holds, by the small-parameter calculation of Section2. Thus the total
negative cycle credit from ALL safe modes, after their weights are applied,
is at most C_aux/b_m, while their total objective contribution is at most
2c^11/b_m.

TROUBLESOME MODES: kappa>0, q<2/3, and there is a level r<=m-1 with
b_r>=h_c. Take the first such r for the following ANALYSIS of the mode,
without changing the common cycle(33). It has r>=18, and all of H_r
strictly contracts. Estimates(5)--(9) and the algebraic identity in(10)
do not need individual legality. They imply

    D_r(z)>=alpha[(1-exp(-(11/2)q^2))/(2q^2)-1].              (36)

The bracket decreases as q^2>0 increases: differentiating
(1-exp(-at))/t and using exp(at)>=1+at proves this. Since q^2<=4/9,

    D_r(z)/alpha
       >=(9/8)(1-exp(-22/9))-1
       >(9/8)(1-81/832)-1
       =103/6656>1/128.                                    (37)

Here we used the explicit exponential bound in(12). Moreover
d=sigma(1+alpha)-2alpha^2>0 and kappa>0, so(32) implies for every u>=r

    D_(u+1)>=|R_u|^2 D_u.

Propagating the positive defect up to m-1 gives

    D_(m-1)(z)
       >=D_r(z) product_(u=r)^(m-2)|R_u(z)|^2
       >=[alpha/128]|p_(m-1)(z)|^2.                        (38)

For the second inequality, p_(m-1)=p_r product_(u=r)^(m-2)R_u and
|p_r|<=1. The empty product when r=m-1 is allowed. Multiplying(38) by
|R_(m-1)|^2 proves that EVERY troublesome mode contributes at least

    [alpha/128]|p_m(z)|^2                                 (39)

to the SAME cycle(33). Thus troublesome modes cannot cancel each other's
positive defects on this cycle, even if their first violating scales differ.

These three groups cover every complex number. Boundary kappa=0 modes
were placed outside, and q=2/3 modes in the safe sector, so no limiting case
is omitted.

### 7c. Joint legality pays for the whole problematic spectral mass

Insert the classification into(33). Outside modes contribute nonnegative
cycle weights; all troublesome modes have the simultaneous lower bound(39).
The total possible negative credit of safe modes is at most C_aux/b_m.
Therefore

    sum_(troublesome l) w_l alpha_l |p_m(z_l)|^2
                                      <=128 C_aux/b_m.       (40)

For ANY joint legal values, interpolation to the optimum followed by the
common unit-step descent gives

    2F(x_(N_m)-g_(N_m))
      <=2<x_(N_m),g_(N_m)>-2|g_(N_m)|^2
      =2sum_l w_l kappa_l |p_m(z_l)|^2.                      (41)

Outside modes have nonpositive terms. Safe modes contribute at most
2c^11/b_m in total. In every troublesome mode kappa_l<=alpha_l, so(40)
bounds their total by256 C_aux/b_m. This proves(29).

No assumption of separable function values, separate legality, a bounded
number of modes, or a spectrum fixed across horizons was made. The Gram
data in(27) are real and PSD by actual direct-sum realization; joint
legality, when it holds, is the only hypothesis used for(33),(41).

## 8. Exact scope of the completed normal-mixture obstruction

Every real finite-dimensional normal operator is orthogonally decomposable
into real scalar blocks and planar complex-multiplication blocks. For a
normalized initial vector, the squared block-coordinate norms give exactly
the weights in(27); initial phases are harmless planar rotations. Therefore
(29) applies equivalently to EVERY jointly legal prefix of the form

    x_i=P_i(A)x_0,  g_i=A x_i,

with one real normal operator A, arbitrary real Gram dimension, arbitrary
legal values, and arbitrary free terminal completion. No spectral restriction
on A is imposed. The finite direct-sum formulation(27) also states the
whole result without using operator terminology.

The horizon after the unit step is2^m. Multiplying(29) and the canonical
Huber bound(20) by B_(2^m)<=1+4*2^(4m/3), proved in Section5, makes both
ratios tend to zero, since c^3>16. This is a full rigorous candidate
obstruction for the chosen NORMAL spectral-mixture ansatz. It does not
upper-bound unrestricted W or exclude a genuinely NONNORMAL kernel.

## Remaining possible repairs

1. Jointly repairing illegal normal modes works in the finite3D calibration,
   but Sections6--7 show it cannot restore the benchmark uniformly on H_m.
   More such normal modes or altered legal values do not avoid(29).
2. A definite nonnormal polynomial kernel can have correlations absent from
   every normal spectral mixture. Replacing it by an arbitrary Gram form
   would merely restate the original problem and is not a quantitative repair.
3. The legal growing Chebyshev constructions remain valid scoped candidates.
   They do not supply the missing uniform comparison on this recursive family.

Multiplying every interpolant by an arbitrary prescribed L>0 restores the
project's smoothness normalization without changing these normalized bounds.

# Correlated Euclidean stages: a finite calibration and an exact remote cycle

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical draft, max61. No assertion below is a
new accepted fact. This is one frame mechanism and one explicit terminal
correction, within TASK.md and guidance42998ccb0a64c7b8; the later guidance
2915a98c60a7ec96 leaves this allocation unchanged. No mathematical program,
numerical experiment, solver, executed recurrence, formal submission,
installation, consult or sub-agent was used.

The result is a complete six-step, two-checkpoint static-function
calibration with changing gradients on BOTH incoming ordinary blocks,
followed by a value-independent three-query obstruction to the natural
repeated gluing rule. The calibration is not an all-word checkpoint lemma.
The three-query obstruction does not exclude every correlated realization.

All displayed calculations are joint proofs in this draft, not deductions
from unverified shared claims. Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 is used for the
project's directed-row convention and scope. We also prove the required
envelope and row identities directly. Throughout the payoff is W=2R:

    2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L dist(x_0,argmin f)^2).

## 1. Actual frame, primal metric, dual metric, and support arcs

Let e_0,...,e_k be an orthonormal basis and 0<=r<1. Write
s=sqrt(1-r^2), u_0=e_0 and u_(i+1)=-r*u_i+s*e_(i+1).
Here r is the task's r_corr, not a covariance law for queried gradients.
Induction, using the new coordinate e_(i+1), gives

    ||u_i||=1, <u_i,u_j>=(-r)^|i-j|.                     (1)

Thus, for arbitrary real coefficients a_i,

    ||sum_i a_i u_i||^2
      =sum_i a_i^2+2 sum_(i<j) (-r)^(j-i) a_i a_j.      (2)

The coefficients are NOT orthonormal coordinates. If b_i=<u_i,x>,
the orthonormal coordinates of x satisfy

    <e_0,x>=b_0,
    <e_i,x>=(b_i+r*b_(i-1))/s, i>=1,
    ||x||^2=b_0^2+sum_(i=1)^k (b_i+r*b_(i-1))^2/s^2. (3)

For k>=1 the inverse of the Gram matrix in (1) is tridiagonal:
its two end diagonal entries are 1/s^2, its interior diagonal entries
are (1+r^2)/s^2, and each adjacent off-diagonal entry is r/s^2.
This follows by expanding (3), since b=U^T x for U with columns u_i.
For k=0 both matrices are [1]. In particular constant positive inner
products b_i=d require EXACTLY

    ||x||^2=d^2[1+k*(1+r)/(1-r)].                       (4)

This is a conditional radius cost for that prescribed array, not a
claim that every correlated witness must pay it.

Absolute row sums of the Gram matrix and its inverse give

    (1-r)/(1+r)<=lambda_min(Gram)
       <=lambda_max(Gram)<=(1+r)/(1-r).                (5)

For fixed r and growing k both extremes are approached: insert the
constant and alternating-sign coefficient columns in (2), divide by
k+1, and sum the resulting geometric series. The Rayleigh quotients
tend (1-r)/(1+r) and (1+r)/(1-r), respectively. Consequently the
conditioning can approach ((1+r)/(1-r))^2. If k*(1-r) tends to0,
the alternating-sign Rayleigh quotient is (k+1)(1+o(1)); an interior
diagonal entry of the inverse also implies
lambda_min(Gram)<=s^2/(1+r^2) for k>=2. Both types of degeneration
are relevant, depending on the joint limit.

Fix epsilon>0 and define the endpoint and its actual Euclidean data by

    w_i=epsilon*u_i-u_(i+1)
       =(epsilon+r)*u_i-s*e_(i+1),
    omega=sqrt(1+epsilon^2+2*epsilon*r),
    theta=arctan(s/(epsilon+r)).                        (6)

In the ORTHONORMAL plane (u_i,e_(i+1)), put

    E_i(t)=cos(t)*u_i-sin(t)*e_(i+1),
    T_i(t)=-sin(t)*u_i-cos(t)*e_(i+1),
    c=omega*exp(kappa*theta),
    V_i(t)=c*exp(-kappa*t)*E_i(t), 0<=t<=theta,
    N_i(t)=E_i(t)+kappa*T_i(t), kappa>0.               (7)

The endpoints are c*u_i and w_i. If delta=z-t, then

    <N_i(t),V_i(z)>/[c*exp(-kappa*t)]
      =exp(-kappa*delta)*(cos(delta)+kappa*sin(delta)).

Its derivative in delta is
-(1+kappa^2)*exp(-kappa*delta)*sin(delta). As |delta|<pi/2,
the expression has its maximum1 at0; its inner product with the
additional slope0 is strictly smaller. Thus N_i(t) supports the
convex hull of0 and the whole arc at V_i(t). At t=0, e_(i+1) is
also a supporting normal. This verifies the actual Euclidean arc.

Writing V_i(t)=p*u_i-q*u_(i+1) gives

    p=c*exp(-kappa*t)*(cos(t)-r*sin(t)/s),
    q=c*exp(-kappa*t)*sin(t)/s,
    p>=epsilon*q>=0, p<=c.                            (8)

For t>0 the first inequality follows from cot(t)>=(epsilon+r)/s;
at t=0 it follows from p=c,q=0.
For two such slopes in consecutive stages one has EXACTLY

    <p*u_i-q*u_(i+1), p'*u_(i+1)-q'*u_(i+2)>
       =-(r*p+q)*(p'+r*q').                           (9)

For stage separation d>=1 the right side is multiplied by
(-r)^(d-1). This follows by substituting (1) into the four terms.
In particular adjacent slopes have negative inner products, while
slopes two stages apart have positive ones when r>0. No assertion
about Markov dependence of all gradients follows from (9).

## 2. Two genuinely changing ordinary steps and their exact values

This section specifies a finite block without root finding. Let
Delta=theta/2 and define

    A_D=exp(-kappa*Delta)*(cos(Delta)+sin(Delta)/kappa),
    B_D=exp(-kappa*Delta)*sin(Delta)/kappa,
    t_0=0, t_1=B_D, t_2=B_D*(1+A_D),
    theta_l=theta-l*Delta, l=0,1,2,
    R_l=c*exp(-kappa*theta_l),
    v_(i,l)=R_l*E_i(theta_l),
    r_(i,l)=R_l*((1+t_l)*E_i(theta_l)
                            +kappa*t_l*T_i(theta_l)),
    alpha_l=1-exp(-kappa*Delta)*cos(Delta)+B_D
                            +(1+kappa^2)*B_D*t_(l-1), l=1,2. (10)

Both alpha_l are strictly positive. Resolving radial and tangent
components in the basis (E_i(theta_l),T_i(theta_l)) proves

    r_(i,l)-r_(i,l-1)=alpha_l*v_(i,l), l=1,2.          (11)

Indeed, cancellation of the tangent component is exactly
t_l=B_D+A_D*t_(l-1), and the remaining radial coefficient is alpha_l.
The vector r_(i,l)-v_(i,l)=R_l*t_l*N_i(theta_l) supports the
finite slope hull conv{0,v_(i,0),v_(i,1),v_(i,2)} at v_(i,l).
Hence the chronological ordinary word (alpha_2,alpha_1) has gradients
v_(i,2),v_(i,1), followed at its checkpoint query by v_(i,0)=w_i.
These three gradients have different directions when r<1.

Use the following exact coefficients of its initial state:

    r_(i,2)=A*u_i-B*u_(i+1),
    B=c*kappa*t_2/s,
    A=c*(1+t_2)-r*B,
    Z=A+r*B=c*(1+t_2),
    R0^2=||r_(i,2)||^2
         =c^2*((1+t_2)^2+kappa^2*t_2^2).              (12)

By (11), r_(i,2)=w_i+alpha_1*v_(i,1)+alpha_2*v_(i,2).
Consequently A>=epsilon, B>=1 and A-epsilon*B>=0, despite the
subtraction in the formula defining A. The same positive combination
also shows ||r_(i,l)||<=R0: all arc pairs have positive inner product,
and the backward additions in (11) strictly increase the norm.

For any scale a>0 and center C define the finite local nonsmooth piece

    phi_(i,a,C)(y)=max{0, a*<v_(i,l),y-C>:l=0,1,2}.   (13)

At x=C+a*r_(i,l), its envelope with parameter1 has

    g=a*v_(i,l), y=x-g,
    phi_(i,a,C)(y)=a^2*R_l^2*t_l,
    f_local(x)=a^2*R_l^2*(t_l+1/2).                  (14)

These are retained physical values and proximal heights. They will
not be reselected when the pieces are assembled below.

## 3. Complete two-checkpoint calibration, including the terminal correction

For this calibration restrict sqrt(2/3)<=r<1 and 0<epsilon<=1/2, and take

    kappa=2*s/r,
    ell=1+epsilon*r,
    beta=ell/(epsilon+r),
    M=A+beta*B=Z+s^2*B/(epsilon+r),
    a=ell/M, C_0=0, C_1=-w_0-a*r_(1,2).              (15)

The first local scale is1, the second is a. Put

    Hpref=omega+(1+2*a)*(R0+c).                       (16)

Choose ANY real d strictly larger than the following explicit finite
maximum (this is a definition, not a parameter scan):

    max{2*c, 8*c*r*epsilon, sqrt(8*c*r*a*Z),
        c*r/epsilon,
        [1+2*(epsilon+c)/r]/epsilon,
        [Hpref+r*ell]/(epsilon*r)}.                  (17)

Set b_0=2, b_1=1+d/a and choose the actual six-step word

    h=(alpha_2,alpha_1,b_0,alpha_2,alpha_1,b_1).        (18)

The proposed prefix and terminal positions are

    x_0=r_(0,2), x_1=r_(0,1), x_2=w_0,
    x_3=-w_0=C_1+a*r_(1,2),
    x_4=C_1+a*r_(1,1), x_5=C_1+a*w_1,
    x_6=C_1-d*w_1.                                   (19)

The prefix gradients at indices0,...,5 are, in that order,

    v_(0,2),v_(0,1),w_0,a*v_(1,2),a*v_(1,1),a*w_1.  (20)

Equations (11), b_0=2 and b_1=1+d/a prove every GD update
x_(j+1)=x_j-h_j*g_j, including both checkpoints.

Define a terminal center and ONE static max-affine function by

    C_T=x_6-d*u_2,
    J(y)=max{phi_(0,1,0)(y),phi_(1,a,C_1)(y),
                                      d*<u_2,y-C_T>},
    F(x)=min_y [J(y)+||x-y||^2/2].                    (21)

There are seven nonzero affine planes and the plane0, all fixed before
the run. We next prove that (19)-(20), with exactly (14), are the
actual prefix jets of F and compute its actual terminal jet.

### 3.1 Future stage planes at all first-block proximal points

A nonzero first-block normal r_(0,l)-v_(0,l) is a positive multiple
of N_0(theta_l). Directly,

    <u_1,N_0(t)>=-r*cos(t)-s*sin(t)
                          +kappa*(r*sin(t)-s*cos(t))<0, (22)

because tan(t)<=s/(epsilon+r)<s/r. Every stage1 slope has component
p'+r*q'>0 along u_1 and its other component along e_2. It therefore
has nonpositive inner product with every first-block proximal point.
Moreover, for any stage1 slope v=p'*u_1-q'*u_2,

    <v,-C_1>=-ell*(p'+r*q')
                    +a*((p'+r*q')*A+(r*p'+q')*B)<=0. (23)

Here (r*p'+q')/(p'+r*q')<=beta, by (8), and a*M=ell.
Equations (22)-(23) prove that every future stage1 plane is
nonpositive throughout the first block, including its checkpoint.

### 3.2 Every old plane at all second-block proximal points

Let an old slope be v=p*u_0-q*u_1. At the second-block endpoint
proximal point C_1 its inner product is

    <v,C_1>=-epsilon*(p+r*q)-(r*p+q)
                                     +a*(r*p+q)*Z.  (24)

The ratio (p+r*q)/(r*p+q) is at least
(epsilon+r)/ell. Thus (24) is nonpositive provided
a*Z<=omega^2/ell. This holds because

    a*Z=ell*Z/M<ell, omega^2-ell^2=epsilon^2*s^2>0.   (25)

At an earlier second-block proximal point, its displacement from
C_1 is a*(r_(1,l)-v_(1,l)). The old slope has inner product

    <v,r_(1,l)-v_(1,l)>
      =-(r*p+q)*R_l*t_l*(cos(theta_l)-kappa*sin(theta_l)).

The stated restriction r>=sqrt(2/3) gives

    kappa*tan(theta)<=1.                              (26)

Indeed (26) is equivalent to 2*(1-r^2)<=r*(epsilon+r).
Consequently the cosine difference and t_l are nonnegative, so the
old plane value at every earlier proximal point is at most its
value at C_1. Thus every old plane is nonpositive and the old
piece, which includes the plane0, equals0 there. This pays the
actual normal geometry in the second-block maximum.

### 3.3 The terminal plane is inactive throughout the prefix

Every prefix proximal point has norm at most Hpref. For the first
block use ||r_(0,l)-v_(0,l)||<=R0+c. For the second use
||C_1||<=omega+a*R0 and then add a*(R0+c); (16) bounds both.
From (19)-(21),

    <u_2,C_T>=-r*ell+a*(r*A+B)+epsilon*r*d>Hpref,     (27)

by (17) and A,B>0. Hence the terminal plane is negative at every
prefix proximal point and at0. Equations (23)-(25) also show the
second piece is zero at0; the first piece is zero there by definition.
Thus J(0)=0, min J=0, and every claimed prefix proximal point has
the indicated local plane active in the global maximum. Its local
gradient in (20) is a subgradient of J there, so (14) gives the
actual prefix values and heights of F. No earlier plane, value or
height has been removed.

### 3.4 The direct terminal jet fails, and its exact correction

The tempting pure new-direction terminal jet would be

    g_6^ideal=d*u_2, f_6^ideal=d^2/2,
    y_6^ideal=C_T, proximal height0.                   (28)

Let

    K0=a*Z+epsilon*d-1,
    V=c*(r*K0-epsilon),
    Dmix=c^2+d^2-2*c*d*r^2.                           (29)

The first cap plane at C_T has value V. Its original interpolation
row with the retained first jet and (28) is EXACTLY

    Q_(0,6)^ideal=V>0.                                (30)

This follows either by inserting (14) into Q, or by canceling the
first cap's affine height to obtain c*<u_0,C_T>. The fifth entry
in (17) makes K0>2*(epsilon+c)/r, which proves V>0.
This is a failure of the specified terminal jet, not a
value-independent cycle. For six steps it is an incoming terminal
row; using that jet to begin any further ordinary block makes it
a required queried-prefix row. The full-function request cannot
ignore it. We do not claim that (30) alone excludes value reselection.

The ONE correction in this calibration is to keep J unchanged and
use its actual mixed terminal gradient. Put

    eta=V/Dmix,
    g_6=(1-eta)*d*u_2+eta*c*u_0,
    y_6=x_6-g_6.                                     (31)

We prove 0<eta<1 and that these are the actual terminal data. First,
Dmix>=(d-c)^2>d^2/4, while

    V<c*r*a*Z+c*r*epsilon*d<d^2/4

by the first three entries in (17). Thus eta is strictly between0
and1. For every 0<=t<=1 set y(t)=C_T+t*(d*u_2-c*u_0).
The old cap minus the terminal plane at y(t) is V-t*Dmix, so they
tie at t=eta.

To check ALL other planes, project y(t) into (u_0,e_1). Its coordinates
are

    (r*(K0+t*d*r)-epsilon-t*c, -s*(K0+t*d*r)).        (32)

Since kappa*r-s=s and K0>2*(epsilon+c)/r, its first coordinate
is positive and its downward second magnitude is at most kappa
times that first coordinate. Therefore (32) is a nonnegative
combination of N_0(0)=u_0-kappa*e_1 and e_1. Both support the
first-stage hull at c*u_0. The cap dominates every first-stage
plane, and its value is positive.

For any second-stage slope v=p'*u_1-q'*u_2, direct expansion gives

    <v,y(t)-C_1>
       =-d*epsilon*(p'+r*q')-t*d*(r*p'+q')
                                     +t*c*r*(p'+r*q')<=0, (33)

because d*epsilon>c*r. Thus no second-stage plane intervenes.
At y_6 the old cap and terminal plane tie above all remaining
planes, and their convex combination in (31) belongs to partial J(y_6).
As x_6-y_6=g_6, this proves the proximal condition exactly.

Evaluating the envelope yields

    F(x_6)=d^2/2+V^2/(2*Dmix).                        (34)

For an explicit check, the envelope value associated to the mixture
t is d^2/2+t*V-t^2*Dmix/2: at the tie, use the weighted average
of the two active affine values at x_6 and subtract half the squared
norm of their averaged slope. Substitution of eta gives (34).
This changes only the illegal proposed terminal jet (28); every
prefix value and every prefix proximal height in (14) is unchanged.

### 3.5 Global smoothness, every directed row, and the exact radius

The function J is a finite convex maximum, nonnegative and zero at0.
For every x, J(y)+||x-y||^2/2 is coercive and strictly convex, hence
has a unique minimizer y. The optimality condition is x-y in partial J(y):
the forward implication follows by testing y+t(z-y) and letting t
decrease to0, and the reverse follows by completing the squared norm.
For two pairs write g=x-y and g'=x'-y'. The two subgradient
inequalities imply

    ||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>.

Using y as a trial point at x+w gives the upper estimate
F(x+w)<=F(x)+<g,w>+||w||^2/2; the subgradient inequality gives
F(x+w)>=F(x)+<g,w>. These bounds prove differentiability with
gradient g, convexity, and a globally1-Lipschitz gradient.
The minimum is0 and argmin F={J=0}.

For this calibration the least global Lipschitz constant is actually1.
At0 the planes with slopes0,c*u_0,w_0,a*w_1 are all active; the
last one is active because a*M=ell makes its inequality in (23)
an equality. The three nonzero slopes are linearly independent
by (1),(6), so their convex hull with0 has nonempty Euclidean
interior and belongs to partial J(0). At every input in that
interior the proximal point is0 and grad F(x)=x. Thus no global
gradient Lipschitz constant strictly below1 is possible.

For any two actual samples, terminal and minimizers included,
write H_i=J(y_i), f_i=H_i+||g_i||^2/2 and x_i=y_i+g_i. Then

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =H_i-H_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i><=0.                  (35)

This proves every old/new directed interpolation row, in both
orientations, without a matrix of unchecked remote entries.

Finally x_0=r_(0,2)=w_0+alpha_1*v_(0,1)+alpha_2*v_(0,2)
is a nonnegative combination of first-stage slopes active at0.
Every z with J(z)=0 has nonpositive inner product with each of
these slopes, hence <x_0,z><=0. Consequently

    ||x_0-z||^2>=||x_0||^2+||z||^2,
    dist(x_0,argmin F)^2=R0^2,                       (36)

and0 is its unique closest minimizer. The complete finite conclusion is

    W_6(h)>=[d^2+V^2/Dmix]/R0^2.                     (37)

The function L*F has smoothness bound L, uses precisely the same
positions for physical steps h_i/L, and gives the same normalized
payoff. There is no half-sum, preconditioning or concealed step rescaling.

## 4. The first remote cycle of the repeated pure-stage rule

The terminal mixture above is not the prescribed initial gradient of
another fresh local arc. Here is the exact failure if one instead
continues the locally maximal, pure-stage zero-height gluing rule.
It uses the SAME frame and arcs, now in R^4 to allow u_3, not a
new mechanism or an attempted second correction.

Retain the parameters and the two-step ordinary block (10). Choose
T0>0 and T1>0, and define

    a_0=1, a_1=T0*ell/M, a_2=T1*ell/M,
    C_0=0,
    C_1=-T0*w_0-a_1*r_(1,2),
    C_2=C_1-T1*w_1-a_2*r_(2,2),
    b_0=1+T0, b_1=1+T1/a_1.                         (38)

The proposed trajectory consists of the three ordinary blocks with
their translated/scaled local data (14), with the two checkpoints
b_0,b_1 between them. Every GD update agrees with (11),(38).
To keep the three decisive samples strictly in the queried prefix,
take the physical nine-step word

    (alpha_2,alpha_1,b_0,alpha_2,alpha_1,b_1,
                                      alpha_2,alpha_1,1). (39)

The final ordinary step1 is executed from the third endpoint query;
it does not introduce a third selected checkpoint. The endpoint
queries at physical indices2,5,8 have proposed data

    X_i=C_i+G_i, G_i=a_i*w_i, F_i=||G_i||^2/2,
    i=0,1,2, corresponding respectively to indices2,5,8. (40)

These are local data, not claimed jets of a common function.
Define the positive quantities

    z=ell*Z/M,
    H=epsilon+r-r*z
       =s^2*[epsilon+r*ell*B/((epsilon+r)*M)],
    K=omega^2-ell*z
       =s^2*[epsilon^2+ell^2*B/((epsilon+r)*M)].      (41)

All equalities follow from M-Z=s^2*B/(epsilon+r) and
omega^2-ell^2=epsilon^2*s^2. In particular H,K>0.
For the original row convention the following is an EXACT
value-independent cycle:

    Q_(2,8)+Q_(8,5)+Q_(5,2)
                 =-T0*K+T1*ell*H.                  (42)

Proof: with the convenient test values in (40), the elementary
cancellation in Q gives Q_(i,j)=<G_i,C_j-C_i>. Now

    <w_1,w_0>=-(epsilon+r)*ell,
    <w_i,r_(i,2)>=(epsilon+r)*M,
    <w_0,r_(1,2)>=-ell*Z,
    <w_0,r_(2,2)>=r*ell*Z.

These follow from (6),(9),(12). Therefore

    Q_(5,2)=a_1*[-T0*(epsilon+r)*ell
                                      +a_1*(epsilon+r)*M]=0,
    Q_(8,5)=a_2*[-T1*(epsilon+r)*ell
                                      +a_2*(epsilon+r)*M]=0,
    Q_(2,8)=-T0*omega^2+a_1*ell*Z
                          +T1*(epsilon+r)*ell-a_2*r*ell*Z
             =-T0*K+T1*ell*H.

The function values cancel in the sum of ANY three original Q rows
around this cycle. Thus calculating it with (40) proves (42) for
EVERY possible reassignment of all values on these same positions
and gradients. Translation of all positions also leaves it unchanged.
There is no minimizer in this cycle.

In particular, take the explicit positive choice

    T1=2*T0*K/(ell*H).                                (43)

Then the cycle equals T0*K>0. At least one of its three original
queried-prefix rows is positive, independently of the entire value
array, of any choice or relocation of the minimizer, and of all
unqueried terminal data. No convex1-smooth function realizes these
jets. This is stronger than the single failed terminal row (30).

The rejected hypothesis is precise: identical chosen local arcs and
ordinary blocks, local endpoint proximal heights0, Euclidean GD,
and maximal adjacent scales a_(i+1)=T_i*ell/M as in (38), with
T1/T0>K/(ell*H). It is not a theorem about all scale choices, all
height-bearing interpolants, arbitrary arcs, or all correlated frames.
The local blocks themselves remain legal. Their pairwise gluing
does not extend to the third block by that rule.

The six-step calibration in Section3 is not advertised as a repair
of the nine-step word (39). It gives the exact static function and
actual mixed terminal jet BEFORE demanding a third pure local block.
No part of the failed third block is silently discarded from a claim
about that same longer word. No second correction of (42) is attempted.

## 5. Local activation, transfer, radius, and turning costs

The following accounts for the apparent small-angle gain and its
limits. It does not turn the failed repeated rule into a lower bound.

For a general ordered nonnegative ordinary word, a backward arc
trajectory can be constructed in the actual orthonormal plane.
Start at w=(epsilon+r,-s). Suppose the current backward point is
(X,-Y), projected to V(theta_j), with positive X,Y. For a step alpha,
if

    Y<=kappa*[X+(alpha-1)*c],

the preceding projection is c*u and the preceding point is obtained
by adding alpha*c*u. Put mu=X+(alpha-1)*c; its normal is
mu*N(0)+(kappa*mu-Y)*e_new, with nonnegative coefficients.
Otherwise choose the unique angle t in (0,theta_j] for which

    <(X,-Y),E(t)-T(t)/kappa>/[c*exp(-kappa*t)]=1-alpha. (44)

The left side equals1 at theta_j. Its value at0 is
(X-Y/kappa)/c<1-alpha, and its derivative is
(kappa+1/kappa)*(X*cos(t)+Y*sin(t))/[c*exp(-kappa*t)]>0.
The root therefore exists uniquely. Resolving radial and tangent
components shows that adding alpha*V(t) gives a nonnegative
supporting normal at that slope. The tangent coefficient is
nonnegative because the polar angle of the current point is at
least theta_j and the new angle is no larger. Backward addition
preserves X>0,Y>0 and Y/X<=s/(epsilon+r). A capped point stays
capped in all earlier steps. These observations inductively prove
the asserted local construction, including alpha=0.

At an uncapped point use the representation R*((1+t)*E+kappa*t*T).
For two consecutive backward angles separated by Delta>=0, its
normal coefficient obeys exactly

    t_old=B(Delta)+A(Delta)*t_new,
    A(Delta)=exp(-kappa*Delta)*(cos(Delta)+sin(Delta)/kappa),
    B(Delta)=exp(-kappa*Delta)*sin(Delta)/kappa.        (45)

Let d0=1-kappa^2. Differentiation gives
A'-(d0/kappa)*A=-(1+1/kappa^2)*exp(-kappa*Delta)*sin(Delta)<=0.
For kappa<1, A<=exp(d0*Delta/kappa) and
1+d0*B<=exp(d0*Delta/kappa); the latter follows from
B<=Delta/kappa and exp(t)>=1+t. Hence telescoping (45) yields

    t<=T=[exp((1-kappa^2)*theta/kappa)-1]/(1-kappa^2).

For kappa>=1, A<=1 and B<=Delta/kappa instead give t<=T=theta/kappa.
These bounds also bound the vertical coordinate at the first cap,
which is unchanged by subsequent backward cap additions.

Write the resulting initial point as A_b*u_i-B_b*u_(i+1), and let
S be the physical sum of this ordinary block. Equation (8) implies

    A_b=epsilon+P, B_b=1+Q,
    0<=P<=c*S, Q>=0, P>=epsilon*Q,
    B_b<=Bmax=c*[(1+T)/omega+(kappa/s)*T].             (46)

For the last bound use sin(t)<=sin(theta)=s/omega and R<=c in
the downward orthonormal coordinate R*((1+t)*sin(t)+kappa*t*cos(t)),
then divide by s. These are actual vector coefficients, with (2)
still required for all norms.

If the local trajectory starts at D*u_i, is scaled by a, and its
center is D*u_i-a*(A_b*u_i-B_b*u_(i+1)), its required zero value
at the old origin is equivalent to

    D/a>=M_b:=A_b+beta*B_b.                            (47)

Indeed divide each endpoint or arc plane inequality at0 by its
positive factor p+r*q. The ratio (r*p+q)/(p+r*q) is maximized
at the endpoint p/q=epsilon, giving beta. Thus (47) is necessary
as well as sufficient for that zero set; it is not only a loose
displacement estimate. In particular

    M_b<=c*S+Aadd, Aadd=epsilon+beta*Bmax.             (48)

For the source-style buffer point which retains the coefficient
of u_(i+1) just before a checkpoint, the plane residual is proportional to

    (1-b)*epsilon*(p+r*q)+(r*p+q).

Consequently that particular buffer has the exact threshold

    b>=1+beta/epsilon.                               (49)

The actual post-checkpoint point, with its additional b*a*u_(i+1),
is in the current piece's zero set already for b>=1. Equation (49)
prices an extra zero-region buffer. It must not be imposed as a
necessary threshold for every correlated construction: Section3
uses b_0=2 and proves its cross-plane conditions directly.

For consecutive stages, a preceding checkpoint with T_i=(b_i-1)*a_i
can supply the next scale only up to

    a_(i+1)<=T_i*ell/M_(i+1)                           (50)

if the next endpoint plane is to vanish at the preceding center.
This follows by exactly the calculation (23). When (26) holds,
the other direction's old-plane conditions also hold for the local
arc normals. Thus (50) is a legitimate ADJACENT transfer bound.
The gain ell is real Euclidean geometry and cannot be dropped or
replaced by an orthogonal-coordinate transfer formula.

The full local initial norm is

    ||A_b*u_i-B_b*u_(i+1)||^2
       =A_b^2+B_b^2+2*r*A_b*B_b.                    (51)

For the actual calibration (36) supplies its closest-minimizer
interpretation; (51) alone does not establish such an interpretation
for an arbitrary global gluing. If an initial state includes other
stages, its full norm must instead use (2), or (3) for prescribed
inner products. Neither can be replaced by a sum of stage squares.

### 5.1 Limits as r tends to1

Keep 0<epsilon<=1/2 fixed and kappa=2*s/r. Then

    theta/s -> 1/(1+epsilon),
    theta/kappa -> 1/[2*(1+epsilon)],
    c -> 1+epsilon,
    T -> exp(1/[2*(1+epsilon)])-1,
    Bmax -> (1+T)+2*(1+epsilon)*T,
    beta -> 1.                                      (52)

Thus the LOCAL activation allowance (48) stays finite: the
orthogonal spiral's exponential in 1/kappa has not been imported
into this different Euclidean angle. This observation is paid for
by the actual geometry, but it is not a global checkpoint theorem.

For the specific two-step ordinary block let

    tau=1/[4*(1+epsilon)], Ttwo=2*tau+tau^2.

Equations (10)-(12),(15) give, without executing a recurrence,

    alpha_1 -> tau, alpha_2 -> tau*(1+tau), t_2 -> Ttwo,
    B -> 2*(1+epsilon)*Ttwo,
    M,Z -> (1+epsilon)*(1+Ttwo),
    a -> 1/(1+Ttwo),
    R0^2 -> (1+epsilon)^2*(1+Ttwo)^2.                 (53)

These limits are finite and positive. The threshold (17) remains
bounded for each fixed epsilon; near r=1 it is O(1/epsilon) as
epsilon tends to0, with constants bounded for 0<epsilon<=1/2.
Thus a fixed sufficiently large d satisfies (17) for all r close
enough to1. For such a fixed d,

    V -> (1+epsilon)*epsilon*d,
    Dmix -> [d-(1+epsilon)]^2,
    eta -> (1+epsilon)*epsilon*d/[d-(1+epsilon)]^2.    (54)

The correction is not identically zero in this small-angle limit.
For a prospective next physical step h, the actual next position
minus the position predicted using the pure jet is EXACTLY

    h*(g_6^ideal-g_6)=h*eta*(d*u_2-c*u_0),
    norm = h*V/sqrt(Dmix).                            (55)

For large d and then r near1 this has scale h*(1+epsilon)*epsilon.
There is no uniform bound over arbitrary subsequent physical h.
One cannot carry the improved terminal value (34) into a further
ordinary block while silently restoring the pure terminal gradient.

### 5.2 Accumulated factors and the exact surviving cycle cost

An adjacent-only multiplication of (48),(50), for positive ordinary
sums S_i, would incur the squared coefficient factor (c/ell)^(2k),
activation factors and the checkpoint numerators b_i-1. Its exact
logarithmic loss relative to the purely diagnostic product using
b_i/S_i is

    2*k*log(c/ell)+2*sum_i log(1+Aadd/(c*S_i))
                                      -2*sum_i log(1-1/b_i). (56)

This is only an accounting identity for the proposed adjacent
estimate. Because (42) can be positive, it is NOT an actual
lower bound for a repeated k-stage witness. When S_i=0 use the
undivided denominators in (48); the diagnostic (56) is not defined.

The Euclidean coefficient in (56) satisfies EXACTLY

    log(c/ell)=kappa*theta
                    +(1/2)*log(1+epsilon^2*s^2/ell^2). (57)

The ell transfer cancels the leading endpoint-norm factor. In
particular, with epsilon fixed and r tending to1,

    log(c/ell)/s^2
      -> 2/(1+epsilon)+epsilon^2/[2*(1+epsilon)^2].   (58)

Keeping this coefficient loss negligible as k grows would require
k*s^2 small. But then r^d is near1 for all stage separations
d<=k: none of the remote stages decouples.

This is not just a conditioning warning. Set
zeta=2*Ttwo/(1+Ttwo). The exact remote cycle constants in (41)
obey

    H/s^2 -> epsilon+zeta,
    K/s^2 -> epsilon^2+(1+epsilon)*zeta,
    K/(ell*H)
      -> [epsilon^2+(1+epsilon)*zeta]
                          /[(1+epsilon)*(epsilon+zeta)] < 1. (59)

The strict inequality follows because the denominator exceeds the
numerator by epsilon. In particular even the equal-budget choice
T1=T0 fails for every r sufficiently close to1, and

    [Q_(2,8)+Q_(8,5)+Q_(5,2)]/(T0*s^2) -> epsilon>0. (60)

A small positive interpolation defect is still incompatible with
an exact common function. Equation (60) is not permission to omit
it at each stage or to average illegal realizations. The specific
saturated repeated pilot is already excluded at this finite length;
it has no legal unbounded-stage scaling to price as a payoff.

For reference, the entire old-plane memory is also explicit. With
possibly differing local starts A_i*u_i-B_i*u_(i+1), let
Z_i=A_i+r*B_i, T_i=(b_i-1)*a_i and retain the center update
C_(i+1)=C_i-T_i*w_i-a_(i+1)*r_(i+1,start).
For every j<i,

    <u_j,C_i-C_j>
      =-(epsilon+r)*T_j
       -sum_(t=j+1)^(i-1) (-r)^(t-j)
                                  [a_t*Z_t+(epsilon+r)*T_t]
       -(-r)^(i-j)*a_i*Z_i.                          (61)

Every old cap at a proposed zero-height endpoint requires the
right side to be nonpositive. It follows by expanding the center
recursion with <u_j,r_(t,start)>=(-r)^(t-j)*Z_t and
<u_j,w_t>=(-r)^(t-j)*(epsilon+r). Thus all old centers and all
physical displacements remain present. Absolute geometric weights
sum to (1-r^(i-j))/(1-r); their effective number grows on the scale
min{i-j,1/(1-r)}. Their signs may matter; this absolute sum is not
claimed to be an unavoidable radius loss. In the small total-turning
regime the full old-plane memory has order k, not order1.

## 6. Smoothness overlap if a sum were substituted

The actual construction (21) uses one envelope and pays global
smoothness1 exactly as proved, irrespective of r. There is no
overlap factor in (37). A half-sum argument from orthogonal stages
would be invalid here without a new proof.

To quantify that statement, let P_i be the orthogonal projector onto
span(u_i,e_(i+1)). Since these two vectors are orthonormal,
P_i=u_i*u_i^T+e_(i+1)*e_(i+1)^T. Consequently

    sum_(i=0)^(k-1) P_i+u_k*u_k^T
       =U*U^T+sum_(j=1)^k e_j*e_j^T=:Aov,
    lambda_max(Gram)<=lambda_max(Aov)
                              <=lambda_max(Gram)+1. (62)

For the concrete half-sum of local quadratic functions

    Fquad(x)=(1/4)*[sum_(i<k)||P_i*x||^2+<u_k,x>^2],

the TRUE least gradient Lipschitz constant is
lambda_max(Aov)/2, because its Hessian is Aov/2. For fixed k and
r tending to1, Aov tends
(k+1)*e_0*e_0^T+sum_(j=1)^k e_j*e_j^T, so this constant tends
(k+1)/2. In particular it exceeds1 when k>=2 and r is sufficiently
near1. This refutes the generic old half-sum bound in these spans.
It does not assign that least constant to our particular local
envelopes or claim they all attain their largest Hessians together.
A sum construction would have to prove its own global constant
and charge the corresponding division of every physical step.

## 7. Source audit, status, and the exact missing interface

The immutable assemblies c8fcf4ced5a34046 and6be9249882fd0868 were
read before this construction. Their single-envelope and half-sum
proofs use orthogonal physical stage coordinates and particular
zero-region bounds. They are not premises here. The full evidence
of138b57940a77871b was recovered from GM after its proposed shared
mirror path returned "No such file or directory". Its fixed endpoint
and prescribed zero-height minimum impose its own original endpoint
row. Changing the actual Euclidean angle changes that local row to
(47), but does not erase the new global cycles (42).

The primary source Yuhan Ye and Kaizhao Liu, Silver Rate Is (Almost)
Optimal for Gradient Descent, arXiv:2609.09152v2, 2026, was opened on
the web and its existing exact local PDF extraction was read at
Appendix A.3. Its orthogonal overlap and half-physical-step argument
was checked against (62). It supplies context, not a black-box
theorem used in this proof. Matlas returned three unrelated results
about convex-hull membership, penalized proximal methods and a
descent lemma; none was imported. No new external theorem is a premise.

The actual rejected hypotheses of75d019fd344b8136 were checked:
it has constant-gradient halves, its prescribed maximal reset-norm
law, a particular signed carrier Gram, and its positive original
row(16,35), with a separate value-rigidity argument. Those are not
our changing arc gradients or our common static max. Similarly,
88f3db7b0a2bb506 concerns a specified Haar-direction rank-one
gradient recurrence and its cycle on physical queries1,2,3. We did
not rename or re-use that recurrence. Our new cycle is the explicit
three-endpoint cycle (42), on queries2,5,8 of (39).

Accepted0e7e78056dc6258c assumes orthogonality of the expected inner
products of EVERY pair of queried gradients. Equation (9) already
violates that hypothesis in the present deterministic example. No
randomization or averaging of illegal transcripts has been used.

What is established only at joint-draft level:

* A full actual Euclidean metric calculation and local support-arc
  construction, including activation, adjacent transfer and thresholds.
* One complete two-checkpoint six-step static envelope, all physical
  updates, every retained value and proximal height, every directed row,
  a common attained minimum and its EXACT closest radius (36).
* The sole correction's actual terminal mixture and value (31),(34).
* An exact value-independent queried-prefix cycle excluding the
  saturated repeated rule (38) whenever T1/T0>K/(ell*H), with its
  precise small-angle scaling (59)-(60).
* Full metric and overlap accounting. The simple initial norm of the
  calibration stays bounded; the dual-frame cost (3)-(4) applies only
  when the corresponding coordinate constraints are actually prescribed.

The missing unbounded interface is now specific. A continuation must
construct one static function for arbitrarily many stages, retain the
actual mixtures and their positive proximal heights, prove ALL original
rows including the signed remote constraints replacing (61), and control
the resulting initial closest radius and the accumulated physical-step
effects in (55). Alternatively it must change the adjacent scale/height
policy and give a new all-history proof. Merely multiplying (50), using
the local O(1) activation in (52), or showing a positive stage Gram is
not sufficient; the original cycle (42) survives all value assignments.

Even a successful unbounded-stage interface would still need a uniform
comparison, for every sufficiently large physical horizon n and every
nonnegative word h, with the ORIGINAL all-split benchmark B_n:
for every delta>0 a legal payoff at least (1-delta)/B_n. No such
comparison, no original ruler asymptotic, and no exact-profile theorem
is claimed here. W=2R is unchanged. The original target remains OPEN.

This ends the ONE authorized analytical round after completed60.
The outer loop, not this document, records completed61/attempted62
after return. No resource renewal or formal gate is inferred.

## 8. Local glossary

r is r_corr; s=sqrt(1-r^2); u_i are actual unit frame directions and
e_i orthonormal innovations; Gram is their matrix (1), U its synthesis
matrix. b_i in (3)-(4) are explicitly scoped dual measurements, whereas
b_0,b_1 and b_i elsewhere are physical checkpoint steps. omega is the
actual endpoint norm, theta its actual angle, kappa arc curvature, c
the initial arc magnitude, E_i,T_i radial/tangent unit vectors, V_i
the arc and N_i its supporting normal. A_D,B_D,t_l,theta_l,R_l,
alpha_l,v_(i,l),r_(i,l) define the finite two-step local path.
A,B,Z,R0 describe its actual initial vector and norm, not benchmark
masses. ell=1+epsilon*r, beta=ell/(epsilon+r), M=A+beta*B and a=ell/M
are the adjacent transfer quantities. C_0,C_1,C_T are affine centers;
J is the single static nonsmooth maximum and F its envelope. Hpref is
the explicit prefix proximal-norm bound. d is the terminal amplitude,
V the old-plane violation, Dmix the squared slope difference, eta the
actual mixing weight. T0,T1 in the cycle section are positive physical
checkpoint displacement budgets; H,K there are the positive constants
(41), not functions or Gram matrices. T is the general local normal
bound, Bmax,Aadd its activation allowances. tau,Ttwo,zeta are explicit
small-angle limits. P_i,Aov describe the separate overlap calculation.
Q_ij,W_n retain the accepted project meanings. All new definitions
are local to this joint unverified draft and introduce no verified facts.
