# Clock-balanced terminal pool: endpoint success and a column-capacity obstruction

JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical report. Worker max, invocation86.
Original exact-profile minimax problem OPEN. No formal submission or new fact.

## 1. Outcome and scope

The prescribed new terminal mixture has clock V=2 exactly. Its positive
metric, literal skew completion, all terminal and optimum multipliers, and
full original-space PSD identity are explicit below. Both complete endpoint
row-moment screens pass, uniformly for every enclosing m>=1536:

    first-row S0<-p/16, G0>p/64, P0-b*S0<0;
    last-row Slast<-1/8, Hlast>1/4000, Jlast<0.

Nevertheless the law is impossible, INCLUDING EVERY skew correction C with
Ce=Ct=0. The last queried column has total 2alpha_l, but its invariant
diagonal is P_ll. The exact guards give

    alpha_l>19/20, P_ll<1/3,
    sum_(i!=l)(-N_il)=P_ll-2alpha_l<-3/2.

Consequently at least one original incoming finite multiplier to the last
query is negative. This is a failure of a column mass, not either endpoint
row moment, and not a numerical test of the literal completion alone.

The second mechanism derives a finite transport formulation retaining the
forbidden diagonal AND the full symmetric-factor equality. Its last-column
cut excludes an entire family: terminal mass alpha_l>=1/2, the same
clock-zero endpoint common column, t^T Kt<=3/p, and at least 256p of the
NONLAST metric weight at clocks <=(T+1)/2. Every positive metric satisfying
these conditions fails for every skew completion and every nonnegative
incoming optimum load. This includes a precisely specified family with any
number of sparse interior metric atoms, arbitrary positive last weight,
and a positive tail regularizer. No probabilistic existence theorem is
used or assumed sufficient for the coefficient law.

All these statements are one jointly derived UNVERIFIED proof draft.
Accepted facts1252b8823bb5a290 and931b9653ce0f7201 are used only at their
stated scopes. Earlier unverified reports supply directions, not premises.
This excludes specified coefficient architectures, not the unrestricted
dual cone, not legal primal trajectories, and not the original minimax
problem. It proves no full-class upper bound on W.

## 2. Original problem and accepted interfaces

For a predetermined nonnegative word h of length n, W_n(h) is the supremum
of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2) over every finite Euclidean
dimension, convex differentiable L-smooth f with an attained minimizer,
and x_0!=x_star, using x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Thus W=2R.
The original question asks whether W_n_star/U_n^ZJ tends to1, where
W_n_star=inf_(h>=0)W_n(h), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, and the ALL-SPLIT benchmark is

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt(x^2+6xy+y^2))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)).

Accepted1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the signed interpolation convention and
full terminal-square interpretation. Only incoming edges at the actual
UNQUERIED terminal n may be deleted. No queried gradient or prefix pair
is deleted here. Accepted931b9653ce0f7201 supplies the physical word and
mass formula below, and B_n/D<47/50. Its799/800 separation is CONDITIONAL
on the missing full-class bound W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D) for EVERY m>=1536.
That hypothesis remains unproved.

Fix an arbitrary integer m>=1536 and use its SAME parameters in every child:

    eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps, c=(1+sqrt(2))(1+eps), a=beta/c,
    b=1+a, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), j<m,
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, l=n-1, r=l-1,
    S=sum_(i<n)h_i, D=1+2S, p=1/(1+S), qtar=17/(16D).

Here l and r are last and penultimate QUERIED indices. The terminal n
remains distinct. All short symbols in this report are local aliases;
public names for newly published quantities carry the suffix86, avoiding
any reassignment of project glossary entries. In particular rho below is
a probability VECTOR, not the benchmark exponent. Ltri is a matrix; T is
a scalar clock; superscript T denotes transpose.

Let e be the all-ones column on n queries and e_i its coordinate columns.
Define the strictly increasing clocks

    t_i=1+sum_(j<i)h_j, T=t_l=1/p-b, Delta=T-1,
    hgap=1+beta=2-eps, t_r=T-hgap.

The last three physical steps are (b,hgap,b). The needed scalar bounds are

    12/5<c<5/2, 7/8<beta<1,
    2/5<a<sqrt(2)-1<5/12, 1-2a>a^2,
    7/5<b<17/12, 0<eps<=1/6144.                    (2.1)

For the lower bound on a, sqrt(2)<10/7 gives
 a>7*6143/(17*6145)>2/5; the last inequality is equivalent to
35*6143>34*6145. The upper bound uses beta/(1+eps)<1 and
sqrt(2)<17/12. The strict quadratic inequality follows by squaring
1+a<sqrt(2). Bounds on c follow directly from eps<=1/6144 and these
rational bounds for sqrt(2).

The exact mass is

    1+S_j=(1-vword)2^j+vword*c^j,
    vword=beta/[c(c-2)] in(0,1).

It follows either from the cited mass formula or by substituting in
1+S_(j+1)=2(1+S_j)+a*c^j. Therefore

    c^(-m)<p<2^(-m)<2^(-40),
    3/(4p)<Delta<1/p, Delta-b>1/(2p),
    dclock:=1/Delta satisfies p<dclock<4p/3,
    rclock:=hgap/Delta satisfies 0<rclock<4p.        (2.2)

Every elementary small-p comparison below uses this SAME all-m bound;
there is no limit-only argument or evaluated recurrence.

## 3. Tail probabilities and the exact mixed terminal clock

For integer suffix length Len in[0,n], write sbit(Len) for its binary
digit sum and define

    rtail_Len=product_(j=n-Len)^(n-1)(h_j-1)
             =beta^Len*c^(-sbit(Len)),
    s=rtail_n=beta^n/c^m,
    k_i=h_i product_(i<j<n)(1-h_j),
    u_i=|k_i|, U=sum_i u_i, rho_i=u_i/U.

Here v2(j) is the exponent of2 dividing the positive integer j.
The suffix formula follows from h_(n-j)-1=a*c^v2(j) and
sum_(j=1)^Len v2(j)=Len-sbit(Len). With Len=l-i,

    u_i=rtail_Len+rtail_(Len+1), k_i=(-1)^i u_i,
    u_0=(b/a)s, u_l=b, u_r=a*hgap, u_(l-2)=b*a*beta.

The last three terms exceed4/3,5/8,7/18, whose sum169/72>7/3;
thus U>7/3 and every rho_i is positive. We will use

    0<s<u_0<p^2/64, rho_0<1/10, s<p.               (3.1)

For completeness, 2^m-1>16m^2 for m>=16 follows by induction (the
base is the integer inequality at16, and doubling dominates the next
quadratic). Also beta^(-4m)>(1+1/(4m))^(4m)>=2, so beta^(4m)<1/2.
Then beta^n<2^(-4m). Using b/a<4 and p>c^(-m) gives
u_0/p^2<4 beta^n c^m<4(5/32)^m<1/64. The remaining bounds follow
from U>7/3 and p<2^(-40). All these are symbolic inequalities.

Let Ltri_ii=1, Ltri_ij=h_j for j<i, and zero otherwise. Finite
product telescoping gives

    e^T k=1+s, Ltri^T k=h, t=Ltri*e, t^T k=S.      (3.2)

Define the two endpoint terminal-clock values

    Vtail=t^T[k-(1-p)rho], Vlast=b(1-p).

We need Vtail>32, which is rederived here. Put

    Ztail=sum_(Len=0)^n rtail_Len
         =product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1+beta^(2^j)/c),
    Mtail=sum_(Len=0)^n Len*rtail_Len.

Expansion over binary digits gives

    U=2Ztail-1-s,
    Mtail/Ztail=sum_(j=0)^(m-1)
                       2^j beta^(2^j)/(c+beta^(2^j)).

Choose j with m/2<2^j<=m. Bernoulli's inequality gives
beta^(2^j)>=3/4 and c+beta^(2^j)<7/2, so Mtail/Ztail>3m/28.
Since ns<1 by beta^n<2^(-4m) and n<2^m, a direct index shift yields
sum_i rho_i(l-i)>(Mtail/Ztail-1)/2>3m/56-1/2.
Indeed the numerator of this mean is 2Mtail-Ztail+1-ns, its denominator
is U<2Ztail, and the numerator is positive. The suffix from i through
the last step has mass at least b+(l-i). As
Vtail=(1-p)sum_i rho_i(1/p-t_i) and1-p>1/2,
this proves Vtail>32 for every m>=1536.

Now set the EXACT proposed law

    xiT=(2-Vlast)/(Vtail-Vlast),
    alpha=(1-p)[(1-xiT)e_l+xiT*rho], alpha_star=p,
    v=k-alpha, E=p+s, F=p-s, eta=F/E.

Here Vlast<17/12<2<Vtail. Since b>7/5 and p<1/21,
Vlast>4/3. Hence 2-Vlast<2/3 and
Vtail-Vlast>30, so

    0<xiT<1/45, sum alpha_i=1-p,
    V:=t^T v=(1-xiT)Vlast+xiT Vtail=2,
    alpha_l>19/20, a+p<=v_l<1/2,
    0<v_0<u_0, 0<alpha_0<u_0,
    e^T v=E, eta in(0,1).                           (3.3)

For alpha_l use (1-p)(1-xiT)>(1-p)44/45>19/20.
Since alpha_l<=1-p, v_l>=a+p. Since b<17/12 and alpha_l>19/20,
v_l<7/15<1/2. At index0, alpha_0/u_0=(1-p)xiT/U<1, proving the
source guards. Define

    R=Ltri^T v,
    R_i=h_i(p+sum_(j<=i)alpha_j)-alpha_i
       =h_i(p+sum_(j<i)alpha_j)+(h_i-1)alpha_i>0.

The identity follows by subtracting Ltri^T alpha from (3.2).
In particular R_0=bE-a v_0<2p.

## 4. Complete original-space coefficient identity

Normalize smoothness to1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Keep
x_0,g_0,...,g_n independent formal vectors and substitute ONLY the
actual GD updates. From fact1252b8823bb5a290 use the signed residual

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

For every queried index set y_i=x_i-g_i and delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.
Then

    y=e*x_0-Ltri*g, g=Ltri^(-1)(e*x_0-y),
    x_n=-s*x_0+k^T y.                               (4.1)

The unit-diagonal triangular matrix Ltri is invertible; hence this
coordinate change retains every original queried-gradient direction.
The actual terminal gradient g_n is independent of these coordinates.

For ANY positive weights w_i, let Ww=sum_i w_i and

    K=diag(w)-ww^T/Ww.

For any z with e^T z=E and any zeta with e^T zeta=0 define

    P=K+vv^T+eta zz^T,
    d=zeta+F(v-z),
    A^T=-A, Ae=d, N=Ltri^T(P+A).                    (4.2)

For distinct original indices the literal signed array is

    lambda_(n,star)=2p, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,
    lambda_(j,n)=0 for j in {star,0,...,l},
    lambda_(i,j)=-N_ij for distinct i,j<n,
    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i=(Ne)_i,
    lambda_(star,i)=nu_i=(N^T e)_i-2alpha_i.          (4.3)

Diagonal multipliers vanish. Each matrix entry is explicitly

    N_ij=K_ij+A_ij+h_i sum_(u>i)(K_uj+A_uj)
                            +R_i v_j+eta Zout_i z_j,
    Zout=Ltri^T z.                                  (4.4)

Thus no finite pair is omitted or replaced by a feasibility assumption.
The total finite-prefix outgoing mass at i, including its optimum edge,
is N_ii; the incoming prefix mass is N_ii-2alpha_i; the terminal adds
2alpha_i. Finite-node divergence is zero, terminal divergence is2,
and optimum divergence is-2, even before any sign claim.

For prefix indices, including star,
Q_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i>. The remaining prefix quadratic
is -g^T N y=-<x_0,(Pe-d)^T y>+y^T P y. The terminal contribution
WITH -2f_n included is

    ||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2-||-s*x_0+v^T y||^2
                                         -2sum_i alpha_i delta_i.

Its value terms cancel the prefix divergence terms. Since
Pe=Ev+Fz and eta E^2=p^2-s^2, the full identity is

    M(q):=q||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij
      =||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2+y^T K y
        +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2
        +(q-p^2)||x_0||^2+<x_0,zeta^T y>.           (4.5)

With ybar_w=sum_i w_i y_i/Ww and e^T zeta=0, this becomes

    M(q)=||g_n+s*x_0-v^T y||^2
      +sum_i w_i||y_i-ybar_w+(zeta_i/(2w_i))*x_0||^2
      +eta||z^T y-E*x_0||^2+(q-p^2-J)||x_0||^2,
    J=(1/4)sum_i zeta_i^2/w_i.                      (4.6)

The initial charge is paid once. Necessity of q>=p^2+J follows by
setting scalar x_0=1, y_i=c0-zeta_i/(2w_i),
c0=1+sum_i z_i zeta_i/(2w_i E), and g_n=-s+v^T y;
then every square vanishes. Equation (4.1) reconstructs all gradients.
Thus full PSD is equivalent to q>=p^2+J, on the entire original formal
space, not just on legal trajectories. On the actual common queried
gradient direction g_i=G, put X=t^T Kt, Z=t^T z and theta=t^T zeta.
The entire prefix remainder is

    (q-p^2)||x_0||^2-theta<x_0,G>+(X+eta Z^2)||G||^2.

The common derivative has not been discarded.

## 5. The literal metric, paid column, skew, and all optimum signs

The fixed common column and incoming optimum load are

    z=E(T e_0-e_l)/Delta, Z=t^T z=0,
    ellload=1/64, nu_target=ellload*e_0.

This z is not proportional to v, since its interior coordinates vanish
and every odd interior v_i is strictly negative. Choose

    xiM=a(1-a)-a^3/8, sigma=xiM/(1-xiM), omega=p^2,
    what=e_0+e_l+sigma e_r+omega*rho,
    theta0=2-6p, theta=theta0+ellload,
    Xreq=theta0/p-4+ellload=2/p-10+ellload,
    tbase=sum_i what_i t_i/sum_i what_i,
    Vbase=sum_i what_i(t_i-tbase)^2,
    scale=Xreq/Vbase, w_i=scale*what_i,
    zeta=(theta/Xreq)Kt.                            (5.1)

One has 0<xiM<1/4 and0<sigma<1/3: positivity follows from
1-a-a^2/8>1-5/12-25/1152>0, and xiM<a(1-a)<=1/4.
Every what_i is positive. The two unit endpoints imply
Vbase>=Delta^2/2 by minimizing their two square terms over the center.
Also Xreq>0. Consequently all w_i>0, X=Xreq, e^T zeta=0,
t^T zeta=theta, and

    J=theta^2/(4Xreq).

Useful exact bounds are

    1<theta0<2, 2<theta<3,
    Xreq>(1023/1024)*theta0/p, Xreq<2/p,
    1<Xreq/Delta<2, |Xreq/Delta-2|<8p,
    theta/Xreq>p.                                  (5.2)

For the first lower bound on Xreq, theta0/p>1024*4 and
Xreq=theta0/p-4+ellload. For the ratio, subtract2 from
[2-(10-ellload)p]/[1-(b+1)p]; the numerator difference is
(2b-8+ellload)p, negative in absolute value less than8p after division
by1-(b+1)p>3/4. The lower ratio1 follows directly from the same formula.
Finally theta-pXreq=ellload(1-p)+4p>0.

General multiplication in (4.2) gives

    mu=2pR+Ltri^T zeta,
    nu=Kt+Vv+eta Zz-At-2alpha,
    e^T nu=2pV+theta-2(1-p),
    t^T nu=X+V^2+eta Z^2-2alpha^T t.                (5.3)

The last two identities use e^T A=-d^T and t^T At=0. Here
alpha^T t=1/p-3, V=2 and Z=0, so the two moments in (5.3) are both
ellload, precisely matching nu_target. A literal skew realizing both
Ae=d and this nu is as follows:

    rvec=Kt+2v-2alpha-nu_target,
    tmean=(e^T t)/n, cvec=t-tmean*e, Ve=cvec^T cvec>0,
    A0=(d e^T-e d^T)/n,
    fvec=rvec-tmean*d+(d^T t)e/n,
    A=A0+(fvec cvec^T-cvec fvec^T)/Ve.               (5.4)

The two matching moments imply e^T rvec=-d^T t and t^T rvec=0.
Hence e^T fvec=0 and cvec^T fvec=0. Direct multiplication proves
Ae=d and At=rvec; each summand is skew. Every other compatible skew
is A+C with C^T=-C and Ce=Ct=0. Equations (4.3)-(4.4) and (5.4)
define every actual coefficient, with the literal law taking C=0.

All terminal multipliers are nonnegative. Incoming optimum multipliers
are exactly ellload at index0 and zero elsewhere. Every outgoing
optimum multiplier is strictly positive: zeta_i has the sign of
t_i-tbase, changes sign only once, and has zero sum. Every proper
suffix sum is positive, using either the negative preceding prefix or
the strictly positive remaining tail. If zeta_i<0, then
sum_(j>i)zeta_j>=-zeta_i, giving

    (Ltri^T zeta)_i=zeta_i+h_i sum_(j>i)zeta_j
                                  >=(h_i-1)(-zeta_i)>0.

If zeta_i>=0 the expression is positive as well, including i=l.
Together with R_i>0 this proves mu_i>0 for all queries.

Full PSD also passes. By (5.2),

    J<(theta0*p/4)(1+ellload/theta0)^2*(1024/1023)
      <(4225/8184)p.

Since4225/8184<67/128 and p<1/128, one gets

    p^2+J<(17/32)p<17p/[16(2-p)]=qtar.             (5.5)

Thus PSD and every optimum sign are valid for this SIGNED coefficient
law. The remaining finite coefficients must still be nonnegative to
constitute an upper-bound certificate; Section7 proves they cannot be.

## 6. Both endpoint row-moment screens pass

All identities in this section follow from (4.2)-(5.4), not from
assumed finite multiplier signs. They are unchanged by every C with
Ce=Ct=0.

For the first row define

    S0=sum_(j>0)N_0j,
    P0=sum_(j>0)(t_j-1)N_0j,
    G0=P0-Delta*S0=sum_(j>0)(t_j-T)N_0j.

The first row of Ltri^T is b e^T-a e_0^T. Since
Pe-d=2s v+2Fz-zeta and (P+A)t=2Pt-2alpha-nu, direct multiplication gives

    S0=aP_00+zeta_0+2pR_0-2bs v_0-2bFz_0,
    P0=b(2sV+2FZ-theta)-2a(Pt)_0
                                  +2a alpha_0+a nu_0-mu_0.       (6.1)

If every finite first-row multiplier is nonnegative, then

    S0<=0, Delta*S0<=P0<=b*S0,                      (6.2)

because b<=t_j-1<=Delta for j>0. These necessary conditions comprise
the complete total/first-moment screen for that row; they are not
individual coefficient signs.

Put Y=-(Kt)_0=w_0(tbase-1)>0 and dclock=1/Delta. Normalize clocks by
xclock_i=(t_i-1)/Delta in[0,1]. For any base what define

    B=sum what, M=sum what*xclock, M2=sum what*xclock^2,
    B0=what_0, H=M2-M^2/B,
    f=B0*M/B, k0=B0(1-B0/B).

Here H>=1/2, B<5/2, f>2/5, H<=B/4<5/8, and

    Y=(Xreq/Delta)f/H>1/2,
    K_00/Y=dclock*(B-B0)/M.

Since B-B0<3/2 and M>=1, (2.1)-(2.2) give
 aK_00/Y<(5/6)p, while theta/Xreq>p.
Writing S0=aK_00-theta Y/Xreq+Bsource, the remaining term is

    Bsource=2pR_0-2bs v_0-2bFz_0+a(v_0^2+eta z_0^2)<7p^2.

For this use R_0<2p, v_0<u_0<p^2/64, and0<z_0<2p.
Therefore

    S0<-p/12+7p^2<-p/16.                           (6.3)

### 6.1 First-row clock, with finite errors and the load retained

Write K_00=Xreq/Delta^2+eK and Y=Xreq/Delta+eY. Expansion of (6.1)
using R_0=bE-a v_0, z_0=ET/Delta and Z=0 yields

    G0=Gendpoint+Epert,
    Epert=a(2eY-Delta eK)+theta(Delta-a)eY/Xreq,
    Gendpoint=(a/Delta)[btheta0-4-(p^2-s^2)T^2]
       +2bs(2-ET)
       +v_0[2apT+2bsDelta-4a]-aDelta v_0^2+2a alpha_0.           (6.4)

The incoming load cancels exactly in Gendpoint: before cancellation its
first bracket is Xreq-Ttheta-(p^2-s^2)T^2 and there is +a nu_0;
Xreq-Ttheta=btheta0-4-Delta*nu_0. This uses the ACTUAL load ellload,
not a zero-load substitution.

As theta0=2-6p and pT=1-bp,

    btheta0-4-(p^2-s^2)T^2
                  =2b-5-4bp-b^2p^2+s^2T^2.

The bounds (2.1)-(3.3) now give

    |Gendpoint-a*dclock*(2a-3)|<20p^2.              (6.5)

Here the term a*dclock*(4bp+b^2p^2) is <7p^2; the s^2 term is
<s^2/p<p^2; 2bs|2-ET|<6s<p^2; the v_0 bracket has absolute value
<4 and contributes <p^2; aDelta v_0^2<p^2 and2a alpha_0<p^2.
The displayed20p^2 safely bounds their sum.

For an AUXILIARY calculation omit omega*rho only, retaining unit masses
at0 and1 and mass sigma at1-rclock. Put

    Dclock=1-2xiM*rclock+2xiM*rclock^2.

Direct use of the base moments f,k0,H in (6.4) gives the exact interior
metric correction

    Eint=(xiM*rclock/Dclock)
      [theta(1-a*dclock)(1-2rclock)
                               -2a Xreq*rclock*dclock].        (6.6)

This is an auxiliary formula; the positive tail weights are restored
below. One has 1-2p<Dclock<=1 and
 |1/Dclock-1|<rclock. The bracket differs from theta in absolute value
by less than35p, using a*dclock<p, rclock<4p, theta<3 andXreq*dclock<2.
Thus

    |Eint-xiM*hgap*dclock*(2+ellload)|<106p^2.       (6.7)

Indeed comparison first with xiM*rclock*theta costs <100p^2, and
replacing theta by2+ellload costs <6p^2.

Adding omega*rho changes B,M,M2,B0 by at most omega. Both old and
new H are >=1/2, and the old f,k0 are <=1. Direct quotient subtraction
bounds the changes of f,k0,H by2omega,4omega,5omega respectively;
hence each ratio f/H,k0/H changes by at most32omega. The exact metric
part of G0 is

    a(Xreq/Delta)(2f/H-k0/H)
                       +theta(1-a/Delta)f/H-btheta.

Using a Xreq/Delta<5/2 and theta<3 bounds its change by400omega.
Each base is rescaled by its OWN variance and the SAME Xreq,theta;
there is no neglected change of scale. Combining (6.5)-(6.7),

    |G0-dclock*Cfirst|<526p^2,
    Cfirst=a(2a-3)+xiM*hgap*(2+ellload).

Substitution of xiM gives

    Cfirst=a(1-2a)-a^3/2+2ellload*xiM
                                      -eps*(2+ellload)*xiM
            >a^3/2-3eps/4>1/32.                   (6.8)

The last inequality follows from a>2/5, eps<=1/6144:
4/125-3/24576>1/32. Also |Cfirst|<3. Since p<2^(-40),

    p/64<G0<7p,
    P0-bS0=G0+(Delta-b)S0<7p-1/32<0.               (6.9)

Equations (6.3),(6.9) prove the entire screen (6.2).

### 6.2 Last-row clock, including the nonzero incoming-load cost

The last row of Ltri^T is e_l^T, so

    N_ll=P_ll,
    (Nt)_l=2(Pt)_l-2alpha_l-nu_l.

For the last row define

    Slast=sum_(j<l)N_lj=mu_l-P_ll,
    Jlast=sum_(j<l)(t_j-1)N_lj=(Nt)_l-mu_l-Delta*P_ll,
    Hlast=sum_(j<l)(t_j-t_r)N_lj
                  =(Nt)_l-t_r*mu_l-hgap*P_ll.

Nonpositive off-diagonals require Slast<=0, Jlast<=0 andHlast>=0.
Define actual base ratios

    Alast=what_l(1-M/B)/H,
    Blast=what_l(1-what_l/B)/H.

Then (Kt)_l=(Xreq/Delta)Alast, K_ll=(Xreq/Delta^2)Blast,
zeta_l=(theta/Delta)Alast. Both ratios are positive and <3.
For the auxiliary base without omega*rho,

    Alast=(1-xiM+xiM*rclock)/Dclock>=1-xiM.

Restoring omega*rho changes this ratio by at most32omega: its numerator
changes by at most3omega, H by5omega, H>=1/2 and the old numerator<=1.
Thus the actual Alast>=1-xiM-32p^2.

Using V=2, alpha_l=b-v_l, nu_l=0 and z_l=-E/Delta gives the EXACT
last-row invariant

    Hlast=Cclock*Alast-hgap*(Xreq/Delta^2)*Blast
       +4v_l+2p(b+hgap)v_l-hgap*v_l^2-2b-hgap*eta*z_l^2,
    Cclock=[2Xreq-(T-hgap)theta]/Delta.              (6.10)

Since T=Delta+1,
 Cclock=2Xreq/Delta-theta+theta*(hgap-1)/Delta.
By (5.2),

    2-ellload-20p<Cclock<3,
    Cclock*Alast>(2-ellload)(1-xiM)-40p.

The subtracted metric term has magnitude <24p, using Blast<3,
hgap<2, Xreq/Delta<2 and1/Delta<2p. Also
eta*z_l^2=(p^2-s^2)/Delta^2<4p^4.
The function4v-hgap*v^2+2p(b+hgap)v is increasing for a<=v<=v_l<1/2,
since its derivative is >2. Consequently (6.10) implies

    Hlast>2a-2a^2-2xiM-ellload(1-xiM)-65p
          =a^3/4-ellload(1-xiM)-65p
          >3/8000-65p>1/4000.                      (6.11)

Here the positive eps*a^2 term was dropped. The difference
(2/5)^3/4-1/64 is3/8000. The load penalty ellload(1-xiM) is therefore
explicitly paid; it is not hidden in an asymptotic error.

Finally mu_l=2p v_l+zeta_l<19p and P_ll>=v_l^2>a^2>4/25.
Thus Slast<19p-4/25<-1/8. Since (Nt)_l=2(Kt)_l+4v_l-2alpha_l<14,

    Jlast<14-(3/(4p))*(4/25)<0.

Every last-row total/first-moment condition passes. Neither endpoint
screen, even together with all optimum signs and full PSD, guarantees
nonnegative entries in the full array.

## 7. Decisive column failure of the prescribed law

The incoming optimum equation in (4.3) is a COLUMN equation:

    sum_i N_il=nu_l+2alpha_l=2alpha_l.

If each off-diagonal N_il were nonpositive, it would imply
N_ll>=2alpha_l. But N_ll=P_ll for every skew A, not just (5.4).
The metric scale is small:

    scale<=2Xreq/Delta^2<8p,
    K_ll<=w_l=scale*(1+omega*rho_l)<12p.

Together with (3.3) and eta*z_l^2<4p^4, this gives

    P_ll=K_ll+v_l^2+eta*z_l^2<12p+1/4+4p^4<1/3,
    P_ll-2alpha_l<1/3-19/10=-47/30<-3/2.            (7.1)

Thus the putative nonnegative incoming transport masses have negative
total:

    sum_(0<=i<n, i!=l)lambda_(i,l)
      =sum_(0<=i<n, i!=l)(-N_il)=P_ll-2alpha_l<-3/2.

At least one original finite edge INTO the last queried node has a
negative multiplier. This conclusion is invariant under all Ce=Ct=0
corrections and, more generally, under any skew with the same incoming
column sum. The complete prescribed law fails for every m>=1536.
This is not a primal counterexample, an infeasibility proof for all
possible coefficient architectures, or a conclusion about W itself.

## 8. Second mechanism: transport with diagonal exclusion and the full factor

This section treats an entire family, rather than adding another metric
atom. First give the exact finite transport interface for any fixed
P,mu,nu,alpha in the architecture above. Write

    D_i=N_ii, a_ij=-N_ij for i!=j, a_ii=0,
    r_i=D_i-mu_i,
    c_i=D_i-nu_i-2alpha_i,
    b_i^clock=t_i D_i-(Nt)_i.                       (8.1)

Here a_ij denotes transport mass and is unrelated to the physical scalar
a. If the original finite multipliers are nonnegative, then a_ij>=0 and

    sum_j a_ij=r_i, sum_i a_ij=c_j,
    sum_j t_j a_ij=b_i^clock.                       (8.2)

Thus every r_i,c_j is nonnegative, sum r=sum c, and
sum_i b_i^clock=sum_j t_j c_j. A zero r_i forces b_i^clock=0.
For positive r_i the mean b_i^clock/r_i lies in the interval spanned
by {t_j:j!=i}; retaining the exclusion is essential at either endpoint.
With Kmass=sum r=sum c, the forbidden diagonal further implies

    r_i+c_i<=Kmass.                                 (8.3)

Indeed all mass from row i must land outside column i, whose total
capacity is Kmass-c_i. This is a true cut inequality.

There are elementary clock cuts as well. For any real tau,

    sum_i max(0,b_i^clock-tau*r_i)
                              <=sum_j c_j max(0,t_j-tau).       (8.4)

For each row, max(0,sum_j a_ij(t_j-tau))<=sum_j a_ij max(0,t_j-tau);
summing proves (8.4). More generally for any convex function phi on[1,T],
finite convexity gives

    sum_(i:r_i>0) r_i phi(b_i^clock/r_i)
                                      <=sum_j c_j phi(t_j).    (8.5)

This is merely the finite convex-combination inequality, provable by
induction on the number of nonzero row entries, and imports no transport
existence theorem. It is necessary, not sufficient for the original law.
The following more general elementary cut keeps the missing diagonal
visible: if real Ucut_i,Vcut_i,Zcut_j obey
Ucut_i+Vcut_i*t_j+Zcut_j>=0 for EVERY i!=j, then

    sum_i Ucut_i*r_i+sum_i Vcut_i*b_i^clock+sum_j Zcut_j*c_j>=0. (8.6)

Multiply each allowed coefficient by a_ij>=0 and sum. No condition on
Ucut_i+Vcut_i*t_i+Zcut_i is imposed, because that edge is forbidden.

Crucially, the chronological symmetric-factor constraint remains

    Ltri^(-T)N+N^T Ltri^(-1)=2P.                    (8.7)

A transport coupling satisfying row/column masses and clock conditions
alone may violate (8.7). This equality cannot be replaced by PSD of P.
For precision, given D and an array a with zero diagonal, nonnegative
off-diagonals, and the row and column sums in (8.2), define
N=diag(D)-a. If (8.7) holds, then A=Ltri^(-T)N-P is skew.
For the fixed architecture mu=2pR+Ltri^T zeta, one has
Ae=Ltri^(-T)mu-Pe=d. Moreover
(P-A)t=N^T e=nu+2alpha, so At=Pt-2alpha-nu.
Thus this system recovers exactly the required skew and all original
coefficients; the clock equations follow with

    Nt=Ltri^T(2Pt-2alpha-nu).

This is an equivalence of constraint descriptions, NOT an asserted
feasible unknown matrix or a new certificate law. We use it only to
prove the explicit family exclusion below.

### 8.1 Last-column cut and a metric concentration inequality

The last row of Ltri^(-T) is e_l^T. The (l,l) entry of (8.7) therefore
pins D_l=P_ll. The nonnegative column mass c_l gives

    K_ll+eta*z_l^2 >= 2alpha_l+nu_l-(b-alpha_l)^2.   (8.8)

This combines the transport column cut with an exact original-space
factor constraint. Allowing D_l to float freely would lose it.

For ANY strictly positive metric weights w, set X=t^T Kt and

    Qgap=sum_(i<l) w_i (T-t_i)^2 / sum_(i<l)w_i.

The weighted variance identity is

    X=(1/Ww)sum_(i<j)w_i w_j(t_i-t_j)^2.

Retaining only pairs involving l and using
K_ll=w_l(Ww-w_l)/Ww gives the exact necessary estimate

    X>=K_ll*Qgap, so K_ll<=X/Qgap.                  (8.9)

Every denominator is positive since w_i>0 and the clocks are distinct.
For d0>0 define the NONLAST far-clock fraction

    Ffar(d0)=sum_(i<l:T-t_i>=d0)w_i / sum_(i<l)w_i.

Then Qgap>=d0^2*Ffar(d0). If
 Bneed:=2alpha_l+nu_l-(b-alpha_l)^2-eta*z_l^2>0,
(8.8)-(8.9) imply the quantitative concentration requirement

    Ffar(d0)<=X/[d0^2*Bneed].                       (8.10)

This is a global statement about ALL nonlast metric weights; it does
not assume any sparse support or any special skew completion.

### 8.2 A full family excluded, including arbitrary sparse metric atoms

Fix any same-enclosing-m word as in Section2. Consider ALL choices with

 (i) alpha_i>=0, sum alpha_i=1-p, alpha_l>=1/2;
 (ii) v=k-alpha, z=E(T e_0-e_l)/Delta, eta=F/E;
 (iii) arbitrary positive w with X=t^T Kt<=3/p;
 (iv) Ffar(Delta/2)>=256p;
 (v) any skew A and any nonnegative incoming optimum vector nu
     satisfying the original equations (4.2)-(4.3).

There is no restriction to the literal skew (5.4), to nu=ellload e_0,
or to the paid minimum-energy zeta. If the divergence and PSD happen
to pass, they still cannot repair the following finite signs.

For alpha_l in[1/2,1-p], the function
2alpha_l-(b-alpha_l)^2 is strictly increasing, because its derivative
is2+2b-2alpha_l>0. Using b<17/12 gives

    2alpha_l-(b-alpha_l)^2
                >=1-(b-1/2)^2>23/144.              (8.11)

Also eta*z_l^2=(p^2-s^2)/Delta^2<4p^4<1/144.
On the other hand, (8.9) and condition(iv) yield

    Qgap>=64p*Delta^2,
    K_ll <=(3/p)/(64p*Delta^2)<1/12,                (8.12)

where Delta>3/(4p) was used. Therefore

    c_l=P_ll-nu_l-2alpha_l
       <1/12+1/144-23/144=-5/72<0.                 (8.13)

This contradicts a nonnegative column mass. Every member of the entire
precisely specified family has some negative original multiplier into
l. No existence theorem, unknown optimizer, asymptotic coefficient law,
or numerical evaluation enters this exclusion.

As a concrete sparse-metric subfamily, take ANY finite subset
Jsubset of {1,...,l-1}, arbitrary coefficients Mlast>0 and b_j>=0,
and omega0>0, with

    what=e_0+Mlast*e_l+sum_(j in Jsubset)b_j e_j+omega0*rho,
    1+sum_j b_j+omega0 <= 1/(256p).

Scale what by ANY positive scalar for which t^T Kt<=3/p. The positive
regularizer retains every coordinate. The initial atom is far from T,
and its fraction of the nonlast base weight is at least
1/(1+sum_j b_j+omega0)>=256p. Hence every such metric lies in the
excluded family whenever the terminal mass is >=1/2 and the common
column is as above. The number and positions of interior atoms and the
last weight are unrestricted. The prescribed law of Section5 belongs
to this family: alpha_l>19/20, Xreq<2/p, Mlast=1, b_r=sigma<1/3,
omega0=p^2, and1+sigma+p^2<3/2<1/(256p).

Equivalently, even without the coarse256p threshold, (8.10)-(8.11)
show that a putative law in this architecture with alpha_l>=1/2 and
X<=3/p must have Ffar(Delta/2)<140p. Indeed Bneed>11/72 and
Delta>3/(4p) give4X/(Delta^2 Bneed)<(1536/11)p<140p.
This records the quantitative missing interface rather than prescribing
another unsupported atom. It does not exclude terminal laws with less
mass at l, metrics with sufficiently little far-clock mass, another
common column, or architectures outside (4.2).

## 9. Author audit, resources, and handoff

The audit retains all original queried gradients, all prefix rows, the
actual free terminal gradient, the terminal square, the independent
common square, its derivative, and the global initial charge. The
terminal and metric mixtures have separate names xiT andxiM. The
positive omega*rho weights are never dropped from the actual law;
auxiliary endpoint formulas include an explicit restoration error.
The incoming load penalty in the last-row clock is ellload(1-xiM),
which is necessary for the stated finite margin. Both endpoint screens
pass, but the column cut fails; no sufficiency is inferred from moments.
The second mechanism retains the original factor equality (8.7), and
its exclusion covers all skews in the stated family, not just one
Euclidean completion.

Recall used TASK.md and assignment guidance d7e1059c3cc31a14, relevant
shared findings/dead ends, own prior report762b936882b2b6bb, and the two
accepted interfaces. The later awareness-only guidance235e485e10077887
explicitly leaves active worker tasks unchanged; its separate structural
candidate is not used as a premise or explored as a third mechanism.
Fact search found no accepted result replacing the
new column/transport claims. The query-memory, direct-proving and
search-math-results procedures were used. The Matlas query on finite
transport returned unrelated VRJP/Yuan/log-sum statements; none was
imported. Web background identified standard martingale transport
literature, but no external theorem is used. The chosen mechanism
reduces entirely to finite sums, the variance identity, and the already
audited chronological coefficient regime. No new probabilistic machinery
or extra paper premise is needed; (8.4)-(8.10) are proved explicitly.

This is the ONE capped invocation86 with TWO mechanisms. Mathematical
programs, numerical/arithmetic/trajectory/recurrence replay, solvers,
optimizers, benchmarkDP, formal calls, fact_submit, installations,
consults and subagents: all ZERO. Retrieval and administrative text

The full bound W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D) and original minimax target remain

A subsequent coefficient law must check column masses (especially
P_ll>=nu_l+2alpha_l) BEFORE relying on row moments. No third mechanism

# max4 round64: inherited-parameter secondary-dual pattern report

**Completed numerical discovery; no new fact; original minimax OPEN.** All six
authorized jobs completed: calibration, four full secondary-dual solves, and
one independently coded H4 readback. All five solver statuses were `optimal`.
The canonical ledger is `closed_exhausted`:180 accounted seconds,
41.86519651800336 measured external child seconds, five solver invocations,
zero remaining slots. No correction, failed job, timeout, retry, or fallback.

The useful pattern is asymmetric. The right child's finite multipliers nearly
reproduce the previous inherited child's finite multipliers, while its optimum
attachments have a different scale, near0.6316. The left child changes
substantially, with large backward coefficients near dyadic endpoints. At H6
there are also small, clearly resolved forward cross-block coefficients.
These observations suggest a richer recursive law; they do not establish one.

## Exact object and frozen objective

All four words use the SAME enclosing M=6 and tau=1/4:

    lambda_s=1+sqrt(2), beta_word=23/24,
    c=(25/24)lambda_s, a=(23/24)/c,
    H0=(), H(j+1)=(Hj,1+a*c^j,Hj), j=0,...,5.

The discovery words are the actual children H3,H4,H5,H6 of that definition.
Their smaller-depth parameters are not recomputed with enclosing3,4,5.
All physical steps and every gradient coordinate, including the terminal
gradient, are retained. The terminal gradient supplies no additional update.

Fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the original W=2R normalization and interpolation
convention. Normalize L=1 and x_star=g_star=f_star=0. For independent formal
vectors(x0,g0,...,gn), use x_i=x0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t and ALL distinct ordered rows

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Both optimum directions and both terminal directions are included. If
Q=F f+A vec(G), the numerical model is

    minimize J=sum w_ij*lambda_ij,
    lambda_ij>=0, F^T lambda=2e_n,
    C=q_target E00+reshape(A^T lambda) PSD,
    q_target=101/[100(1+2sum h)].

Thus the full intended identity is
q_target||x0||^2-2f_n+sum lambda_ij Q_ij=<C,G>, with every value coefficient
cancelled. This level was a feasibility target, not an assumed upper bound.
There is no rank, support, product-kernel, or incoming-terminal restriction.

The exact weights were published BEFORE job1 in plan9be7ee41a1245d03 and
`preregistration.md`. Let N=n+1=2^j. For distinct finite labels i,k, let
ell=bit_length(i XOR k), the depth of their smallest aligned dyadic block.
Use w_ik=2^(ell-1)/N and w_(star,i)=w_(i,star)=1. Terminal n=N-1 uses the
same finite rule in both directions. This ONE linear functional is unchanged
in all cases; there is no second optimization or adaptive regularization.

Conditioning uses K_cond=1+sum h, a scalar distinct from M=6, and
D_cond=diag(1,1/K_cond,...,1/K_cond). The solver constrains
C_scaled=K_cond D_cond C D_cond PSD. Multipliers and value flow are in original
units throughout. Original and scaled maps/matrices and the inverse congruence
are saved. The independent H4 readback checks the inversion explicitly.

## Calibration and feasibility defects

For h=(3/2), the preregistered nonzero reference multipliers are
lambda_(0,star)=lambda_(1,star)=lambda_(1,0)=1. At q=101/400,

 q||x0||^2-2f1+Q_(0,star)+Q_(1,star)+Q_(1,0)
   =||x0/2-g0-g1||^2+||x0||^2/400.

The exact reference objective is5/2. The charged model evaluation had zero
matrix-entry and flow discrepancy against this reference. The calibration
solver returned J=2.499999943615519 with secondary gap1.645353719936793e-8.
Every preregistered calibration check passed. The raw calibration still has
minimum multiplier-1.2612436727442586e-8 and minimum original eigenvalue
-9.956744939364884e-9. Passing numerical tolerances is not exact feasibility.

The following values are copied from the saved in-job summaries. J is the
secondary objective, NOT the original PEP objective2f_n.

| H3 | 7 | 0.03782489677601333 | 2.853465898662487 | 3.2180014208904595e-9 | 16 |
| H4 | 15 | 0.015182158499335989 | 3.107557434785927 | 1.241881131264222e-8 | 20 |
| H5 | 31 | 0.006138546371301919 | 3.5082763702999706 | 3.408905424606701e-8 | 26 |
| H6 | 63 | 0.0024832359777307244 | 4.175331273969408 | 3.1075637174637905e-8 | 38 |

| Child | Min original multiplier | Min eigenvalue of original C | Max absolute flow defect | Max secondary stationarity defect |

There are20,82,362,1381 negative raw multipliers respectively; all are retained.
No eigenvalue or multiplier was clipped. The nonnegativity constraints are in
the model, but none of these floating-point outputs is an exact sign/PSD
certificate. Full spectra and residual arrays are preserved, not only minima.

For the secondary conic dual, let y be the flow equality multiplier, s the
nonnegativity dual, and Z_original=K_cond D_cond Z_scaled D_cond. Its saved
stationarity is w+F y-A vec(Z_original)-s=0; its objective is
-2y_n-q_target Z_original[0,0]. These are the actual primal/dual diagnostics
for J. The nonzero right side w means this dual is not automatically an
original interpolation-feasible transcript or a legal adversarial function.

## Different hierarchy coefficients and the suggested relation

The charged diagnostics compared each parent's two induced children against
the previous SAVED inherited child. The left child includes finite sample
labels0,...,r, and the right child r+1,...,n, where r=(n-1)/2 is the physical
root bridge index. Each comparison also retains both optimum directions.
The scalar fit is a descriptive closed-form least-squares calculation, not
another solver call. The relative residual is normalized by the parent vector.

| Parent / child | Right finite factor | Right finite residual | Right optimum factor | Right optimum residual | Left finite residual |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| H4 / H3 | 1.0007690219603347 | 0.007239491890668989 | 0.6315969265208133 | 0.018536547699762426 | 0.6446437457246462 |
| H5 / H4 | 1.00030299279191 | 0.018844299469799174 | 0.6318115947637603 | 0.021986792708540426 | 0.7859783772146531 |
| H6 / H5 | 1.0002894706516186 | 0.0054076217631775685 | 0.6314755899700785 | 0.024142689287816408 | 0.8786389603659166 |

The right finite and optimum coefficients therefore need separate treatment.
A single scalar copy of a full child does not describe these optimizers.
Even separate scalars do not give exact identities: the displayed residuals
are much larger than numerical stationarity defects. The left finite block
is particularly far from a scalar copy.

The H6 root row illustrates real coefficient differences. All entries below
are original multipliers, with r=31 and the physical h values retained:

| Destination k | Physical level nu_2(k+1) | lambda_(31,k) | lambda_(31,k)/(h_31 h_k) |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| 30 | 0 | 7.416348190399652 | 0.13653774941895674 |
| 29 | 1 | 1.311857903575235 | 0.017032583903911765 |
| 27 | 2 | 0.24672903616397593 | 0.0018396847590614928 |
| 23 | 3 | 0.0476281112112982 | 0.0001715116516773268 |
| 0 | 0 | 1.0386633518121214e-8 | 1.9122181539960158e-10 |

Destinations30 and0 have the same physical level but very different weights.
Position within the hierarchy matters as well as step level. The earlier root
rows show the same concentration near successive dyadic endpoints: at H3,
lambda_(3,2)=0.45711281504070095 and lambda_(3,1)=0.09836338877232588; at H4,
lambda_(7,6)=1.1297980965705121, lambda_(7,5)=0.23467954457630183 and
lambda_(7,3)=0.003942262395092079; at H5, lambda_(15,14)=2.8653798695720165,
lambda_(15,13)=0.5449511942690242 and lambda_(15,11)=0.09483804561591222.
This is descriptive evidence for level- and position-dependent corrections,
not an exact support law. All smaller coefficients remain in the raw data.

Within the top dyadic split of finite samples, the backward cross-block sums
are0.7435105990164873,0.7283108670456813,0.7243282976299297,
0.7252981469443092 for H3 through H6. The forward blocks of H3,H4,H5 have
no entries above1e-7. H6 differs: its forward block has six entries above1e-7,
sum0.0021584484010527287 and maximum0.0015462200468230186. Thus deleting
all forward cross-block coefficients would change the selected H6 result
well beyond its residual scale. This is not an impossibility result for some
other certificate with different support.

The full saved singular spectra also argue against declaring a universal
one-channel correction. H5's backward cross-block leading singular values
are0.22003430918415515 and1.0969626725889478e-7, whereas H6's are
0.1829102833539127 and0.001228526081829379. The H6 forward block has leading
singular values0.0016618026040199432 and3.3281783246296826e-8. These are
threshold-dependent numerical observations; no exact rank is asserted.

The useful next analytical hypothesis is an asymmetric parent construction:
retain most of the right finite block, transport its optimum attachments
separately, and add position-dependent left and cross-block corrections.
The observed near-copy relation, optimum scale, and dyadic endpoint pattern
are clues to derive from the full identity, not premises. The data contradict
a faithful description by one scalar child copy or the previous two-parameter
product kernel; they neither exclude other duals nor prove a recursion.

Incoming terminal sums are1.2896450517851894e-9,7.309053742620733e-9,
3.061772810715e-8,4.16240364021452e-8. These rows were all present in the
model and saved without deletion. Their small numerical values are not exact
zeros. Support counts above1e-7 are28,72,162,416; at1e-9 they are37,153,553,1747.
The threshold sensitivity is another reason to retain the complete arrays.

## Independent original-cone readback

Slot6 imported no solver or solve module. It reconstructed every H4 Q matrix

This independently checks model/convention/export agreement. It does not

# Max41: explicit variable-step continuation and paid mixed blocks

Status: COMPLETE JOINT PROOF DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No new assertion in this
manuscript is a fact-graph node. This is the second and final analytical
round of guidance78e668c9782fc528: completed baseline39, completed
ceiling41, attempted ceiling42 including the historical interruption.
Completion of this running session is recorded only after return. The
wave authorizes no numerical, solver, machine-algebra, replay, formal
verifier, installation, consult, or subagent work.

The original goal remains the all-nonnegative-word, all-sufficiently-
large-horizon lower profile W_n(h)>=(1-o(1))/B_n, in the normalization
W=2R. It remains OPEN. The quantifiers of the families below are stated
explicitly and do not replace that goal.

Accepted inputs, read in the fact graph:

- 62a7d9329121fd75: the finite triangular jets on(2,2,1), with its three
  stated finite reference comparisons only.
- 1252b8823bb5a290: the ordered smooth-convex interpolation inequalities,
  the explicit conjugate construction, and scaling.
- 1fca3e5dd555cb4a: the complete alternating-ray reference F_ray_n.
- dfde2fa326c908bf: the full schedule-dependent complex-polynomial
  reference D_h, Gap_h and its terminal upper bound.
- b98bb807d514e80d: K_n, the original benchmark recurrences and their
  polynomial upper bound B_n<=1+4n^p, p=log_2(3).

The previous joint8d13a825327585c0 is a retrieval cursor, not a premise.
Minimum rigidity, the unrestricted step2 identity, the variable-step
construction, all interpolation rows, true radii, and every new-word
comparison needed here are proved together below.

## 1. Notation and retained data

All spaces are finite-dimensional real Euclidean spaces. Let E be a
two-dimensional plane, and use the local abbreviations

    p=(1,2sqrt(3)), q=(-2,-sqrt(3)), e=(1,0),
    nu=(1,-sqrt(3)).

Here p,q,e are accepted p_ft,q_ft,e_ft. The letter p in this local
vector notation is unrelated to the benchmark exponent log_2(3).
Vectors in E are embedded isometrically when the dimension increases.
The four retained ABSOLUTE jets are

    position     gradient     value
    p            p            13/2
    -p           q             9/2
    3e           e             5/2
    2e           e             3/2.                         (1)

They obey p-2p=-p, -p-2q=3e, and3e-e=2e. Their ordered interpolation
rows and an actual static function are supplied by62a7d9329121fd75.

For any jet(x_i,g_i,f_i), define its residual and conjugate height by

    y_i=x_i-g_i,
    beta_i=<x_i,g_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2.

The signed ordered interpolation expression is

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =beta_j-beta_i+<y_j,g_i-g_j>.                    (2)

For actual convex1-smooth data, Q_ij<=0 in BOTH orientations, by
1252b8823bb5a290. The old heights are0; their residuals, in order(1),
are0,nu,2e,e. A subgradient y of a convex function psi at g means
psi(w)>=psi(g)+<y,w-g> for every w. The notation conv denotes finite
convex combinations. The positive part a_+ means max(a,0).

Every smooth convex interpolant of(1) has minimum value0 at the origin.
Indeed, with v=grad F(0), interpolation from each old gradient
b in{p,q,e} gives F(0)>=||v||^2/2-<b,v>. The positive relation
p/6+q/3+e/2=0 implies F(0)>=||v||^2/2. Smooth descent from the jet
at p gives F(0)<=13/2-13+13/2=0. Thus F(0)=0 and v=0.
For ANY minimizer m, the old/minimum rows give
<p,m>,<q,m>,<e,m><=0. The same positive relation makes all three
products zero. Since p,e span E,

    argmin F is a nonempty closed convex subset of E-perpendicular. (3)

Consequently minimum normalization and movement of the closest minimizer
cannot be chosen independently of the old jets.

## 2. The sharp unrestricted step2 predecessor, retained in this joint

Let F range over all convex1-smooth interpolants of(1), in every finite
dimension. Add any actual jet with gradient z at X=p+2z, and let m be
a closest minimizer to X. Write z=v+t, with v in E and t perpendicular
to E, so m is perpendicular to E by(3). Let beta be the new height.

The COMPLETE new/old rows say

    beta>=0, beta>=<nu,z>-1,
    beta>=2(<e,z>-1), beta>=<e,z>-1,
    beta<=<p+z,z-b> for b=p,q,e.                         (4)

The two distinct old points with gradient e give the two displayed lower
rows. The new/minimum row says beta>=<m,t>. Four nonnegative slacks are

    A_p=||z||^2-13-beta,
    A_q=||z||^2+<p-q,v>+8-beta,
    B_nu=beta-<nu,v>+1,
    C_min=beta-<m,t>.

Direct expansion gives the all-dimensional identity

    ||p+2z-m||^2
       =17+||v+p||^2+||m-t||^2
           +2A_p+A_q+B_nu+2C_min.                       (5)

In detail the slack sum is3||z||^2+2<p,v>-17-2<m,t>, because
p-q-nu=2p; adding the squared terms in(5) gives exactly the left side.
Thus every such predecessor has true squared radius at least17.
Equality forces v=-p, m=t, and, from B_nu=C_min=0,

    ||t||^2=beta=4, z=-p+t, X=-p+2t, F(X)=17/2.         (6)

Section3 below with k=1 and c_1=2 constructs this equality case,
including all rows and its exact minimum segment. Hence the unrestricted
infimum is attained and is exactly17, and the best retained-jet payoff
is3/17. Every optimizer has a transverse gradient of norm2 and a
closest minimizer different from0. Neither an old gradient wedge nor
a fixed minimizer nor any rank assumption was imposed in this lower
bound. This is not a formula for unrestricted W_4(2,2,2,1).

## 3. An explicit continuation for every sequence of steps above1

Fix k>=0 and arbitrary real steps c_1,...,c_k>1. These are indexed
BACKWARD: c_1 is immediately before the old word(2,2,1), while the
actual full chronological word is

    (c_k,c_(k-1),...,c_1,2,2,1).                        (7)

Define the positive products and nonnegative height numerators

    t_i=c_i-1, P_0=1, P_i=product_(l=1)^i t_l,
    H_(-1)=H_0=0,
    H_i=max{H_(i-2),(5-P_i)_+}          if i is odd,
    H_i=max{H_(i-2),(2-2P_i)_+}         if i is even.    (8)

The P_i in(8) are products, not the prefix sums of the accepted K_n
formula. The H_i are scalar height numerators, not objective functions.
Choose pairwise orthogonal vectors a_i perpendicular to E with

    ||a_i||^2=(H_(i-1)+H_i)/P_(i-1),
    M_0=0, M_i=sum_(l=1)^i a_l.                        (9)

A zero right side permits a_i=0. Work in E+span{a_1,...,a_k}; its
dimension is at most k+2. Define ONE static function for this full word:

    psi_k(w)=max{0,<nu,w>-1,2<e,w>-2,
                      <M_1,w>,...,<M_k,w>},
    Phi_k(w)=||w||^2/2+psi_k(w),
    F_k(x)=sup_w{<x,w>-Phi_k(w)}.                       (10)

Its backward-indexed queried gradients, positions and proposed values are

    g_0=p, g_i=(-g_(i-1)+a_i)/t_i,
    X_i=M_i+g_i,
    beta_i=H_i/P_i,
    D_i=||g_i||^2,
    f_i=D_i/2.                                         (11)

The actual initial point is X_k, not X_0. The physical trajectory is
X_k,...,X_0=p,-p,3e,2e. The following exact radius is the paid output:

    D_k=[13+sum_(i=1)^k P_(i-1)(H_(i-1)+H_i)]/P_k^2,
    dist(X_k,argmin F_k)^2=D_k,
    normalized terminal payoff V=3/D_k.                (12)

This is a finite explicit recurrence and a displayed static objective;
it is not a call to an unknown feasible-set oracle. The function can
depend on the complete supplied word. Its extension preserves every
earlier queried point, gradient, absolute value and affine plane in the
natural isometric embedding.

### 3.1 Smoothness and the exact minimum set

Since psi_k is a finite maximum of affine functions and includes0,
Phi_k is finite, continuous,1-strongly convex and at least||w||^2/2.
For each x the conjugate maximum has a unique maximizer g(x). Optimality
says x is a subgradient of Phi_k at g(x). Adding the two strong
subgradient inequalities at x,x' gives

    <x-x',g(x)-g(x')>>=||g(x)-g(x')||^2.

Thus g is1-Lipschitz. The maximizing inequalities also give

    0<=F_k(x')-F_k(x)-<g(x),x'-x>
       <=<g(x')-g(x),x'-x><=||x'-x||^2.

It follows that F_k is finite, convex, differentiable and has gradient
g. In particular it is globally1-smooth. If a plane of psi_k with slope
y is active at g, then x=g+y has gradient g and value

    F_k(x)=<x,g>-||g||^2/2-psi_k(g).                    (13)

The exact minimum value and set are

    min F_k=0, argmin F_k=K_k:=conv{M_0,...,M_k}.        (14)

For x in K_k, <x,w><=max_j<M_j,w><=psi_k(w), so the conjugate is0,
attained at w=0. Conversely, for x outside this compact convex set,
let m_0 be its closest point and d=x-m_0. The projection inequality
implies <d,M_j><=<d,m_0> and
<x,d>><d,m_0>>=max_j<M_j,d>. At w=epsilon d for sufficiently small
epsilon>0, the planes with intercepts-1,-2 are inactive. Hence

    <x,epsilon d>-Phi_k(epsilon d)
      =epsilon[<x,d>-max_j<M_j,d>]-epsilon^2||d||^2/2>0.

Thus F_k(x)>0, proving(14). The projection inequality used here follows
by differentiating squared distance along each segment in K_k from
m_0; expanding the square proves its sufficiency as well.

### 3.2 All planes, all ordered rows, and the physical updates

The planar component of g_i is s_i p, where s_i=(-1)^i/P_i. We prove
the following complete support extrema among the minimum planes:

    max_(0<=j<=i)<M_j,g_i>=<M_i,g_i>=H_i/P_i,
    min_(0<=j<=i)<M_j,g_i>=-H_(i-1)/P_i.               (15)

At i=0 both are0. For j<i, orthogonality in(9) and(11) gives
<M_j,g_i>=-<M_j,g_(i-1)>/t_i. Thus the old maximum becomes the new
minimum -H_(i-1)/P_i, and the old minimum becomes H_(i-2)/P_i.
The new plane at its proposed gradient equals

    <M_i,g_i>
       =[||a_i||^2-<M_(i-1),g_(i-1)>]/t_i
       =H_i/P_i.

This is at least H_(i-2)/P_i and at least0 by(8), proving(15).
For j>i, <M_j,g_i>=<M_i,g_i> because future increments are orthogonal
to all previous coordinates. Thus(15) also bounds EVERY future plane.

The remaining fixed planes have values -5s_i-1 and2s_i-2 at g_i.
When i is odd, their maximum together with0 is(5/P_i-1)_+;
when i is even it is(2/P_i-2)_+. Equation(8) therefore proves

    psi_k(g_i)=beta_i=<M_i,g_i>.                        (16)

The M_i plane is active, so(13) proves all new jets(11). At p,q,e,
every M plane vanishes. The zero plane is active at p, the nu plane
at q, and the zero and2e planes at e. These give residuals0,nu,2e,e
and all four absolute old values(1).

For completeness, formula(2) gives every ordered new/new row as

    Q_ij=<M_j,g_i>-beta_i<=0.                          (17)

For a new i and the four old jets, in order(1), its four rows are

    -beta_i, -5s_i-1-beta_i,
    2s_i-2-beta_i, s_i-1-beta_i.                        (18)

They are nonpositive by(8),(16); the fourth follows from the maximum
of0 and2s_i-2. Each reversed old/new row is0 because every M_i is
perpendicular to E and <M_i,g_i>=beta_i. Old/old rows are exactly those
of62a7d9329121fd75. For every m in K_k the new/minimum row is
<m,g_i>-beta_i<=0, while the reverse row is0. For the four old jets
their rows to any m are0; the reverse rows are0,-1,-2,-1.
Thus no old, new, terminal or minimum row in either orientation is lost.

The actual recursion is an identity:

    X_i-c_i g_i=M_i-t_i g_i
       =M_(i-1)+g_(i-1)=X_(i-1).                      (19)

In particular no unproved inverse-map assertion is used.

Since M_i maximizes <m,g_i> on K_k, for all m in K_k,
<g_i,m-M_i><=0. Expanding ||M_i+g_i-m||^2 shows its minimum is
||g_i||^2, attained at M_i. This proves the TRUE radius assertion in(12),
also for earlier queried points after all future planes have been added.
Finally orthogonality gives

    D_i=[D_(i-1)+(H_(i-1)+H_i)/P_(i-1)]/t_i^2.

Multiplication by P_i^2 and summation, starting from D_0=13, proves(12).
The case k=1,t_1=1 gives ||a_1||^2=4, K_1=[0,a_1], D_1=17,
and exactly the equality data(6), completing Section2's attainment.

### 3.3 Exact optimality within the declared orthogonal policy

The family restriction in this subsection is explicit. Retain(1), use
the SAME form(10), choose arbitrary fresh orthogonal increments a_i,
require residual M_i at each new gradient, use the physical steps c_i,
and retain every intermediate jet. A member is admissible only if all
these are actual jets of its static function. No unrestricted interpolant
or arbitrary nonorthogonal choice is included in this family assertion.

For an admissible member write its height numerator as J_i=P_i beta_i,
with J_(-1)=J_0=0. Its residual M_i is a subgradient of psi_k at g_i.
The subgradient inequality evaluated at0, where psi_k(0)=0, gives
psi_k(g_i)<=<M_i,g_i>. The reverse inequality holds because the
M_i plane is one of the defining planes. Thus that plane must be
active. The support-extrema argument proving(15) applies and forces

    J_i>=J_(i-2),
    J_i>=(5-P_i)_+ for odd i,
    J_i>=(2-2P_i)_+ for even i,
    ||a_i||^2=(J_(i-1)+J_i)/P_(i-1).                   (20)

Conversely these inequalities make the same proof of(15)-(19) work.
They therefore characterize this declared family exactly, including
all cross-history rows. Induction gives J_i>=H_i from(8), coordinate
by coordinate. Its squared radius is exactly the expression(12) with
H replaced by J, all of whose coefficients are nonnegative. Hence(8)
attains the MINIMUM true radius over the entire declared policy, even
if larger earlier increments are chosen with knowledge of the full
word. No hidden greedy-optimality or continuity assumption is required.

## 4. A variable large-step family with linear paid radius

Fix an integer m>=1 and t in[3/4,1]. Put

    a=1+1/t, b=1+t, A=a+b=2+t+1/t,
    C(t)=(1+t)(5-t)=5+4t-t^2,
    h^(m,t)=((a,b) repeated m times,2,2,1).             (21)

The physical horizon is exactly n=2m+3. For t<1, each pair has one
step above2 and one strictly between1 and2. These are actual steps,
not altered enclosing parameters.

Use k=2m and the backward t_i alternately t,1/t. Then P_i=t for odd
i and1 for even i, H_i=5-t for odd i and0 for even i. Thus each odd
increment has squared norm5-t and each even one(5-t)/t. All are
positive. Formula(12) becomes

    D_(2m)=13+m C(t),
    V_(m,t)=3/[13+m C(t)].                             (22)

The gradients change at every new query because a new nonzero orthogonal
coordinate is added. Equations(8)-(19) specify its full static function,
absolute jets, heights, minimum polytope and all rows. In particular(22)
is an attained true-distance payoff, not just a feasible Gram score.

## 5. Complete SAME-word alternating-ray comparisons

We use the COMPLETE channel in1fca3e5dd555cb4a, with its specified
initial point1 and minimizer0. For a checkpoint decomposition of a word,
write the ordinary block sums as s_j and selected steps as b_j. With
ell>=1 checkpoints, its magnitudes and remote offsets obey

    m_0=1/(1+s_0), O_0=0,
    m_(j+1)=[(b_j-1)m_j-O_j]/(1+s_(j+1)),
    O_(j+1)=O_j+s_j m_j.

They must satisfy m_j>0 and m_j>=m_(j-2) where applicable. At the
last checkpoint put z_term=(b_(ell-1)-1)m_(ell-1)-O_(ell-1), and
m_old=m_(ell-2) if ell>=2, otherwise0. Feasibility includes

    z_term>=(1+s_ell)m_old.                             (23)

The payoff is the maximum of
2 e_term+2q z_term-(2s_ell+1)q^2 over the accepted interval
[m_old,z_term/(1+s_ell)], where e_term is the sum of s_j m_j^2
over0<=j<ell with j congruent to ell modulo2. No value of this
accumulated area can cure an empty interval(23). Empty checkpoints give1/(1+2S), where
S is total mass. A singleton step h_j>=1 gives exactly

    (h_j-1)^2/[(1+Psum_j)^2(1+2Tsum_j)],               (24)

where Psum_j and Tsum_j are the sums before and after that step.
Singleton steps below1 are infeasible; the zero-payoff step1 is allowed.
F_ray is the maximum over ALL feasible choices. K is precisely the
maximum of the empty and singleton values with positive parts.

For(21), a selected final step1 makes any multi-checkpoint set infeasible:
z_term=-O<=0 while m_old>0. Otherwise the unselected terminal mass is
at least1. Every selected step minus1 is at most4/3; all offsets are
nonnegative. Thus, for ANY multi-checkpoint set,

    z_term<=(4/3)m_(ell-1)<=(16/9)m_old
             <2m_old<=(1+s_ell)m_old.

This excludes all such sets by the full terminal constraint.

Here the first step a>=2 is also the largest physical step. A useful
explicit denominator comparison eliminates every later singleton. For
any step h with preceding sum P and following sum T, let S=P+h+T and
C0=1+2(S-a). Direct expansion gives

    (1+P)^2(1+2T)-C0
       =P[(P+2)(1+2T)-2]+2(a-h)>=0.                   (25)

Its numerator(h-1)_+^2 is at most(a-1)^2. The empty value is smaller
than the first cut because(a-1)^2>=1 and C0<1+2S. Consequently the
COMPLETE same-word references are exactly

    F_ray_(2m+3)(h^(m,t))=K_(2m+3)(h^(m,t))
       =1/[t^2(2m A+11-2a)].                          (26)

The new payoff exceeds(26) for every stated m,t. Clearing the positive
denominators reduces the strict comparison to

    m(6t^3+13t^2+2t-5)+(27t^2-6t-13)>0.               (27)

Both parenthesized polynomials increase on[3/4,1]. At3/4 the first
is203/32 and the second is-37/16. Thus the left side is at least
129/32 for m>=1. The exact ratio and its limit are

    V_(m,t)/F_ray=3t^2(2m A+11-2a)/[13+m C(t)],
    lim_(m->infinity) V_(m,t)/F_ray
       =6t(t+1)^2/[(1+t)(5-t)]>1.                    (28)

At t=1 this recovers limit3, but(21)-(28) include a continuum of
nonconstant physical words.

## 6. Full accepted complex-polynomial reference: necessary suffix cycles

We keep exactly the accepted reference dfde2fa326c908bf. For a complex
z=alpha+i beta, its points are xi_j=Poly_j(z), where
Poly_j(z)=product_(l<j)(1-h_l z); its gradients are gamma_j=z xi_j,
and xi_*=gamma_*=0. For every directed index-simple cycle define
Area_C=sum Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i) and Energy_C=sum|xi_i-xi_j|^2 over
its directed edges j->i. The FULL legal set D_h requires

    (alpha-|z|^2) Energy_C>=2|beta Area_C|

for every such cycle. The accepted Gap_h(z) is the largest normalized
terminal value for those exact points and gradients, with initial
point1, specified minimizer0 and denominator1. The accepted estimate is

    0<=Gap_h(z)<=(2alpha-|z|^2)|Poly_n(z)|^2.            (29)

No closest-minimum renormalization or universal-lens substitution is
made for this reference.

Every word used below ends in the actual suffix(2,2,1). Put

    w=1-2z=d+i v, sigma=|w|^2, delta=1-sigma.

The optimum/index0 two-cycle forces alpha-|z|^2>=0, equivalently
0<=sigma<=1. If the prefix polynomial at the start of the suffix is0,
the terminal polynomial is0 and(29) gives Gap_h=0. Otherwise the
suffix starts at a nonzero common complex scale. The two actual cycles
with suffix positions(0,1,w) and(1,w,w^2), with0 denoting the optimum,
have areas v and v|1-w|^2 and energies
2(1+sigma-d) and2|1-w|^2(1+sigma+d), up to the SAME positive scale
factor. If w=1, z=0 and the gap is0. In all other cases division gives

    2v^2<=delta(1+sigma-|d|),
    d^2>=(sigma^2+2sigma-1)/2.                         (30)

All these cycles use distinct sample indices even when coordinates
coincide. They are necessary constraints on the full legal set, not
a replacement definition of it.

The contribution of the last(2,2,1) to the terminal bound is exactly

    T(z)=(2alpha-|z|^2)|w^2(1+w)/2|^2
        =sigma^2[4-(2d+sigma-1)^2]/16.                 (31)

Two uniform consequences of(30) will be used:

    T(z)<=4/25,
    T(z)<=(5/4)sigma^2 delta.                          (32)

For the first, if sigma<=4/5, (31)<=sigma^2/4<=4/25. Otherwise(30)
gives d^2>=31/50>9/16. If d>=3/4, then2d+sigma-1>=13/10 and
T<=231/1600<4/25. If d<=-3/4, then2d+sigma-1<=-3/2 and
T<=7/64<4/25. This proves the first bound.

For the second, let the bracket in(31) be J. When delta>=1/5,
J<=4<=20delta. When0<=delta<=1/5, (30) implies
d^2>=1-2delta+delta^2/2>=(1-2delta)^2. If d>=1-2delta,
2d-delta>=2-5delta>=0, so J<=20delta-25delta^2<=20delta.
If d<=-1+2delta, then2d-delta<=-2+3delta<0, so
J<=12delta-9delta^2<=20delta. Substitution in(31) proves(32).

## 7. Full complex comparison for EVERY reciprocal-pair word(21)

For(21), the actual pair polynomial is

    Q(z)=(1-a z)(1-b z)=1-A z+A z^2
        =gamma w^2-(gamma-1),
    gamma=A/4 in[1,49/48].                            (33)

Indeed ab=A, and A is decreasing on[3/4,1] from49/12 to4. The full
terminal bound in(29) is |Q(z)|^(2m) T(z). We bound this over EVERY
z in the full D_h, using(30), including all boundary cases.

If delta>=1/5, sigma<=4/5, and

    |Q|<=gamma sigma+gamma-1<=67/80,
    |Q|^2<3/4.

Thus the gap is at most(4/25)(3/4)^m. For integers m>=1,

    (13+8m)(3/4)^m<=261/16.                            (34)

To verify(34), the values at m=1,2 are252/16 and261/16, respectively.
The successive ratio is3(21+8m)/[4(13+8m)]<=1 for all m>=2,
because11<=8m. Hence

    (4/25)(3/4)^m<3/(13+8m)<=V_(m,t),                 (35)

where C(t)<=8 and(4/25)(261/16)=261/100<3 were used.

If0<=delta<=1/5, the second condition in(30) gives

    |Q|^2<=1-2gamma(3-2gamma)delta
                  +gamma(2-gamma)delta^2
           <=1-(7/4)delta.                            (36)

For an explicit verification of the last coefficient, gamma(2-gamma)
is positive on the stated interval, and

    2gamma(3-2gamma)-gamma(2-gamma)delta
       >=gamma(28-19gamma)/5
       >=20237/11520>7/4.

The middle quadratic decreases on[1,49/48]; its displayed value is
at49/48. Also sigma^2=(1-delta)^2<=1-(7/4)delta for delta<=1/5.
Combining(32),(36), putting x=7delta/4, and maximizing on[0,1] yields

    Gap_h(z)<=(5/4)delta[1-(7/4)delta]^(m+1)
       <=5/[7(m+2)] * ((m+1)/(m+2))^(m+1)
       <=5/[14(m+2)]
       <3/(13+8m)<=V_(m,t).                           (37)

The maximum follows by differentiating x(1-x)^(m+1); the factor at
its maximum is at most1/2 since the first two terms of
(1+1/(m+1))^(m+1) already sum to2. The last strict comparison is
65+40m<84+42m. The case delta=0 gives zero gap by(31),(30).

Equations(35),(37) are uniform in z. In particular

    sup_(z in D_h) Gap_h(z)
      <=max{(4/25)(3/4)^m,5/[14(m+2)]}<V_(m,t).         (38)

This bounds the FULL accepted channel with all its cycles and legal
values. It also bounds every scalar quadratic, since its curvature
theta in[0,1] gives a legal real z=theta and its actual quadratic
value is no larger than Gap_h(theta). An orthogonal sum of convex
quadratics has a normalized payoff which is a nonnegative weighted
average of the scalar payoffs, including zero-curvature components;
therefore that entire quadratic class is dominated as well. No claim
about every nonlinear scalar function is made.

## 8. An actual short/large TWO-step prepend on one static function

There is also a genuinely mixed construction containing a step below1.
Fix u in[1/2,1] and define the EXACT five-step word

    h^(u)=(1-u,1+1/u,2,2,1).                           (39)

Let a be perpendicular to E with||a||=2, set g=-p+a, and use the
single three-dimensional function

    psi(w)=max{0,<nu,w>-1,2<e,w>-2,<a,w>},
    F(x)=sup_w{<x,w>-||w||^2/2-psi(w)}.                 (40)

This is(10) with one minimum increment. Its exact minimum set is[0,a]
and its minimum value is0 by Section3.1. Define two new queried jets

    X=a+g=-p+2a,       gradient g,      value17/2,
    Z=a+u g,          gradient u g,    value17u^2/2.    (41)

At g the a-plane has value4 and is active. At u g it has value4u,
while the nu-plane is5u-1<=4u, the2e plane is-2u-2, and the zero
plane is0. Hence it is active there too, and(13) proves(41).
Both new residuals equal a; their heights are4 and4u. The updates are

    X-(1-u)g=Z,
    Z-(1+1/u)(u g)=a-g=p.                             (42)

The remainder is exactly(1). For u<1, these are genuinely changing
gradients g,u g,p. At u=1 the first step is the actual zero step and
its duplicate jet remains in the physical five-step word.

All ordered rows are explicit. The new/new rows in both directions
are0. For Z to the four old jets they are

    -4u, u-1, -6u-2, -5u-1,

and for X they are these expressions at u=1. All reversed old/new
rows are0. For every minimum lambda a with0<=lambda<=1, the new/minimum
rows are4(lambda-1) and4u(lambda-1), while their reverses are0.
The old/minimum and old/old rows are those already proved in Section3.2.
Thus the inserted short step does not omit its absolute value or any
new/old directed constraint.

The closest minimizer to X is a, since <g,lambda a-a>=4(lambda-1)<=0.
Its TRUE initial squared radius is17, and its actual terminal payoff is

    V_mix=3/17,                                        (43)

for EVERY u in[1/2,1]. The function(40) itself is independent of u;
the intermediate point and its absolute value vary with the word.

### 8.1 Complete same-word ray reference

A step1-u<1 cannot occur in a feasible checkpoint set: it either makes
the next required magnitude negative or makes the final z_term negative.
A final step1 also makes every multi-checkpoint set infeasible; its
singleton value is0. Remaining possible checkpoints are c=1+1/u and
the two old steps2. Their multipliers b_j-1 are at most2.

If the last checkpoint is the first old2, its predecessor can only be c;
z_term<=2m_old, while the unselected tail has mass3 and(23) requires
at least4m_old. If the last checkpoint is the second old2, and its
predecessor is the first old2, then z_term<=m_old<2m_old. If its
predecessor is c instead, the unselected intervening step2 gives
m_(ell-1)<=2m_old/3, again contradicting the required2m_old. The last
checkpoint cannot be c in a multi-checkpoint set because none before
it is admissible. This treats ALL multiple checkpoints.

The empty value is1/(15+2/u-2u)<=1/15. The c singleton has value

    1/[11u^2(2-u)^2]>=1/11.

The two old2 singletons are
1/[7(3+1/u-u)^2]<=1/63 and1/[3(5+1/u-u)^2]<=1/75. Therefore

    F_ray_5(h^(u))=K_5(h^(u))=1/[11u^2(2-u)^2],
    V_mix/F_ray=33u^2(2-u)^2/17>=297/272>1.            (44)

The last bound uses that u(2-u) increases on[1/2,1], with minimum3/4.

### 8.2 The full same-word complex reference

Keep the notation and necessary suffix cycles of Section6. The actual
two-step prefix polynomial in(39) is

    Q_mix(z)=(1-(1-u)z)(1-(1+1/u)z)
       =w+rho(w^2-1), rho=(1/u-u)/4 in[0,3/8].         (45)

If Q_mix=0, the gap is0. Otherwise(30) applies. Direct expansion gives

    |Q_mix|^2=sigma+rho^2(delta^2+4v^2)-2rho d delta.

Use2v^2<=delta(1+sigma-|d|) from(30), then |d|<=1, sigma<=1 and
0<=rho<=3/8. This gives

    |Q_mix|^2
      <=1+delta[-1+rho^2(3+sigma)+2rho(1-rho)|d|]
      <=1+delta[-1+2rho+2rho^2]
      <=1+delta/32<=33/32.                             (46)

The polynomial in rho increases on the stated interval and equals1/32
at3/8. Combining the actual terminal bound |Q_mix|^2 T(z) with(32),

    sup_(z in D_(h^(u))) Gap_(h^(u))(z)
       <=33/200<3/17=V_mix.                            (47)

The strict comparison is561<600 after multiplying by3400. Thus EVERY
word(39), including its zero-step endpoint, beats the complete accepted
ray and full complex references, as well as scalar and orthogonal-sum
quadratic objectives by the argument following(38). This is not a
comparison with all nonlinear scalar trajectories.

## 9. Original benchmark and exact limits of the continuation policy

Use exactly the original benchmark quantities C_n=1+S_circle(n) and
B_n=1+2S_bullet(n) defined in b98bb807d514e80d. Their mass recurrence is

    B_0=C_0=1,
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),
    G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2.

Choosing j=0 gives B_n>=G(1,B_(n-1))>=B_(n-1)+2. Induction proves
B_n>=2n+1. Thus for the actual horizon n=2m+3 of(21),

    B_n V_(m,t)>=3(4m+7)/(13+8m)>1.                   (48)

For(39) the actual horizon is5, even when u=1, and B_5 V_mix>=33/17>1.
Both diagnostic families therefore already give a lower bound above
the original reciprocal profile on their OWN physical words.

This does not supply the original all-word theorem. There is a complete
scoped obstruction for the entire policy of Section3.3, not only for
one bad choice of its increments. Fix ANY t in(0,1), and take all k
prepended steps equal to1+t. Its products are P_i=t^i, so(12),(20) give
for the MINIMUM possible policy radius and the MAXIMUM policy payoff

    D_k>=13/t^(2k),
    V_policy<=3t^(2k)/13.                              (49)

The ordinary Huber reference on exactly this word is
1/[2k(1+t)+11]. Therefore

    V_policy/Huber
       <=[3(2k(1+t)+11)/13]t^(2k) ->0.                 (50)

The actual horizon is k+3. Using the ACCEPTED bound
B_(k+3)<=1+4(k+3)^p, p=log_2(3), also gives

    B_(k+3) V_policy
       <=(3/13)[1+4(k+3)^p]t^(2k) ->0.                 (51)

These limits follow, for example, because the successive ratios of the
positive polynomial-times-t^(2k) expressions tend to t^2<1; eventually
they are bounded above by one fixed number less than1. The bounds hold
even when all increments in the declared policy are chosen with
complete knowledge of the word, by the exact optimality proof(20).
The case t=1/2 already supplies a constant-step3/2 obstruction within
that policy. No assertion about small unrestricted W is implied.

A unit step exposes a separate exact family boundary. Whenever a new
queried point has residual equal to an actual minimizer M, it is X=M+g.
A physical step1 sends it to M. It cannot reach any of the nonminimal
old/new endpoints under discussion. This argument holds for ANY
minimum residual, without orthogonality, and does not say that arbitrary
smooth interpolants fail at unit steps. The mixed construction(39)
uses a strict short step, not a unit step, and pays its intermediate
gradient and value explicitly.

Accordingly, an all-word continuation would have to leave this minimum-
residual policy, redesign the suffix using more of the word, or supply
another justified mechanism. It cannot be deduced from(8), from a
finite gain, or from maintaining only a few of the old rows.

## 10. Scaling, scope and research record

For every constructed word, multiply F by any L>0 and run steps h_i/L.
The points and normalized payoff are unchanged. If unit initial radius
is desired, translate by the ACTUAL closest minimizer M and divide
coordinates by sqrt(D); the function F(M+sqrt(D)x)/D has smoothness1
and the same normalized payoff. Before that optional whole-instance
scaling, all retained old points, gradients and absolute values are
exactly(1). No independent renormalization of a prefix was used.

The unrestricted result of Section2 concerns ONE fixed step2 predecessor.
The exact all-length optimization in Section3.3 concerns ONLY its stated
orthogonal minimum-plane policy. The successful all-m comparisons concern
exactly(21), and the mixed comparison exactly(39). Different finite words
may use different functions and dimensions; each full trajectory uses
one static function. The accepted complex channel keeps its own specified
minimizer and denominator throughout. No all-interpolant optimality for
long prefixes, arbitrary-word competitive invariant, full nonlinear
scalar comparison, W upper bound, or original-target conclusion is claimed.

Literature search was conditioned on the active variable-step minimum-
plane construction. Matlas returned generic conjugacy/max-function
results, not this continuation. Primary-source web retrieval located
the exact already-local arXiv1502.05666; its Sections2.4-2.5, Theorem4
proof and Corollary1 were read. The useful principle is the full affine
maximum in conjugate coordinates. All uses here are reproved directly
or cite the accepted facts above; no new external theorem is imported.

This final joint is a shared proof_attempt, with no fact_id and no formal
verification claim. The original all-h/all-large-horizon goal remains OPEN.
