<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002929 -->
# Three explicit selectors on arbitrary words, and their exact scoped limit

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; worker max3; analytical invocation70.
Assigned ended baseline69, ended ceiling70 / attempted ceiling71, cap1.
Steering: TASK.md and master_guidance32e125bb2cb517f0; consult OFF.

Status: **COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT, UNVERIFIED**. No new fact is
claimed. All new constructions, formulas, and counterexamples in this
document are proved together, rather than used as unverified intermediate
bricks. There were ZERO mathematical programs, arithmetic/numerical
replays, executed trajectories/recurrences, optimizers/DP/solvers, formal
calls, installations, consults, or subagents. This is exactly the three-law
allocation. No extra law, round, audit, or formal submission is inferred.

Result claimed by this draft: a finite explicit attainable portfolio V(h)
on EVERY finite nonnegative physical word, with W_n(h)>=V(h). Laws A and B
have genuinely paid positive middle heights. B is a complete-prefix
completion with an explicit small active-anchor calculation. C completes
scalar quadratic prefixes at the TWO fixed curvatures1 and1/2.

The SEPARATE original comparison is REFUTED FOR THIS PORTFOLIO: on the
SAME-enclosing-m tau1/4 hierarchy, for every m>=1536,

    V(H_(m,1/4))=1/(1+2S_m),
    B_(2^m-1)*V(H_(m,1/4))<237/250.

This excludes the displayed portfolio, not unrestricted W, other selectors,
other planar geometries, other curvatures, other dictionaries, other
initializations, or the original theorem. The original all-n/all-word
target remains OPEN. A missing selector/cover/comparison is theorem-level.

## 1. Normalization, exact target, and permitted accepted inputs

Use real finite-dimensional Euclidean spaces, with the displayed standard
bases, inner products, and norms. For n>=0 and h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1))>=0,
let W_n(h) be the supremum of

    2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L*||x_0-x_star||^2),

over convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient, attained minimum,
a chosen minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, under the ACTUAL updates
x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)*grad f(x_i). A closest minimizer is used for every
constructed witness below. All new instances have true radius1.
Write R_n=W_n/2; no factor2 is omitted. Initially take L=1. Multiplying
the WHOLE fixed objective by L gives exactly the same points with h_i/L,
and exactly the same payoff. Every physical zero step is retained.

The original benchmark uses ALL integer splits:

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,
    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

The requested comparison would be: for EVERY epsilon>0, ONE N_epsilon
independent of h, ALL n>=N_epsilon and ALL h>=0, V(h)>=(1-epsilon)/B_n.
No surrogate benchmark, exponent-only claim, family-only theorem, finite
numerical comparison, or limit along selected horizons substitutes for it.

Accepted correctness premises, at precisely these scopes:

* fact_id1252b8823bb5a290: original signed interpolation residual;
  complete-prefix maximal conjugate completion, including every queried
  value and gradient; its direct proof of smoothness and interpolation.
* fact_idb98bb807d514e80d: the legal canonical/single-cut channel K_n and
  the original all-split definitions.
* fact_id5fd396eba75651e7: ONLY the exact SAME-m hierarchy definition
  and Section10's unconditional numerical original-benchmark inequality
  B_(2^m-1)/(1+2S_m)<237/250 at tau1/4,m>=1536. None of its spiral
  local dynamics or family-optimality arguments is a premise here.
* fact_idc3bfc54321ddec65: its EXACT two specifically defined special
  word families, functions, terminal formulas and benchmark comparisons.
  No arbitrary-word extension of those multistage geometries is inferred.

The prior prototype7d434affa1888c02 and audit8a88b0ff52be018b are only
awareness. No prototype lemma, frozen F/M/E comparison, unverified table,
or audit verdict is a correctness premise for the new laws.

## 2. Law A: a real parameter rule from the whole word

Domain of A and B: h contains at least two entries strictly greater than1.
Select the two largest such entries; rank by decreasing value, then by
earlier original index to break ties. Write their chronological indices
j<k. This is ONE specified pair, not an optimization over unspecified
interpolation or certificate data. Retain all original blocks and put

    P=sum_(i<j)h_i, S=sum_(j<i<k)h_i, T=sum_(i>k)h_i,
    a_sel=h_j-1>0, b_sel=h_k-1>0, M_sel=S+1,
    sigma=1/(P+1), Delta=b_sel/2,
    ell=(M_sel+Delta)/a_sel,
    kappa=Delta/(2T+1), C_sel=M_sel+b_sel.

Here ell,kappa are positive real numbers. They are fixed from h BEFORE
defining the function. Define the two finite guard charges

    E_Q=M_sel*(ell+1)-a_sel*ell,
    Z_max=C_sel-kappa                     if ell>=kappa,
          C_sel-kappa*(T+1)               if ell<kappa,
    E_R=a_sel*kappa*ell+Z_max*(ell-kappa),
    chi=1/[ell^2+kappa*ell+(E_Q)_+/a_sel+(E_R)_+/a_sel].       (A1)

The symbol (u)_+ means max(u,0). All denominators are positive and finite.
In the real plane with e1=(1,0), e2=(0,1), set

    v=(-ell*chi, sqrt(chi-ell^2*chi^2)),
    p_A=sigma*e1, q_A=sigma*v, r_A=-kappa*q_A,
    D_A=conv{0,p_A,q_A,r_A},
    f_A(x)=max_(g in D_A){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}, x0=e1.             (A2)

Thus ||v||^2=chi and <e1,v>=-ell*chi. Formula(A1) gives

    chi*ell^2<1, chi*kappa*ell<1,
    chi*E_Q<a_sel if E_Q>0,
    chi*E_R<a_sel if E_R>0.                                  (A3)

The radicand in(A2) is STRICTLY positive. There is no complex vector,
indefinite metric, dimension limit, or free parameter left to choose.
All four support planes have offsets0 and weights1. The complete static
support function is J_A(u)=max{0,<p_A,u>,<q_A,u>,<r_A,u>}.

## 3. Law A: every initialized query, all support guards, actual terminal

For any compact convex D, the attained maximizer in
max_(g in D)(x.g-||g||^2/2) is the UNIQUE Euclidean projection of x
onto D. For u=x-g its exact necessary and sufficient condition is

    g in D, <u,z-g><=0 for every z in D.                     (A4)

Sufficiency follows by expanding ||x-z||^2-||x-g||^2. Necessity follows
by differentiating that distance along each segment from g to z.
It suffices to check the vertices of D_A. We now check ALL four at
EVERY original query, including selected bridges and zeros.

First sector: for original i<=j, set p_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, 0<=p_i<=P.
Then

    x_i=(1-sigma*p_i)e1,
    g_i=p_A,
    u_i=x_i-p_A=sigma*(P-p_i)e1,
    delta_i=<u_i,p_A>=sigma^2*(P-p_i),
    f_i=delta_i+||p_A||^2/2.                                (A5)

The zero support is below p_A. The q_A comparison is nonpositive
because its first coordinate is negative. The r_A comparison is
sigma^2*(P-p_i)*(kappa*ell*chi-1)<=0 by(A3).
These include BOTH future slopes. At the selected query x_j=p_A,
u_j=0; all support ties leave the projection gradient uniquely p_A.
Its ACTUAL update is

    x_(j+1)=p_A-(1+a_sel)*p_A=-sigma*a_sel*e1.                (A6)

Middle sector: for j<i<=k let mu_i=sum_(j<t<i)h_t, 0<=mu_i<=S.
Then

    x_i=sigma*[-a_sel*e1-mu_i*v], g_i=q_A,
    u_i=sigma*[-a_sel*e1-(mu_i+1)*v],
    delta_i=sigma^2*chi*[a_sel*ell-(mu_i+1)],
    f_i=delta_i+||q_A||^2/2.                                (A7)

The middle height is PAID and strictly positive at EVERY such query:

    delta_i>=sigma^2*chi*Delta>0.                            (A8)

Hence zero and r_A are below q_A. The comparison against the RETAINED
old p_A is

    <u_i,p_A-q_A>
     =sigma^2*[-a_sel+chi*((mu_i+1)*(ell+1)-a_sel*ell)]
     <=sigma^2*[-a_sel+chi*E_Q]<0.                           (A9)

If E_Q<=0 the last strict inequality follows directly from a_sel>0;
if E_Q>0 it follows from(A3). Thus(A7) is the ACTUAL original gradient
at every middle physical step, no matter its individual size. In
particular x_k=sigma[-a_sel*e1-S*v] and the second selected update is

    x_(k+1)=sigma[-a_sel*e1-C_sel*v].                        (A10)

Last sector: for k<i<=n let nu_i=sum_(k<t<i)h_t, 0<=nu_i<=T,
where i=n is the actual terminal. Define Z_i=C_sel-kappa*(nu_i+1).
Then

    x_i=sigma[-a_sel*e1-(C_sel-kappa*nu_i)*v], g_i=r_A,
    u_i=sigma[-a_sel*e1-Z_i*v],
    delta_i=sigma^2*kappa*chi*(Z_i-a_sel*ell)
           =||r_A||^2*(2T-nu_i),
    f_i=delta_i+||r_A||^2/2.                                (A11)

The equality uses Delta=kappa*(2T+1), not an unpaid height assignment.
The least Z_i is C_sel-kappa*(T+1), and

    Z_i-a_sel*ell=Delta-kappa*(nu_i+1)>=kappa*T>=0.

Consequently zero and q_A are below r_A. Against the RETAINED old p_A,

    <u_i,p_A-r_A>
     =sigma^2[-a_sel+chi*(a_sel*kappa*ell+Z_i*(ell-kappa))]
     <=sigma^2[-a_sel+chi*E_R]<0.                            (A12)

The choice of Z_max in(A1) is exactly the worst endpoint for this
linear expression, including ell=kappa. If E_R<=0 the inequality
is immediate from its displayed negative term; otherwise use(A3).
All old, present, future and remote dictionary supports are retained.

Induction on original indices using(A5)--(A12) proves every actual
update. Nonnegative steps put partial masses inside the stated closed
intervals even if individual entries are unbounded or unordered. Empty
blocks, interior zero steps, trailing zeros, T=0, and k=n-1 are included.
No reset or rescaling of an inherited state took place.

In particular the UNQUERIED terminal and its REAL gradient/value are

    x_n=sigma[-a_sel*e1-(C_sel-kappa*T)*v],
    grad f_A(x_n)=r_A,
    V_A(h)=2f_A(x_n)=sigma^2*chi*Delta^2/(2T+1)
                         =(2T+1)*||r_A||^2>0.              (A13)

T=0 gives zero proximal height at the terminal, but still the printed
positive gradient-square objective. Its value was evaluated on the fixed
function, not inferred from a preceding positive-height snapshot.

## 4. Law A: global legality, every row, minimum, true radius, actual Gram

The projection normal inequalities at x,x' give
<x-x',g-g'> >= ||g-g'||^2. Thus the projection is1-Lipschitz.
Evaluation of the two attained maxima bounds
f_A(x+d)-f_A(x)-g.d between0 and(g'-g).d, whose absolute value
is at most||d||^2. This proves differentiability with continuous
gradient g=projection_D_A(x), global convexity, and1-smoothness.
The nonempty interior of the triangle D_A has gradient x and Hessian I,
so the least global smoothness is exactly1. The function is finite
everywhere, and all parameters were fixed before GD.

The COMPLETE attained minimum set and minimum value are

    C_A={z:z1<=0, <v,z>=0}, min f_A=0.                       (A14)

Indeed all slope inner products are nonpositive there. Conversely a
positive inner product with a vertex makes f_A positive by taking a
sufficiently small positive multiple of that vertex in D_A. Opposite
q_A,r_A force equality in their common direction. For z in C_A,
||e1-z||^2=1-2z1+||z||^2>=1, with equality only at z=0.
Thus origin is the UNIQUE closest minimizer, TRUE radius1.

For EVERY ordered pair of actual samples, including terminal and any
minimizer, define Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.
For two projection samples the exact cancellation is

    Q_ij=<u_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                                  (A15)

This uses the complete normal(A4), so it covers both orientations and
remote pairs without a nearest-neighbor assumption. For a minimum z,
g_star=f_star=0, Q_(i,star)=z.g_i<=0 and
Q_(star,i)=-u_i.g_i<=0. Repeated actual jets have Q=0.

An explicit recipe gives EVERY actual Gram entry. Write every relevant
vector as sigma*(a_u e1+b_u v); then

    <u,w>=sigma^2*[a_u*a_w-ell*chi*(a_u*b_w+b_u*a_w)
                                      +chi*b_u*b_w].         (A16)

For (x0,g0,...,gn), use coefficient(1/sigma,0) for x0, (1,0) for
each first-sector gradient, (0,1) for each middle gradient, (0,-kappa)
for each last-sector gradient including gn, and(0,0) for origin.
Positions have their explicit coefficients in(A5),(A7),(A11).
The two-dimensional metric in(A16) has positive determinant
chi*(1-ell^2*chi), so these are actual REAL Euclidean Grams and are PSD.
Actual gradient repetitions at zero steps are retained, not deleted as
formal coordinates in an upper certificate.

## 5. Law B: complete retained prefix, six anchors, new true minimum

Keep the SAME parameters, initial point, full original old prefix and
terminal point as law A. Retain only actual QUERIED endpoints when
compressing affine anchors. Define a finite list of pairs(y,delta):

    O:       (0,0),
    P0:      ((1-sigma)e1, sigma*(1-sigma)),
    Q0:      (sigma[-a_sel*e1-v],
                       sigma^2*chi*(a_sel*ell-1)),
    Qend:    (sigma[-a_sel*e1-M_sel*v], sigma^2*chi*Delta).      (B1)

If k<n-1, append the two RETAINED r_A-query anchors

    R0:      (sigma[-a_sel*e1-(C_sel-kappa)*v],
                                             2T*||r_A||^2),
    Rlast:   (sigma[-a_sel*e1-
                       (C_sel-kappa*(T-h_(n-1)+1))*v],
                                    (T+h_(n-1))*||r_A||^2).   (B2)

If k=n-1, neither R0 nor Rlast is a queried anchor, so append NEITHER.
This distinction includes a final selected bridge and prevents inserting
an unpromised terminal jet. Repeated anchors may be kept; they cause no
change of maximum. There are at most SIX anchors.

For each original prefix jet let y_i=x_i-g_i,delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.
The conjugate lower quadratic of accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 is
exactly ||w||^2/2+y_i.w-delta_i. In each of the THREE sectors in
(A5),(A7),(A11), BOTH y_i and delta_i are affine in its spent mass.
First-sector endpoints are P0 and the zero anchor at the actual j-query.
Middle endpoints are Q0 and Qend, both actual queries. Last endpoints,
when queried, are R0 and Rlast with last spent mass T-h_(n-1).
Every intervening prefix anchor is a convex combination of those two
QUERIED endpoints. If the spent interval has length0 all its anchors
coincide. This proves equality of the FULL original prefix maximum with

    H_B(w)=||w||^2/2+max_(a in(B1),(B2)){y_a.w-delta_a},
    f_B(x)=sup_w{x.w-H_B(w)}.                               (B3)

All offsets and weights(1), the dimension2, metric and initialized
prefix are fully specified. Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 applies
to the whole prefix because(A15) proves every ordered row. It preserves
EVERY preterminal x_i,g_i,f_i, including zeros and bridge queries.
Thus one f_B fixed BEFORE the actual GD run has exactly the original
prefix by induction. It is globally finite, convex C1 and1-smooth.
It is the maximal completion of THIS prefix, not an unknown optimal
transcript or a black-box terminal oracle.

The new COMPLETE minimum set follows independently from(B3). H_B(0)=0,
so f_B>=0. Exactly the zero-offset anchors are active at0 and
argmin f_B=partial H_B(0), their convex hull. P0 has positive delta
unless P=0, when its slope is0; the zero bridge anchor is already O.
Both Q deltas are strictly positive. If T>0, both r-query deltas
are positive. If T=0 and k<n-1, all remaining steps are zero and
both zero-offset r anchors coincide at

    y_R=-sigma*a_sel*(e1+ell*v), (y_R)1<0.                  (B4)

If k=n-1 there is no r-query anchor. Consequently

    argmin f_B={0}                   if T>0 or k=n-1,
    argmin f_B=conv{0,y_R}            if T=0 and k<n-1,
    min f_B=0.                                             (B5)

Every minimum has first coordinate<=0. The distance calculation used
in(A14), applied to THIS new hull, proves origin is uniquely closest
to e1 and the TRUE radius is1. The old radius was not carried across
completion without proof. A finite maximum of affine planes has an
open region of a unique winning plane; on its translated conjugate
region f_B has Hessian I. Hence its least global smoothness is1.

## 6. Law B: complete finite terminal evaluation, both orientations, Gram

Here is a closed finite real calculation on the already-proved SIX-anchor
template, not optimization of an unknown feasible interpolation/PSD set.
At x=x_n from(A13), enumerate every nonempty subset I of the known
anchor list with at most3 affinely independent slopes. Choose its first
anchor0 as base (this subscript means the subset's base, not necessarily O).
For a singleton put w=x-y_0. For a larger subset let D_I have columns
y_a-y_0 and let e_I have components delta_a-delta_0. Set

    K_I=D_I^T D_I,
    lambda_I=K_I^(-1)[D_I^T(x-y_0)-e_I],
    w_I=x-y_0-D_I*lambda_I.                                (B6)

K_I is either a positive1x1 or positive2x2 matrix. Invert it by the
ordinary scalar reciprocal or determinant formula; all entries are
given real scalar products in(A16). Retain the subset if

    every lambda component>=0, sum lambda<=1,
    y_a.w_I-delta_a<=y_0.w_I-delta_0 for EVERY retained anchor. (B7)

A singleton has vacuous lambda conditions. The last inequalities,
including all six anchors, are mandatory. Use the lexicographically
first retained subset. Define

    g_n^B=w_I,
    f_B(x_n)=(x_n-y_0).w_I-||w_I||^2/2+delta_0,
    V_B(h)=2f_B(x_n).                                      (B8)

To prove existence, maximize x.w-H_B(w). Quadratic growth gives
an attained unique maximizer. Its optimality says
x-w is in the convex hull of its active anchor slopes. A point in a
planar finite convex hull is a convex combination of at most3 affinely
independent slopes: whenever more than3 are present, an affine dependence
of their coefficients permits moving weights until one becomes0;
repeat, and delete any remaining dependent coefficients. The surviving
slopes satisfy(B6),(B7). Conversely(B6) imposes equal active-plane
values and x-w=y_0+D_I*lambda_I. Conditions(B7) make this a convex
combination of ACTUAL active slopes. Thus x is a subgradient of H_B
at w_I, proving optimality and(B8). All retained subsets yield the
same w_I because of strict convexity. This also handles tied faces,
boundary weights, zero repeats, small/zero last step and T=0.

For ANY actual prefix/terminal/minimum pair, f_i=x_i.g_i-H_B(g_i)
and x_i is a subgradient of H_B at g_i. Its1-strong convexity gives

    H_B(g_i)>=H_B(g_j)+x_j.(g_i-g_j)+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

The EXACT cancellation of the signed residual is

    Q_ij=H_B(g_j)-H_B(g_i)+x_j.(g_i-g_j)
                                        +||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0. (B9)

This is ALL directed and optimum rows in BOTH orientations, not only
the outgoing terminal rows used to find a completion. With h_(n-1)=0,
x_n=x_(n-1), so the preserved jet forces(B8) to equal the last prefix
value and gradient. No fresh objective charge at that repeated point
is possible. The separately proved(B5) supplies the actual radius.

Every original gradient before n is still p_A,q_A,r_A. The terminal
gradient is the REAL vector w_I in(B6), which need not equal r_A.
If w_I=(w1,w2), let b_w=w2/v2 and a_w=w1+ell*chi*b_w, so
w_I=a_w e1+b_w v. Use(a_w/sigma,b_w/sigma) in(A16) for gn.
That formula specifies the FULL actual Gram, including every queried
coordinate. Its PSD follows from its displayed real positive metric.

Since f_A is one legal interpolant of the same prefix and retained optimum,
the maximality part of fact1252b8823bb5a290 gives f_B(x_n)>=f_A(x_n).
Their two true radii were separately proved1. Thus V_B>=V_A; no illegal
trajectory average or exchanged radius is involved.

One useful quantitative upper for later diagnostics is also explicit.
If k<n-1, the LAST r-anchor alone in(B3) gives, for d=h_(n-1),

    V_B<=2delta_Rlast+||x_n-y_Rlast||^2
         =V_A+d^2*||r_A||^2
         =V_A*[1+d^2/(2T+1)].                              (B10)

This is an upper only on THIS full-prefix completion and THIS paid
prefix, not on unrestricted W. It retains the original last query.

## 7. Law C: two fixed scalar curvatures, full prefix, sorted formula

This law is defined for EVERY word. For n=0 use f(x)=x^2/2,x0=1,
payoff1. For n>=1 choose eta in the TWO-element set{1,1/2}.
Start the old scalar quadratic eta*x^2/2 from1 and specify directly

    xi_i=product_(t<i)(1-eta*h_t), 0<=i<=n,
    gamma_i=eta*xi_i, f_i^old=eta*xi_i^2/2, 0<=i<n,
    y_i=(1-eta)*xi_i, delta_i=eta*(1-eta)*xi_i^2/2.           (C1)

These are symbolic finite formulas, not an executed recurrence or
arithmetic replay. They include every original query with its value
and gradient, any sign, order, size or zero. For old prefix pairs,

    Q_ij=-eta*(1-eta)*(xi_i-xi_j)^2/2<=0,                    (C2)

including the optimum xi_star=gamma_star=f_star=0.
Define the WHOLE completed function, fixed from h before GD, by

    H_eta(w)=w^2/2+max{0,max_(0<=i<n)(y_i*w-delta_i)},
    F_eta(x)=sup_w{x*w-H_eta(w)}.                            (C3)

Fact1252b8823bb5a290 and(C2) preserve every old prefix jet. The
actual run has x_i=xi_i up to n, by physical-index induction; the
terminal gradient is evaluated on F_eta, rather than assumed eta*xi_n.
Both completions are globally convex C1 and1-smooth. At eta1,
H_1=w^2/2 and F_1=x^2/2. At eta1/2, delta_i=y_i^2/2.

For eta1/2 sort the distinct known slopes{0,y_0,...,y_(n-1)} into
u_0<...<u_r; every original jet is still retained, and only identical
planes are merged for evaluation. For each adjacent pair define
theta_a=(u_a+u_(a+1))/2. Plane a wins exactly on the interval
[theta_(a-1),theta_a], with the first interval unbounded below and
the last unbounded above. Indeed the equality of any two parabolic
planes is at their midpoint; ordering shows all more distant planes
are below on the displayed interval. At x=xi_n set

    w_a=clip(x-u_a to that interval),
    E_a=(x-u_a)*w_a-w_a^2/2+u_a^2/2.                        (C4)

Clipping is the usual minimum/maximum with the finite endpoint(s);
an absent endpoint imposes no bound. Choose the first a with largest
E_a. Then

    grad F_(1/2)(xi_n)=w_a, 2F_(1/2)(xi_n)=2max_a E_a.        (C5)

This is a finite explicit real rule on known scalar anchors after
the full original lift, without curvature optimization or a feasible-set
oracle. Strict concavity makes tied maximizing intervals give the
same gradient. Set

    V_C(h)=max{xi_n(eta1)^2, 2F_(1/2)(xi_n(eta1/2))}.         (C6)

The minimum value is0 and the COMPLETE minimum set is{0}: H_eta(0)=0;
for eta1 all affine slopes are0, and for eta1/2 a zero-offset plane
has y_i=0. Therefore partial H_eta(0)={0}. The true radius from1
is1 for each completion. The smoothness constant is exactly1 on a
translated region of any unique winning plane.

Equation(B9), with H_eta and scalar products, proves every directed
prefix/terminal/optimum residual in both orientations. It includes
all repeated queries and the actual terminal from(C5). The FULL Gram
of(x0,g0,...,gn) is G_ab=z_a*z_b, where z_x0=1,
z_i=eta*xi_i for i<n and z_n is the real terminal gradient from
(C3)--(C5); eta1 gives z_n=xi_n. This is an actual scalar PSD Gram.

For n>=1, the LAST retained quadratic alone gives the template upper

    2F_eta(xi_n)<=eta*xi_(n-1)^2
                         *[1-2eta*d+eta*d^2], d=h_(n-1).    (C7)

It follows either by maximizing that one conjugate lower quadratic or
by smoothness at the preserved last jet. It is a completed-family upper,
never a bound on unrestricted W. If d=0, the actual terminal is exactly
that preserved last jet and its value is the original quadratic value.

## 8. A genuinely quantitative channel beyond K, not just a recursion

Take the exact word h=(5,5); both selections have P=S=T=0,
a_sel=b_sel=4,M_sel=sigma=1. Direct symbolic substitution gives

    ell=3/4, kappa=2, E_Q=-5/4,
    E_R=4*2*(3/4)+3*(3/4-2)=9/4,
    chi=1/(9/16+3/2+9/16)=8/21,
    V_A=32/21.                                             (E1)

Here K_2(5,5)=16/11 and V_A/K_2=22/21>1. The paid static
triangle therefore adds a channel beyond K. The same pattern also
has a symbolic gain for h=(b,b), b=1+A, real A>=16. Here

    ell=1/2+1/A, kappa=A/2,
    E_Q=(1-A)/2+1/A<0,
    E_R=A/4+1+1/A,
    chi=1/[A/4+1+2/A+2/A^2],
    V_A=A^2/[A+4+8/A+8/A^2].                               (E2)

The formula for E_R in(E2) follows by expanding
A*kappa*ell+(1+A-kappa)*(ell-kappa), since T=0.
The accepted K for this word is

    max{1/(4A+5), A^2/(2A+3), A^2/(A+2)^2}
             =A^2/(2A+3) at A>=16.                        (E3)

For A>=16, A+4+8/A+8/A^2<2A+3, because
1+8/A+8/A^2<A. Therefore(E2) is STRICTLY above K.
This checks a nontrivial attained terminal, not a trajectory reset or
an unpriced static projection recursion. B is at least A on the same
word with its independently proved radius. C's unit quadratic can be
even stronger on this example; no domination over all other channels
is asserted. No mathematical program was used to evaluate any example.

## 9. Precise portfolio and accepted special-word channel

If A/B's domain is empty put V_A=V_B=0 as unavailable channels, not as
claimed positive-radius constant-function witnesses. The accepted K is

    K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2s(h)),
         max_j (h_j-1)_+^2/[(1+P_j)^2*(1+2T_j)]}.            (P1)

Factb98bb807d514e80d supplies every attained nonzero single-cut term:
from1, derivative clip(x to[-a_H,c_H]) with
c_H=1/(1+P_j), a_H=c_H*(h_j-1)/(1+2T_j), minimum0 and true radius1.
The full prefix gradient is c_H and the full suffix gradient is-a_H.
Its explicit value is x^2/2 on[-a_H,c_H], c_H*x-c_H^2/2
on x>=c_H, and -a_H*x-a_H^2/2 on x<=-a_H. Before the
selected original update x_i=1-c_H*sum_(t<i)h_t; immediately
after it x_(j+1)=-a_H*(2T_j+1), and at later spent suffix mass u
x=-a_H*(2T_j+1-u). These remain in their printed constant-gradient
regions. The terminal is -a_H*(T_j+1), gradient-a_H and value
a_H^2*(T_j+1/2). Its full actual scalar Gram has coordinates1 for
x0, c_H for each queried gradient through j and -a_H afterwards
including gn. All directed rows in both orientations hold by the
explicit smooth convex function, equivalently fact1252's necessity.
The canonical one-sided Huber has threshold d_H=1/(1+2s), derivative
0 at x<=0,x at0<=x<=d_H,d_H at x>=d_H; all iterates from1 stay
in its last sector, its complete minima are(-infinity,0], radius1,
and its actual terminal payoff is d_H. These are static functions.
Its original positions are x_i=1-d_H*sum_(t<i)h_t, and each
gradient, including the terminal gradient, is d_H; its scalar Gram
is the outer product of(1,d_H,...,d_H). Its value is0 at x<=0,
x^2/2 on[0,d_H] and d_H*x-d_H^2/2 at x>=d_H.

Define V_special(h)=0 unless h EXACTLY equals one of the following
accepted SPECIFIC special words of factc3bfc54321ddec65. Recognition
uses h's own ACTUAL length n; it is not a fitted new multistage law.
For a three-stage word, N=floor((n-3)/9)>=2^60; for a return word,
N=floor((n-4)/12)>=2^60. Put, using THAT N,

    M_mc=2N(N+1)+1, A_mc=M_mc^(1/12), B_mc=M_mc^(1/120),
    epsilon=M_mc^(-1/3), C_mc=2B_mc+1,
    Chi_N=(sec^2((2j-1)pi/[2(2N+1)]))_(j=1,...,N).

L_up is THREE increasing copies of Chi_N, L_down THREE decreasing
copies. The three-stage base is

    (L_up, A_mc*M_mc+1, L_down, B_mc*M_mc+1,
                                             L_down, 1+M_mc*epsilon),

length9N+3. Insert exactly n-(9N+3) LEADING zeros. The return base is

    (L_up,A_mc*M_mc+1,L_down,B_mc*M_mc+1,
          L_down,C_mc*M_mc+1,L_down,1+M_mc*epsilon),

length12N+4, with exactly n-(12N+4) leading zeros. Every list mass
is M_mc-1. All hypotheses M_mc>=2,B_mc>=2,A_mc>=4B_mc+4 and
M_mc*epsilon>=2B_mc^2 hold on these specific words, by the accepted fact.

For completeness the accepted function's full real prescription is:
in R3 let a_mc=1-1/M_mc, nu_mc=sqrt(2A_mc-4),

    p_mc=e1/M_mc,
    q_mc=(-2e1+nu_mc*e2)/M_mc, Q_mc=2A_mc/M_mc^2,
    r_mc=-q_mc/2+sqrt((B_mc/2-1/4)*Q_mc)*e3,
    R_mc=||r_mc||^2=B_mc*Q_mc/2,
    y=-A_mc*e1-M_mc*q_mc,
    z=y-B_mc*M_mc*q_mc-M_mc*r_mc,
    w=z-C_mc*M_mc*r_mc-M_mc*q_mc,
    tP=a_mc*e1, deltaP=a_mc/M_mc,
    tQ=y+(M_mc-1)*q_mc, deltaQ=(M_mc-1)*Q_mc,
    tR=z+(M_mc-1)*r_mc, deltaR=(M_mc-1)*R_mc,
    tD=w+(M_mc-1)*q_mc, deltaD=(M_mc-1)*Q_mc.

Its conjugate is ||v||^2/2 plus the maximum of the six planes
0,tP.v-deltaP,y.v,tQ.v-deltaQ,z.v,tR.v-deltaR in the three-stage
case; append w.v,tD.v-deltaD in the return case. All plane weights
are1. Its conjugate function, initial point e1, standard metric,
and ALL these parameters are fixed before the run.

The accepted original query gradients are p_mc,q_mc,r_mc, or
p_mc,q_mc,r_mc,q_mc, respectively. Within each list of spent mass s,
the proximal point is X-(1+s)*g, with X=e1 in the first
sector (giving x=e1-s*p_mc), X=-A_mc*e1 in the next,
X=y-B_mc*M_mc*q_mc in the r sector, and
X=z-C_mc*M_mc*r_mc in the returned q sector. The selected bridge
query is s=M_mc-1, followed by literal subtraction of its displayed
bridge times g. The first r-query anchor tR is retained. Leading
zeros repeat the initial jet. Factc3bfc's Sections1--2 establish
every retained support and directed row, BOTH orientations and zeros,
on precisely these hypotheses; this portfolio reuses that fact_id.

Here is the complete accepted support-height table for those actual
queries, with t=1+s in[1,M_mc]. Values are the printed active
height delta plus half the squared current gradient; all original
interior queries and each following bridge query use this table.

|Current gradient|Actual proximal point|Active height delta|Other retained support heights|
|---|---|---|---|
|p_mc|(1-t/M_mc)e1|(1-t/M_mc)/M_mc|q=-2delta, r=delta, zero=0|
|q_mc|-A_mc e1-t q_mc|(M_mc-t)Q_mc|p=(-A_mc+2t/M_mc)/M_mc, r=-delta/2, zero=0|
|r_mc|y-B_mc M_mc q_mc-t r_mc|(M_mc-t)R_mc|q=-B_mc M_mc Q_mc+t Q_mc/2, p=(2-A_mc+2B_mc-t/M_mc)/M_mc, zero=0|
|returned q_mc|z-C_mc M_mc r_mc-t q_mc|(M_mc-t)Q_mc|r=-C_mc M_mc R_mc+t Q_mc/2, p=(-A_mc+2t/M_mc)/M_mc, zero=0|

The old complete dictionary is conv{0,p_mc,q_mc,r_mc}. Every other
height is at most the printed active height at the accepted hypotheses,
including the initial p/r ties, all caps and boundary equalities.
The sector's actual position is its proximal point plus the current
gradient. Endpoints before/after the literal bridges are respectively
p_mc -> -A_mc e1, y+q_mc -> y-B_mc M_mc q_mc,
z+r_mc -> z-C_mc M_mc r_mc when the returning bridge exists,
and then w+q_mc. The old terminal completion is evaluated by(P2),
retaining ALL six/eight queried-prefix anchors rather than resetting
one of these endpoints. This table and the printed coordinates also
give every original prefix value without an unprinted value choice.

The complete new minima are conv{0,y,z} or conv{0,y,z,w}, with value0,
unique closest minimizer0 and true radius1. The unqueried terminal is
z-M_mc*epsilon*r_mc or w-M_mc*epsilon*q_mc. Its ACTUAL gradient/payoff:

    E3=M_mc*(B_mc+a_mc)*q_mc+M_mc*r_mc,
    D3=2A_mc*[(B_mc+a_mc)^2-(B_mc+a_mc)+B_mc/2],
    b3=A_mc*a_mc*(epsilon-2/M_mc), theta3=(b3)_+/D3,
    g_three=-M_mc*epsilon*r_mc-theta3*E3,
    V_three=A_mc*B_mc*epsilon^2-(b3)_+^2/D3;

    dR=C_mc+a_mc, ER=M_mc*q_mc+M_mc*dR*r_mc,
    DR=2A_mc*[1-dR+(B_mc/2)*dR^2],
    bR=2A_mc*[(B_mc-1/(2M_mc))*epsilon-B_mc*a_mc/(2M_mc)],
    thetaR=bR/DR,
    g_return=-M_mc*epsilon*q_mc-thetaR*ER,
    V_return=2A_mc*epsilon^2-bR^2/DR.                         (P2)

The full actual Gram is the Gram of the printed real coordinates
(e1,p_mc,q_mc,r_mc,g_terminal) with each query gradient repeated in
its original sector, including initial-zero repeats. For example
q_mc.r_mc=-Q_mc/2,p_mc.q_mc=-2/M_mc^2,
p_mc.r_mc=1/M_mc^2,p_mc^2=1/M_mc^2,q_mc^2=Q_mc,r_mc^2=R_mc;
the terminal inner products follow by linearity from(P2). No rank
or absent-query convention changes its PSD or original rows.

Use the matching value in(P2) for V_special. If both recognitions
hold use their maximum. Each is an actual fixed static instance at
its OWN physical horizon, not a module oracle. Accepted factc3bfc
gives, with d_N=floor(log_2 N),

    B_n*V_special >= [3*lambda^3/(2*20^(7/12))]
                      *(lambda/2^(7/6))^d_N>1.              (P3)

Define the precisely scoped all-word portfolio

    V(h)=max{K_n(h),V_A(h),V_B(h),V_C(h),V_special(h)}.        (P4)

Each available value is attained by ONE member of a finite list of legal
fixed-function instances, at its separately proved true radius1. Choose
a largest value, with fixed tie order K,A,B,C,special. The entire choice
depends only on the complete word. Thus W_n(h)>=V(h) on EVERY word,
including empty/all-zero words, unbounded unordered entries and any
small/zero last step. No average, adaptive function replacement,
forced reset, feasible-set search or carried radius occurs.

## 10. SAME-enclosing-m tau1/4 hierarchy: full original diagnostic

Fix m>=8,tau=1/4 ONCE. Throughout EVERY child use

    lambda=1+sqrt2, beta=1-1/(4m), c_h=lambda*(1+1/(4m)),
    a_h=beta/c_h, H_0=(), H_(l+1)=(H_l,1+a_h*c_h^l,H_l),
    S_l=sum H_l, X_l=S_l+1, n_m=2^m-1.

This is NOT a basic-lambda q_m and no enclosing m is reset.
Since sqrt2<17/12, c_h<(29/12)*(33/32)=319/128<5/2.
Also c_h>lambda>2,0<a_h<1/2, and
c_h*(c_h-2)>lambda*(lambda-2)=1>beta. Consequently

    d_h=c_h-2<1/2, 0<a_h<d_h,
    v_h=a_h/d_h in(0,1),
    X_l=v_h*c_h^l+(1-v_h)*2^l,
    a_h*c_h^l<=d_h*X_l, X_l<c_h^l for l>=1.                (H1)

The exact mass identity is obtained from X_(l+1)=2X_l+a_h*c_h^l
by direct substitution and X_0=1; it was not numerically executed.

### 10.1 EVERY cut, not just the selected two

At an original occurrence of level l, its immediately adjacent left
and right complete children are H_l. Thus original P+1>=X_l and
T+1>=X_l. Write A_l=a_h*c_h^l, p=P+1,t=T+1. The cut-to-Huber ratio is

    A_l^2*(2p+2t+2A_l-1)/[p^2*(2t-1)]
      =(A_l/p)^2*[1+2(p+A_l)/(2t-1)].                      (H2)

For fixed A_l,t this expression decreases with p: expanding it gives
positive multiples of1/p^2 and1/p. For fixed p it decreases with t.
Its largest value subject to p,t>=X_l is at p=t=X_l. By(H1) and
X_l>=1 it is at most

    d_h^2*[1+2(1+d_h)*X_l/(2X_l-1)]
                      <=d_h^2*(3+2d_h)<1.                 (H3)

Thus EVERY original single-cut term is strictly below the Huber term,
and K_(n_m)(H_m)=H_base:=1/(2S_m+1). No omitted cut or surrogate
recurrence is involved.

### 10.2 Actual top-two chronology and law A

The largest step is the level(m-1) root. The two second largest are
level(m-2) roots; earlier-index tie-break selects the LEFT root. In
chronological order j=2^(m-2)-1,k=2^(m-1)-1. Put

    X=X_(m-2), A=a_h*c_h^(m-2), alpha=A/X<1/2,
    Y=X_(m-1)=2X+A, b_sel=c_h*A,
    P=S=X-1, M_sel=X, T=Y-1,
    S_m+1=2Y+b_sel.                                       (H4)

These are ACTUAL full blocks; no original step is dropped. Formula(A3)
implies chi<ell^(-2), so(A13) gives

    V_A/H_base
     < [c_h^2*alpha^4/(2+c_h*alpha)^2]
                           *[(4Y+2b_sel-1)/(2Y-1)].        (H5)

The first bracket is <25/256 because c_h<5/2,alpha<1/2 and
2+c_h*alpha>=2. For the second,

    (4Y+2b_sel-1)/(2Y-1)=2+(2b_sel+1)/(2Y-1)
       <2+[(5/2)X+1]/(4X-1)<=19/6.                        (H6)

The last inequality follows by multiplying positive denominators at
X>=1: 6*((5/2)X+1)<=7*(4X-1) is equivalent to13X>=13.
Consequently the quantitative scoped limit is

    V_A<(475/1536)*H_base.                                 (H7)

### 10.3 COMPLETE completion B retains the last query

There are r_A queries after the second selected root; T=Y-1>=1.
The ACTUAL final physical step is d0=1+a_h<3/2. Apply the complete
last-anchor upper(B10), with its newly proved TRUE radius1:

    V_B<=V_A*[1+d0^2/(2T+1)]
         <(7/4)*V_A<(3325/6144)*H_base<H_base.              (H8)

This defeats free terminal completion of THIS whole paid prefix. It
does not propagate a snapshot or erase the tail: the original last
jet and its actual d0 are what bound the final objective.

### 10.4 Scalar completions C: exact level multiplicities

Level l occurs exactly2^(m-l-1) times. For eta1, the full terminal
product, including the final step, has absolute value

    product_l(a_h*c_h^l)^(2^(m-l-1))
       =beta^(2^m-1)*c_h^(-m),                             (H9)

because the two exponent sums are2^m-1 and2^m-m-1. Hence
V_(eta1)<=c_h^(-2m). From(H1), 2S_m+1<2c_h^m, so its ratio
to H_base is <2c_h^(-m)<1.

For eta1/2, set q_count=2^(m-2). At levels0,1 and l>=2 the factors
have the respective upper bounds

    (1-a_h)/2<1/2,
    (1-beta)/2=1/(8m),
    |1-a_h*c_h^l|/2<=c_h^(l-1)/2.

The last bound holds because a_h*c_h^l=beta*c_h^(l-1)>1
for l>=2 and m>=8; beta>=31/32,c_h>2 suffice. Summing the
high-level multiplicities gives q_count-1 and their (l-1)-weighted
sum gives2q_count-m. Thus the EXACT full-word quadratic prefix
formula(C1) has the analytic bound

    |xi_n|<2c_h^(-m)*[c_h^2/(64m)]^q_count
           <2c_h^(-m)*64^(-q_count).                       (H10)

Here c_h^2/(64m)<25/2048<1/64. The last-step factor
(1-a_h)/2>1/4, hence |xi_(n-1)|<4|xi_n|. Applying the RETAINED
last-jet upper(C7) with eta1/2 and d0<2 gives

    2F_(1/2)(xi_n)<=xi_(n-1)^2/2
                    <32c_h^(-2m)*64^(-2q_count).           (H11)

Its H_base ratio is <64c_h^(-m)*64^(-2q_count)<1.
Therefore BOTH real scalar completions, including their actual free
terminal values/gradients, lie below H_base on this SAME physical word.

### 10.5 Recognized c3bfc words are different words

Every accepted SPECIAL word ends in1+M_mc^(2/3)>2. The current
hierarchy ends in1+a_h<3/2. They cannot be identical, regardless
of leading zeros, so V_special(H_m)=0. Basic-lambda F/M/E prototypes
were not exchanged for this hierarchy or for c3bfc's Chebyshev words.

Equations(H3),(H7),(H8),(H9)--(H11) and(P4) prove the EXACT scoped
portfolio equality

    V(H_(m,1/4))=H_base for EVERY m>=8.                     (H12)

At m>=1536 the accepted ORIGINAL all-split benchmark comparison of
fact5fd396eba75651e7, Section10 equation77, gives

    B_(n_m)*V(H_(m,1/4))<237/250.                           (H13)

For example choose epsilon=1/25, so1-epsilon=24/25>237/250.
For every proposed word-independent N_epsilon there is an m>=1536
with2^m-1>=N_epsilon, and(H13) violates the proposed comparison.
This is a precise counterexample to(P4)'s uniform lower functional,
not a full W upper. No limit exchange, fixed-m reset, basic-lambda
substitution or finite numerical evidence appears in this argument.

## 11. Special c3bfc diagnostic at its OWN horizon and parameters

On each recognized SPECIAL word, (P3) proves the portfolio's payoff
is above its original U_n^ZJ at every declared horizon; the accepted
ratio tends to infinity. Those are legal selected-word gains, not a
cover of arbitrary schedules.

The scalar completion C does not silently repair the three-stage
mechanism there. Its actual last physical step is d=1+M_mc^(2/3)>=4.
For eta1/2, the last-jet bound(C7) divided by the same quadratic's
actual terminal payoff gives, when the prefix product is nonzero,

    [1-d+d^2/2]/(1-d/2)^2<=8.                              (D1)

Indeed1-d+d^2/2<=d^2/2 and d/2-1>=d/4 for d>=4.
If the prefix product is zero both the upper and quadratic payoff
are zero. Eta1's completion IS its quadratic. Therefore

    V_C(h)<=8Q_n(h)                                        (D2)

on these exact words. Accepted factc3bfc gives Q_n=o(U_n^ZJ)
on both declared SPECIAL families, with their OWN actual n,N,M_mc.
Thus C remains a deficient channel there; the retained three/four-cell
accepted completion is what supplies the certified special-word gain.
No bound on all nonlinear scalar functions is asserted.

## 12. Failure synthesis and exact handoff interface

All three specified new laws are now explicit actual-function selectors
on their domains; the combined(P4) is legal on all words. A and B have
paid positive middle heights and complete retained dictionaries/anchors.
Every terminal is evaluated on a function fixed before GD; no terminal
jet is assumed from the last queried sector when completion changes it.
The zero-last-step limit preserves the actual promised jet and each
radius is independently proved.

The comparison is not merely stuck for these fixed laws: (H12),(H13)
REFUTE it. The next missing mechanism is a different full-word selector
or cover retaining enough inter-sector geometry that its original
benchmark deficit vanishes uniformly in all h. Enlarging the chosen
pair, changing slopes/curvatures, using a growing noncollinear dictionary,
or a different entire retained-prefix program are OUTSIDE this closure.
Any such future extension must be jointly proved or verifier accepted,
with all original queries/guards, true radii and actual terminal charges.

The original target remains OPEN, and this document provides no claim
that an unrestricted W upper has been found. All new mathematics is
UNVERIFIED because TASK.md expressly allocates ZERO formal calls. The
fact graph remains the sole correctness authority. No fourth law,
extra round, formal call, publication, finalization, revocation, shutdown
or Goal completion is authorized by this handoff.

downloaded text downloads/taylor-hendrickx-glineur-1502.05666.txt was read
at Section2.4, Theorem2 and Lemma2 including both proof directions.
Its F_(0,L) versus nonsmooth strongly convex conjugate classes were

# C93: curvature costs and an exact single-region obstruction for static ridge sums

Status: **UNVERIFIED JOINT PROOF DRAFT**. Current runtime round is 1 of
the new allocation, completed baseline 23 and completed ceiling 25. This
round would bring the completed count to 24 after the runtime returns.
No numerical/model evaluation, sweep, solver, replay, installation,
subordinate agent, or verifier call was used. The original problem is open.

Accepted mathematical predecessors used here are `1252b8823bb5a290`
(necessity of every original smooth-convex interpolation inequality),
`b98bb807d514e80d` (the exact scalar Huber/single-cut lower bound and the
original benchmark), `1fca3e5dd555cb4a` (the explicit alternating-ray
construction), and `04219554e41d9750` (the original dyadic benchmark limit).
All new assertions are proved together below. No shared unverified result
is a mathematical predecessor.

The shared ruler audit `bf591e52671f6d33` suggested the activated-product
quantity used in Section 5. Its needed estimates are rederived here, and
the new conclusion is a resolvent inequality, rather than its quadratic
objective bound. The accepted finite C59 fact `e9a55f57461f253c` and max's
shared actual31 construction `7ffb4d0516ace32f` were read for scope. Neither
is inserted as a child, used as an asymptotic premise, or replayed.

## 1. Family, definitions, and resolved scope

All vector spaces are finite-dimensional real Euclidean spaces. Matrices
are symmetric unless otherwise specified, and matrix inequalities refer
to positive semidefinite order. The identity matrix is denoted I.

For positive radii r_minus,r_plus, define the scalar asymmetric Huber
function phi by phi(0)=0 and

    phi'(s)=max(-r_minus,min(s,r_plus)).                 (1)

Thus phi(s)=s^2/2 inside [-r_minus,r_plus],
phi(s)=r_plus*s-r_plus^2/2 for s>=r_plus, and
phi(s)=-r_minus*s-r_minus^2/2 for s<=-r_minus.
Both joins include their boundaries, where the values and derivatives agree.

Consider ONE STATIC function

    F(x)=x^T A0 x/2 + sum_(j=1)^r alpha_j phi_j(u_j^T x), (2)

where r is any nonnegative integer, A0>=0, every u_j is a unit vector,
alpha_j>0, and every phi_j has its own positive radii. Assume

    H0=A0+sum_j alpha_j u_j u_j^T <= I.                 (3)

The number of ridges, dimension, matrices, directions, and both radii may
depend arbitrarily on the horizon. The ridges need not be orthogonal.
The quadratic part need not commute with any ridge matrix.

For a fixed finite nonnegative word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), set

    S=sum_(i<n)h_i, K=1/(1+2S),
    P_h(t)=product_(i<n)(1-h_i*t),
    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i grad F(x_i), 0<=i<n.                (4)

The normalized payoff is

    V=2F(x_n)/dist(x_0,argmin F)^2,                     (5)

and initial points in the minimizer set are omitted.

A **fixed-region run** means that, for EACH ridge separately, all samples
x_0,...,x_n, INCLUDING the actual unqueried terminal sample, belong to one
of that ridge's three closed intervals: its core, its positive outer ray,
or its negative outer ray. Different ridges may choose different regions.
At a shared boundary either compatible region may be chosen. No condition
is imposed on signs or correlations of the full gradients.

The main quantitative conclusion of this joint draft is:

* If P_h(t)^2 <= 1/(1+2S*t) for every t in [0,1], every fixed-region run
  of (2) has V<=K. This includes all true normalization costs.
* On the ORIGINAL diagnostic H_m defined in Section 5, for every m>=128,
  that polynomial inequality holds, and the supremum of V over the entire
  fixed-region class is EXACTLY K_n(H_m)=1/(1+2 sum H_m).
* Its exact original-benchmark ratio tends to Z_1/(2e)<4/5. Here
  Z_1=lim_(m->infinity)(1+sqrt(2))^(-m) B_(2^m-1), as in accepted
  `04219554e41d9750`, and e is the exponential of 1.

This closes the full fixed-region subclass with arbitrary quadratic
coupling and any number of subunit-curvature ridges. It does NOT close
trajectories that change a ridge region, including a change only at the
terminal sample. Section 8 gives a genuinely coupled static example
with two physical sign changes and actual noncommuting region visits.
It refutes dropping the fixed-region premise even on a word satisfying
the same polynomial inequality. Its payoff is explicitly compared with
the stronger already accepted scalar construction and is NOT presented
as new lower-bound strength.

## 2. Global legality, minimizers, and every original pair

Each phi_j is differentiable, convex, nonnegative, and zero only at zero.
For any x,z, monotonicity and the Lipschitz bound in (1) provide numbers
d_j in [0,alpha_j] such that

    grad F(x)-grad F(z)
      =[A0+sum_j d_j u_j u_j^T](x-z).

If u_j^T(x-z)=0 its contribution is zero and d_j may be taken as zero.
The displayed matrix lies between zero and H0, and hence between zero
and I. Its operator norm is at most 1. This proves global 1-smoothness;
convexity also follows directly from the sum in (2).

Conversely, all radii are positive, so a ball about zero lies in every
core. In that ball F(x)=x^T H0 x/2. Thus global 1-smoothness forces (3).
Condition (3) is exact for this centered model, not merely a sufficient
trace bound.

The minimizer set is precisely the subspace

    N=ker(A0) intersect intersection_j u_j^perp.        (6)

Indeed every summand of (2) is nonnegative, and their simultaneous
vanishing is exactly (6). Let z_0 be the orthogonal projection of x_0
onto N and replace every x_i by x_i-z_0. The objective, gradient, ridge
coordinates, GD recursion, and fixed-region condition are unchanged,
because all gradients lie in N^perp. The initial squared norm after
this replacement equals the TRUE squared distance in (5). We therefore
work below with x_0 in N^perp and denominator ||x_0||^2.

The pair convention is the original one:

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    f_i=F(x_i), g_i=grad F(x_i), x_star=g_star=f_star=0.

Every Q_ij is nonpositive, including both terminal directions and both
optimum directions. To spell out the accepted necessity argument from
`1252b8823bb5a290`, fix i and define
J(z)=F(z)-<g_i,z>. Convexity and grad J(x_i)=0 make x_i a global
minimizer of J. Smoothness gives
J(x_j-grad J(x_j))<=J(x_j)-||grad J(x_j)||^2/2.
The minimum J(x_i) is at most this value. Rearranging gives Q_ij<=0.
This one argument checks every ordered pair of the actual trajectory;
no subset of remote, zero-step, optimum, or terminal rows is discarded.

## 3. A sharp curvature price, including shifted ridge cores

This section treats a broader geometry than (2): allow an additional
linear term and shifts in every ridge argument, and let each scalar
convex ridge have a nonempty open interval where its second derivative
is the constant alpha_j>0. Assume the whole sum is globally 1-smooth
and its quadratic Hessian A0 is positive semidefinite.

At a point in one ridge core, second differences in an arbitrary
direction z, using nonnegativity of the second differences of all
other convex terms, give

    A0+alpha_j u_j u_j^T <= I.                         (7)

For two NONPARALLEL directions u,v, their shifted open core slabs
intersect. In fact the map x -> (u^T x,v^T x) is surjective onto R^2:
on span(u,v) its matrix has determinant 1-<u,v>^2>0. Thus any desired
pair of interior core arguments can be achieved simultaneously.
At such a point the same second-difference argument gives

    A0+alpha u u^T+beta v v^T <= I.                    (8)

These arguments require no differentiability of other summands at that
point. For sufficiently small real t the two selected core terms have
second differences exactly t^2 alpha<u,z>^2 and t^2 beta<v,z>^2,
while 1-smoothness bounds the total symmetric second difference by
t^2||z||^2.

Writing rho=<u,v>, the largest eigenvalue of
alpha uu^T+beta vv^T on their span is

    [alpha+beta+sqrt((alpha-beta)^2+4alpha beta rho^2)]/2.

Consequently (8) implies alpha,beta<=1 and the sharp necessary bound

    alpha beta rho^2 <= (1-alpha)(1-beta).             (9)

To obtain (9), square the nonnegative inequality
sqrt((alpha-beta)^2+4alpha beta rho^2)<=2-alpha-beta
and cancel. With A0=0 it is also sufficient for this pair's matrix to
be bounded by I, so this numerical curvature price is sharp.

There is a sharp bound on coupling to A0 as well. For one ridge let
s=1-alpha and write A0 relative to u and u^perp as

    A0 = [ a  b^T ; b  C ].

If b is nonzero, put v=b/||b|| and c=v^T C v. Positivity of A0 and
of I-A0-alpha uu^T, restricted to span(u,v), gives

    ||b||^2<=a c,  ||b||^2<=(s-a)(1-c),
    0<=a<=s, 0<=c<=1.

If a<=s(1-c), the first bound is at most s c(1-c). If
a>=s(1-c), the second bound is at most that same quantity. Hence

    ||(I-uu^T)A0 u|| <= sqrt(1-alpha)/2.              (10)

The case b=0 already satisfies this. Equality is attained in dimension
two by u=e_1 and

    A_s=(1/2)[s,sqrt(s);sqrt(s),1], alpha=1-s,
    0<s<1.                                          (11)

Both A_s and I-A_s-alpha e_1e_1^T are rank-one positive semidefinite
matrices. This is an ACTUAL available coupling, not a forbidden one.

In particular a ridge of full individual curvature alpha=1 satisfies
A0 u=0: (7) gives u^T A0 u=0, and positive semidefiniteness implies
A0 u=0. Any two nonparallel full-curvature ridge directions are
orthogonal by (9). Parallel shifted ridge terms must be grouped into
one scalar function; they need not have overlapping cores.

It follows that a sum consisting entirely of full-curvature convex
ridges plus a PSD quadratic, with any shifts and any linear term,
separates orthogonally into scalar ridge groups and an independent
quadratic on their orthogonal complement. If its minimum is attained,
its minimizer set is the Cartesian product of the scalar minimizer
sets and the quadratic minimizer affine subspace. Squared distance to
that product is the sum of squared component distances. GD separates
in the same coordinates. For every h the true-distance payoff is
therefore a weighted average of the actual scalar-group payoffs and
quadratic eigenmode payoffs

    t * product_(i<n)(1-h_i*t)^2, 0<=t<=1.             (12)

A component starting in its minimizer set stays there and contributes
zero to both numerator and denominator. This proves that rotating or
adding such full-curvature ridges cannot yield a genuinely coupled
multidimensional improvement over the best component on the SAME word.
It does not bound arbitrary scalar multi-return profiles by K_n(h).

## 4. Exact terminal algebra and the fixed-region bound

Return to the centered model (2). For a fixed-region run absorb all
always-core ridges into the quadratic matrix and write

    A=A0+sum_(j in Core) alpha_j u_j u_j^T,
    b=sum_(j in Outer) epsilon_j q_j u_j,
    D0=sum_(j in Outer) q_j^2/alpha_j,                 (13)

where epsilon_j is +1 or -1 for its chosen outer ray, and
q_j=alpha_j times its radius on that ray. Every actual sample satisfies

    f_i=x_i^T A x_i/2+b^T x_i-D0/2,  g_i=A x_i+b.    (14)

Condition (3) becomes

    A+sum_(j in Outer) alpha_j u_j u_j^T <= I.         (15)

Let L=I-A. For every vector z, scalar Cauchy--Schwarz and (15) give

    (b^T z)^2 <= D0 * z^T L z.                       (16)

Consequently b is orthogonal to ker L. Define L^dagger by reciprocating
its positive eigenvalues and assigning zero to its zero eigenvalues.
Putting z=L^dagger b in (16), or treating b=0 separately, proves

    D0 >= b^T L^dagger b.                            (17)

Thus the intercept cost required by global smoothness is retained even
when many nonorthogonal ridge forces add in the same direction.

Define polynomials (with their continuous values at t=0)

    R_h(t)=(1-P_h(t))/t, R_h(0)=S,
    T_h(t)=(1-P_h(t)^2)/t, T_h(0)=2S.

Unrolling g_i=A x_i+b yields

    x_n=P_h(A)x_0-R_h(A)b.                           (18)

This identity is also proved inductively using
R_(prefix+h)(t)=(1-h*t)R_prefix(t)+h. Substituting (18) into (14),
and using I-A R_h(A)=P_h(A), gives the EXACT identity

    2f_n=x_0^T A P_h(A)^2 x_0
           +2b^T P_h(A)^2 x_0-D0-b^T T_h(A)b.        (19)

No radius, value, forcing term, or terminal quantity has been removed.

Assume now the polynomial condition

    P_h(t)^2 <= 1/(1+2S*t), 0<=t<=1.                 (20)

Diagonalize A orthogonally. For one eigenvalue t in (0,1), abbreviate
p=P_h(t)^2 and

    d=1/(1-t)+(1-p)/t.

By (17), its contribution to the right side of (19) is bounded by

    t p X^2+2p X B-d B^2,                            (21)

where X,B are the coordinates of x_0,b on this eigenvector. We claim
(21)<=K X^2. First d>0 by (20), and
t p<=t/(1+2S*t)<=K. The determinant needed to complete the square is

    (K-t p)d-p^2
       =[K-p(K+t(1-K))]/[t(1-t)] >=0,               (22)

because K/[K+t(1-K)]=1/(1+2S*t). Thus the two-variable quadratic
K X^2-(21) is positive semidefinite.

At t=0, p=1, and its contribution is at most
2XB-(1+2S)B^2<=K X^2 by completing a square. At t=1, (16) gives
B=0, and the contribution is P_h(1)^2 X^2<=K X^2 by (20).
Summing all eigenvector inequalities proves 2f_n<=K||x_0||^2.
Section 2 identified that norm with the true closest-minimizer distance.
This proves the first quantitative claim in Section 1, including zero
eigenvalues, unit eigenvalues, empty outer sets, and zero physical steps.

Equality is attained by the actual scalar global Huber witness for ANY
nonnegative word: take A0=0, one unit ridge of curvature 1, both radii
q=1/(1+2S), and initial point x_0=1. All iterates are
x_i=1-q sum_(t<i)h_t >=(1+S)/(1+2S)>=q. The fixed positive outer
region condition includes the terminal point. Direct substitution gives
2F(x_n)=1/(1+2S), the initial true distance is 1, and the minimizer is
unique. Hence whenever (20) holds the restricted supremum is exactly K.

## 5. The resolvent inequality on the ORIGINAL H_m, with all curvatures

Fix an integer m>=128, and keep the following enclosing-horizon
parameters fixed in EVERY child word:

    sigma=1+sqrt(2), c=sigma(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,
    d_j=1+a*c^j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j), 0<=j<m.

The actual physical word is h=H_m, of length n=2^m-1. Write
S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j, r_c=c-2, and A_mass=a/r_c. Counting the
occurrences of each bridge gives

    M_j=A_mass*c^j+(1-A_mass)*2^j,
    P_j(t)=product_(l=0)^(j-1)(1-d_l*t)^(2^(j-l-1)).   (23)

Here P_j is the polynomial of the complete physical word H_j; P_0=1.

The following bounds follow from squaring the rational comparisons
7/5<sqrt(2)<10/7 and using m>=128:

    12/5<c<49/20, 3/8<a<sigma-2<c-2<9/20,
    127/128<=ac<1, 0<A_mass<1, c*r_c<5/4.            (24)

For the upper c bound, c<(17/7)(129/128)=2193/896<49/20.
For the lower a bound, a>(127/128)/(49/20)>3/8. The upper bound on
a follows from a=(m-1)/[(m+1)sigma]<1/sigma=sigma-2.
In particular (23) gives M_j<=c^j and

    t*S_j >= a*c^j*t/r_c-t.                          (25)

We prove (20) for this exact h. At t=0 it is equality. Fix t in (0,1].
If every d_l*t<1 for 0<=l<m, all factors are nonnegative and
1-z<=exp(-z) gives

    P_m(t)^2 <= exp(-2S_m*t) <= 1/(1+2S_m*t).        (26)

If a factor is zero the required conclusion is immediate. Otherwise
let j be the first level with d_j*t>=1. Set

    v_l=a*c^l*t, Y_l=c*v_l*|P_l(t)|.

If j=0 then Y_0=ac*t<=1. If j>=1, all factors of P_j are on their
nonnegative side, and t<1/d_0<8/11. From (25),

    Y_j<=c*v_j*exp(-v_j/r_c+t)
         <=c*r_c*exp(t-1)<1.                         (27)

The middle bound maximizes v exp(-v/r_c) over v>=0, with maximum
r_c/e. The last inequality uses c*r_c<5/4 and
exp(3/11)>1+3/11=14/11>5/4.

For every l>=j, the exact physical recursion in (23) gives

    Y_(l+1)=[1-(1-t)/v_l]*Y_l^2 <=Y_l^2<=1.          (28)

The bracket is between zero and one, since d_l*t>=1 and t<=1.
Consequently, with z=a*c^m*t,

    P_m(t)^2 <= 1/(c^2*z^2).                         (29)

Activation implies t>=1/d_(m-1). Since m>=3, (24) implies

    z>=c*[a*c^(m-1)]/[1+a*c^(m-1)]
       >(12/5)*(54/25)/(1+54/25)=648/395>3/2.        (30)

Also S_m<c^m by (23). Therefore

    (1+2S_m*t)P_m(t)^2
       <=[1+(16/3)z]/[(144/25)z^2]
       <=25/36<1.                                  (31)

For the final inequality the function z^(-2)+(16/3)z^(-1) decreases
for z>0, and its value at z=3/2 gives 25/36 after multiplication by
25/144. Equations (26) and (31) cover EVERY t in [0,1]. In particular
there was no curvature grid, schedule replay, or fixed-curvature premise.

## 6. Same-word scalar comparison and the ORIGINAL benchmark

We also rederive that the best accepted Huber/single-cut value on this
word is the global Huber value, so the comparison is on the SAME word.
For a cut at level j its squared numerator is (a*c^j)^2. Holding the
total S_m and bridge d_j fixed, the denominator is

    (1+P)^2[1+2(S_m-d_j-P)],

where P is the actual preceding mass. Its logarithm has strictly
negative second derivative in the interior of the allowed mass range.
Thus its minimum over the interval between the first and last occurrence
is at an endpoint. The palindrome H_m makes the following mass at its
first occurrence at least S_(m-1), and its preceding mass is S_j. At
the last occurrence the preceding mass is at least S_(m-1), and the
following mass is S_j. Put M=M_(m-1).

From (23), a*c^j<=r_c*M_j, M_j<=M, and M_m<=c*M. The first-occurrence
cut is at most r_c^2/(2M-1). The last-occurrence cut is at most

    r_c^2*M_j^2/[M^2(2M_j-1)] <= r_c^2/(2M-1),

because x^2/(2x-1) is nondecreasing for x>=1. Finally c<5/2,
r_c<1/2, and M>=1 give

    r_c^2(2M_m-1) <=(5M-1)/4 <=2M-1.

Every cut in accepted `b98bb807d514e80d` is therefore no larger than
1/(2M_m-1). Its global Huber term attains that value. Hence

    sup_fixed-region V = K_n(H_m)=1/(2S_m+1).         (32)

For the limit, divide (23), at j=m, by sigma^m. Since a/(c-2)->1,
(1+1/m)^m->e, and (2/sigma)^m->0, one gets

    M_m/sigma^m -> e.

The accepted ray theorem `04219554e41d9750` supplies
B_(2^m-1)/sigma^m -> Z_1. Thus the exact restricted ratio has limit

    B_(2^m-1)*sup_fixed-region V -> Z_1/(2e).         (33)

For an explicit strict gap, its accepted upper enclosure at b=1,j=0
gives Z_1<=(sqrt(B_1)+3/100)^2. The accepted original recurrence gives
B_1=4. Therefore Z_1<=41209/10000. The positive exponential series
gives e>1+1+1/2+1/6=8/3, whence

    Z_1/(2e)<123627/160000<4/5.                      (34)

No fit, substituted recurrence, internal capacity, or scalar radius
of a higher-dimensional witness is used in this comparison.

## 7. The coupled fixed-region class is nonempty on every word

Here is a fully specified genuinely coupled instance satisfying its
region hypothesis for ANY finite nonnegative h. It is included to rule
out a merely vacuous obstruction. Work in R^2 and put

    u=e_1, w=(e_1+e_2)/sqrt(2),
    eta=1/[8(1+S)], A0=eta*w*w^T,
    alpha=1/2, q=eta, r_minus=r_plus=q/alpha,
    F(x)=x^T A0 x/2+alpha*phi(u^T x), x_0=e_1.       (35)

The matrices A0 and uu^T do not commute. Also
A0+alpha uu^T <=(eta+alpha)I<=5I/8<I. The minimizer is unique zero,
since the two distinct directions w and u span R^2.

To check the actual gates without numerical evaluation, first run the
explicit affine recursion z_(i+1)=(I-h_i A0)z_i-h_i q u, z_0=e_1.
Every factor I-h_i A0 has norm at most one, because
0<=h_i eta<=S/[8(1+S)]<1. Consequently
||z_i||<=1+q sum_(t<i)h_t<=9/8. Its displacement from e_1 satisfies

    ||z_i-e_1||<=sum_(t<i)h_t(eta*9/8+q)<17/64.

Thus u^T z_i>47/64, whereas r_plus=1/[4(1+S)]<=1/4.
Every sample including z_n is strictly in the positive outer region.
It follows inductively that z_i IS the actual GD run of the single
function (35). Section 2 supplies all original pairs and true radius 1.
The payoff need not equal (32); Section 4 shows that on H_m it cannot
exceed (32). This is not a scalar copy hidden behind a coordinate change:
its actual outer Hessian A0 and central Hessian A0+alpha uu^T do not
commute, although this particular run only visits the outer region.

## 8. A static multiple-sign escape, and its honest payoff comparison

The next example attacks the fragile extension that the polynomial
condition alone might bound ALL trajectories of (2) by the global Huber
value. It preserves that condition and releases the fixed-region premise.
It also shows that the curvature rigidity at alpha=1 is not valid for
arbitrarily small positive slack. It is a scope example, not a new
competitive lower bound on H_m or on arbitrary words.

Take the fixed physical word

    h=(1,4,1,4,1), S=11, K=1/23.                    (36)

Its polynomial is P_h(t)=(1-t)^3(1-4t)^2. For 0<=t<=1/4, all factors
are nonnegative, so P_h(t)^2<=exp(-22t)<=1/(1+22t). For t>=1/4 put
z=1-t in [0,3/4]. Then P_h(t)=z^3(3-4z)^2. Its derivative as a
function of z is z^2(3-4z)(9-20z), and the maximum is

    (9/20)^3*(6/5)^2=6561/50000<1/7.

It follows that (1+22t)P_h(t)^2<=23/49<1 on this second interval.
Thus (20) holds on ALL of [0,1] for the same word (36).

Let phi have radii r_minus=3/4 and r_plus=1/2. Fix ANY
0<epsilon<=1/1000000 before the run, put s=epsilon^2, and define

    F_epsilon(x,y)=(epsilon*x+y)^2/4+(1-s)*phi(x),
    (x_0,y_0)=(5/4,0).                              (37)

Its quadratic is A_s from (11), its ridge direction is e_1, and its
ridge curvature is 1-s. Thus it is globally convex and 1-smooth. Its
unique minimizer is (0,0), giving true initial squared distance 25/16.
Its outer and central Hessians are

    A_s=(1/2)[s,epsilon;epsilon,1],
    C_s=A_s+(1-s)e_1e_1^T.

They do not commute for epsilon>0. Both are PSD and bounded by I, since
I-C_s=(1/2)(epsilon,-1)(epsilon,-1)^T.

At epsilon=0, the exact first-coordinate trajectory on (36) is

    5/4, 3/4, -5/4, -1/2, 3/2, 1,                  (38)

and the second coordinate stays zero. The corresponding first gradient
coordinates are

    1/2, 1/2, -3/4, -1/2, 1/2, 1/2.

Its ridge regions are positive outer, positive outer, negative outer,
core, positive outer, positive outer. Every listed point has distance
at least 1/4 from the boundary of its specified open region.

Here is a uniform TEXTUAL perturbation bound establishing those SAME
actual region visits for every epsilon in the specified interval.
The matrix change A_s-A_0 has norm at most epsilon, and |phi'|<=3/4.
At every unperturbed sample, whose norm is at most 3/2, this gives

    ||grad F_epsilon(z_i^0)-grad F_0(z_i^0)||<=3epsilon.

Let E_i=||z_i^epsilon-z_i^0|| for the actual GD runs, and put
D_i=product_(t<i)(1+h_t). Global 1-smoothness gives
E_(i+1)<=(1+h_i)E_i+3epsilon*h_i, and hence

    E_i<=3epsilon(D_i-1)<=597epsilon<1/1000.          (39)

The product at n=5 is exactly 2*5*2*5*2=200. Equation (39) is a
five-step analytic perturbation estimate, not a numerical replay.
It preserves every strict gate in (38). In particular the actual run
visits A_s,C_s,A_s at queried indices 2,3,4. It has TWO actual sign
changes in both the first position coordinate and first gradient
coordinate, with gradient signs +,+,-,-,+,+.

The correlations are not constant, and the gradients are not collinear.
For the first two actual gradients, which use the positive outer region,
direct substitution gives

    g_0=(1/2+s/8,5epsilon/8),
    g_1=((1-s)(1/2+s/16),epsilon(1-s)/16),
    det(g_0,g_1)=-epsilon(1-s)(9+s)/32 !=0.           (40)

Their inner product is positive. The error of any actual gradient from
its unperturbed counterpart is at most E_i+3epsilon<=600epsilon.
Thus <g_1,g_2><0: its unperturbed value is -3/8, while the absolute
change is at most (5/4)(600epsilon)+(600epsilon)^2<1/100.
This proves distinct normalized correlations as well as noncollinearity.

All original ordered Q rows, including the actual terminal rows, follow
from Section 2 applied to (37). No Hessian is chosen online.

The payoff comparison must keep the true radius. At z_5^0=(1,0),

    F_epsilon(1,0)=3/8-s/8,
    grad F_epsilon(1,0)=(1/2,epsilon/2).

This gradient has norm less than one. Convexity, smoothness and (39)
therefore give |F_epsilon(z_5^epsilon)-3/8|<1/100. Since the true
squared radius is 25/16,

    9/20 < V_epsilon=(32/25)F_epsilon(z_5^epsilon)<1/2. (41)

For example the stronger bounds from 3/8 plus or minus 1/100 are
2336/5000<V_epsilon<2464/5000, which imply (41).
This strictly exceeds the global Huber value 1/23 despite (20), so
dropping the fixed-region premise of Section 4 is refuted.

On this SAME word the exact accepted single-cut maximum is

    K_5(h)=max(1/23,9/52,3/49)=9/52<9/20.            (42)

Nevertheless the stronger accepted scalar construction
`1fca3e5dd555cb4a` already gives 73/48>1/2. Its two checkpoints are
4 and 4, with all three ordinary masses equal to 1. The fact's exact
parameters are m_0=1/2,m_1=3/4,O_1=1/2,z_term=7/4,e_term=1/4,
and q_opt=7/12. Feasibility is z_term>=2m_0=1, and its payoff is

    2e_term+z_term^2/3=1/2+49/48=73/48.              (43)

Thus (37) is an actual nonlinear, noncommuting, multiple-sign scope
example with a checked quantitative gap over the single-cut calibration.
It is DOMINATED by an already accepted scalar witness and is not new
lower-bound strength. The word (36) is not H_m and supplies neither the
arbitrary-h nor the non-dyadic quantifiers of the project goal.

## 9. Interface left for the next authorized round

The fixed-region mechanism on the original H_m is exactly Huber-tight,
even with an arbitrary number of centered ridges, arbitrary dimension,
subunit ridge curvatures, and noncommuting quadratic coupling. The
full-curvature relay is additionally forced to split into scalar groups.
These are separate scoped exclusions; neither excludes all static ridge
sums.

The explicit escape (37) shows the remaining mechanism is real: a
subunit ridge can visit its core between opposite outer visits, while
the background quadratic generates changing correlations. What remains
missing is a fixed static gated construction on ORIGINAL H_m with a
terminal value exceeding its accepted scalar baseline, with all region
visits and true distance controlled uniformly. The matrices and affine
forcing are fixed by (37), not freely selectable at each bridge. There
is no authorized numerical search or verifier call for that step.

For max's actual31 state, this result supplies a price only if a proposed
realization is specifically a centered Huber-ridge sum of (2). No claim
is made that its conjugate interpolant has that representation. Its
positive endpoint state, changed values, residuals, and minimizer hull
cannot be inserted into (19) without proving the representation and
the fixed-region hypothesis. C59 likewise is not claimed to belong to
this family, and its fixed H6 parameters were never replaced by those
of another enclosing horizon.

Literature context only: Aravkin, Burke, and Pillonetto, arXiv:1301.4566v2,
Remark 4 (Huber scalar example) and Remark 5 (sum representation), were
read from the exact PDF. The downloaded PDF is retained in downloads/.
Local extraction failed because pdftotext and Python PDF readers were
absent; the web PDF extractor supplied the relevant text. No installation
was made. Those remarks are not external mathematical premises: (1),
its pieces, every matrix bound, and all dynamics above are derived here.
